# Two Disjoint Loops: A K-Uniform Bound on the Renormalised Pair Expectation (27 October 2026)

Derived, for review, given the single-loop inputs listed under *Inherited* and except the R-step inputs (R-eval), (R-eval-cr), (R-member), (R-round), and (R-att-ext) below, which are derived and not reviewed; the single-loop (6.3) needs (R-eval), (R-round), and (R-att-ext) as well: an exact identity for the pair ((8.1)) and a bound on it uniform in K ((8.2)). Setting: two vertex-disjoint squares C₁, C₂ in planes parallel to that of e₁, e₂, with characters χ₁, χ₂. The renormalised pair expectation is W^×_ren(ε) := E_ε[W_{χ₁}(C₁)W_{χ₂}(C₂)]/(κ^{(K′)}_{C₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂}). Here κ^{(K′)}_{C_i} is the single-loop normalisation of C_i with the character χ_i at the common stopping step. This extends (6.3) of `WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md` (the continuum note) from one loop to two. Its first step is V4 Section 10 (`WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V4.tex`): Theorem thm:two-loop-theorem, and Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated for the retreated carrier. Map: `WILSON_IT1_D2_STEP_MAP_2026_10_22.md`, 1614-line version.

**Open (R-eval-old): the mixed term on the old part of Λ.** On Λ^{old} := Λ∖Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, Bałaban's U_k^0 takes the real integration variables V_h of T′_k(X) (LFR II (1.50), p.370 l.4; p.378 l.31), which only χ_{h,1/2} restricts, through the curvature of (1, V_h) (LFR I (1.88), p.197 l.46–48), together with T_h. The post-R configuration takes its own averages there. What is missing is a bound on the part T₂^{old} of the response's mixed term (*The mixed term* in (R-eval)) that is sourced by this real data difference. Either of two inputs would close it: a G-valued gauge u, trivial off Λ, with sup_{Λ^{old}}|log V_h(M^h(U)^u)^{−1}| → 0 as g → 0 on the support of the R-step's indicators; or a reformulation of the evaluation points that keeps V_h out of the comparison. A bound on how the minimiser's linear response depends on real data of size O(1) gives Λ-decay but leaves the term proportional to λ, so it does not close the gap. Bałaban's own chain does not supply either input. It cuts off the dependence on V″⌈_{(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c} only on Z^c (LFR II p.370 l.14–15; U_{1,2} of (1.53) equals U_1 on Z^c, p.371 l.26–27), and on Λ^∼ his extended U_k^0 has "the properties of the configuration U_k″" (p.375 l.13–14). His (1.65) lands in the spaces of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69), whose phase radius on an old layer m exceeds the top layer's by the factor L^{k−m}α_{1,m}/α_{1,k} (LFR I p.190 l.27). 𝒟^λ_k caps this factor at λ, so (1.65) does not give the landing in 𝒟^λ_k on Λ^{old}. A third input would close it on the layers where λ^{(k)}_n = λ. At the points where the next T-step evaluates, every datum has phase per fine bond at most λ(1+β₀)L^{−1}α_{1,k}L^{−k} plus the 𝐇-part (l.54). So a bound C_{resp,∞} on the sup-norm response of U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), ·) at any real base (VAR (190) summed over the sources, in fine-phase units), with C_{resp,∞}(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/4, would bound U_k^0(M(𝐔))'s complex part there directly, with no comparison of bases, once λ also absorbs C_{resp,∞} times the Λ-data's M⁵ amplification. VAR's O(1) depends on d and L (p.299 l.26), so the print does not give this. The large-field note derives this constant for the same minimisers (U_k^0 is among its members). Its sup bound abs(Q_{i′}HB) ≤ C_HL^{−(i−i′)}abs(B), in the units of the data, gives C_{resp,∞} = C_H at i′ = 0, since a datum on an i-block has L^{−i} times its phase per fine bond (WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md l.6; table row 226). There C_H := C(d)(1 + log L)^pC_H^{reg}, with C_H^{reg} the bulk constant (its l.6, l.1008), and (K0) (l.1009) is derived, not reviewed, with constants C(d)(1 + log L)^p in the bulk size θ := C_H^{reg}θ_B. The large-field note bounds the bulk constant, C_H^{reg} ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^{p′} "with constants of d only and no dependence on M or β₀", in VAR's problem by (K0)(f) with θ_B for θ (its l.6, {#0.1}; l.1009, {#K0.f}; derived, not reviewed). So C_H ≤ C(d)(1 + log L)^{p+p′}, the form that the response needs (table l.232), derived, not reviewed. At the interfaces Λ_j/Λ_{j+1} with j ≥ 1 its chain uses the representative A_♮ := A_N + ∂ω_♮ of (K0)(g)(6), ω_♮ ∈ N(Q′), and A_♮ = A_N "at the points of R₀ outside the blocks of Λ₁ that meet R₁" (its l.1009, {#K0.g6} (vi)), which include Λ₀/Λ₁ (its l.6). Its former open item S5 (WILSON_IT1_LF_TRIAGE_R38_2026_11_03.md l.46), the interfaces Λ_j/Λ_{j+1} with j ≥ 1, where a coarser block at multiscale distance O(1) carried L^{d/2−1} times the local-unit sources, is now (K0)(g), also derived, not reviewed: the bound holds there in the units of L^j, and the chain's norm is ‖·‖_{W_ms} with W_ms := max_jW_j. The large-field note's bound is at a real base that is regular in VAR's sense. U_k^0(M(U)) is regular on all of Λ^{old}. LFR I (1.88) splits each component of (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Ω_h by the set P of squares where the curvature of (1, V_h) is at least ½ε_h(L^{k−h}η)² (p.197 l.44–51). The components with P ≠ ∅ carry "new large fields" and are excluded from Z, and on the remaining components, which form the new Z, the factor is χ_{h,1/2} of the whole domain (p.198 l.2). Λ ⊂ Z (p.192 l.43), so P = ∅ on Λ^{old}, the restriction by χ_{h,1/2} stated above. There Bałaban's estimates (1.91) (p.198) and (1.95) (p.199) bound the curvature of U″_{k,Z} by (½ + 2α + O(αε_h))ε_h(L^{k−h}η)², with α the small absolute constant of (1.90) and (1.94) (p.198 l.27). By the properties quoted above, U_k^0 inherits this regularity. If that bound holds, then C_{resp,∞}(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/4 holds for L large given d. λ absorbs C_HM⁵ as it absorbs C_ΛM⁵ (l.73, l.166), and Λ lies on the layers n ≤ k₀, where λ^{(k)}_n = λ since c′^{N₀} ≥ λ for g small (*Where it lands*). So the open comes down to one input: a field bound C_{resp,∞} = o(L) at VAR's representative. In REG's terms, C_{resp,∞} is the norm B₀ of H(U₀) in |A|_{(−1)} ≤ B₀(|J|_{(−3)} + |B₁|) (REG p.86 l.52–53), restricted to the data. REG prints B₀ as depending "on d and L only" (p.87 l.16), and it enters REG's own bounds as B₁ = 5dLB₀ ((1.62), (1.75)–(1.76)). The large-field note now takes its bound for the global Landau representative v = ∂₁*Δ^{−1}F of the response (its l.6), since in its slice gauge the pointwise bound fails at the re-entrant edges and corners of Λ by pure-gauge parts. VAR's representative V̂, at which the landing here is read, satisfies only REG's Landau condition R(U₀)D*a = 0, and v satisfies it too. So at first order V̂'s field is v + Dλ with λ := Δ^{−1}D*a, its gradient part. The bound for v does not control Dλ, and B₀ = o(L) is the input in this form.
**Open (R-eval-curv): the curvature and current conditions at the reduced representative, and CRE (2.39) at the axial form P(𝐔).** The class's bounds at an evaluation point are taken at the reduced-frame representative 𝕌̃ ((iii), *The reduced frame*), which has to lie in 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k. 𝒟_k contains CRE (2.34)–(2.37) at the configuration itself (CRE p.261 l.5–24), and only 𝒟^λ_k's phase, gradient, and current conditions are derived at 𝕌̃. Bałaban's chain gives (2.34)–(2.37) at the u-free representative 𝕌^{(0)} (LFR I p.191 l.39–44, read with p.191 l.33–34's "coincides", although (1.68) prints the factor c = 3 on Ω_k, p.191 l.13–14: the reading *R-coincides*, l.17), with no margin. But 𝕌̃ = (𝕌^{(0)})^{ũ} with a G^c-valued ũ, conjugation by ũ can enlarge |∂𝕌 − 1| and |J^h| by e^{2|Im log ũ|}, and 𝒟_k is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (CRE p.261 l.42–43). A reduction of (2.34) at 𝕌̃ to the real base through 𝒟^λ_k's gradient condition does not work: CRE's constants have α_{1,j} = λ_Rα_{0,j} with λ_R > 1 (table row 53; CRE p.262 l.43–46 imports RG1's restrictions on α_{0,j} and α_{1,j}, among them B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁, RG1 p.277 l.54), so that condition allows a term 2η²|∇A′| of up to 2λ_R times (2.34)'s room. *The reduction to 𝕌^{(0)} (derived).* At each plaquette ∂𝕌̃ = Ad(ũ(x))∂𝕌^{(0)}, with x the plaquette's base point, and J^h(𝕌̃) = Ad(ũ)J^h(𝕌^{(0)}) (RG1 (1.10)). M(𝕌̃) is M(𝕌^{(0)}) transformed by ũ at the block points, and U_{p,X}(V^v) = U_{p,X}(V)^{v̄}, with v̄ constant on blocks and equal to v at their centres, as RG1 uses at complex u_j in (3.38) (p.278 l.3–12). So each quantity in (2.34)–(2.37) at 𝕌̃ is the same quantity at 𝕌^{(0)} conjugated by ũ or by its block-constant form. ũ is real at U (*The reduced frame*), and on a layer-n block |log(ũũ(U)^{−1})| ≤ O(B_G)L^nηΣ_m(ε_m + ε_{m+1}) ≤ O((N+1)B_Gλα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)}, up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**). This follows from *The sources* and *The bound on G*, both of whose terms carry L^iη (l.106, l.108). Since Ad of a real element preserves the norm, conjugation multiplies each quantity on layer n by at most 1 + O((N+1)B_Gλα_{1,k})L^{−(k−n)}. That is below ¼β2^{−(k+1−n)} for g small, uniformly in n, since L ≥ 2 and (N+1)α_{1,k} is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}. So (2.34)–(2.37) hold at 𝕌̃ once they hold at 𝕌^{(0)} with each coefficient f_n lowered by ½β2^{−(k+1−n)}. The missing input is this margin at 𝕌^{(0)}. Bałaban's chain gives the coefficients f_n themselves, and his spaces (1.68) carry c = 3 on Ω_k. A route is his own device on old layers (CRE p.277 l.2–7): the next step's domain has coefficient f_n − β2^{−(k+1−n)} on layer n, and the T-step's points use at most an eighth of this, since map l.928–929 fit them into all of it with C′ ≥ 16 and T_Y's α₀-entry has C_K ≥ 128 ((iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*). The margin would follow if the chain of LFR II (1.65) and LFR I's N preliminary steps carried it, as (1.48) carries the real bounds with factors that are arbitrarily small for γ small (LFR I p.186 l.35–43). The same margin is needed wherever a point is put into 𝒟_k from its phase, gradient, and current alone: at P of the T-step's points at s ≠ 1, and at s = 1 at a localized background ((iii), *The M-factored domain*; *The bounds*, part (a)), at the Schwarz step's scaled points ((iii), *On the layers k₀ < m ≤ k*), at the extension-domain points of D2.7 15.6(c)–(f) ((iv)), at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))), the axial form of the Landau representative, where LFR II (1.65) is applied ((iii), *The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points*), and in SW (6b)'s radius R′ (SW l.93, *The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*). At (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))) the open also covers CRE (2.39), the bounds L^nξ|A′|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξ_UA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{1,n} on the complex part (CRE p.261 l.35–40). 𝒟^{λ,M}_k bounds the phase and gradient only at V̂ = P(𝐔)^{u_L}, and u_L is G^c-valued: its non-unitary part changes A′ additively, by D(Im λ̂) at first order, not by conjugation, and l.107 bounds D₀λ̂ only through P(𝐔)'s own complex part. On Ω_k the rooms of 𝒟^λ_k and 𝒟_k coincide (l.52–53), so (2.39) at P(𝐔) does not follow from V̂'s membership with room to spare. A route avoids P(𝐔): apply (1.65) at (V̂, J^h(V̂)), which lies in 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ Ũ_k^c(Y₄) by definition, and carry the image to P(𝐔)'s frame by the block-constant covariance U_{p,X}(V^v) = U_{p,X}(V)^{v̄} that RG1 uses at complex gauge transformations ((3.38), p.278 l.3–12), with v = u_L^{−1} at the block points, bounded by l.106. This would reduce the part to the margin above. It needs that covariance for LFR II's whole U_k^0, (1.50)–(1.51), and it is not carried out here. The same transfer, with the conjugation by a G^c gauge transformation at the endpoints, is needed for the covariant members (CRE's B-terms, D2.6 closure 5.4's transports, and every member with Cov(k)'s endpoint factors) at the T-step's re-chosen pairs (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉, R(ĥ)ω) of (iii), *The bounds*, part (b). There it enlarges their bounds by at most e^{c_π(|Im λ_ĥ(x)|+|Im λ_ĥ(y)|)} ≤ e^{2c_πa_c}, and by e^{4a_c} for the adjoint-valued B-terms, with |λ_ĥ| ≤ a_c = O(α_{1,k}) by REG (1.70)–(1.72) at steps k and k+1, and the outer fields' domain needs the enlargement e^{2a_c}. These factors must not compound over the steps, since Σ_kα_{1,k} is not bounded uniformly in K.
**(R-eval), derived except for Open (R-eval-old) and Open (R-eval-curv) above, not reviewed apart from the real base current ((iii), *The bounds*, part (b); l.173, packet JB), with the cross insertions' weights in (R-eval-cr) below: the class's bounds at the R-steps' evaluation points, with an α₀-entry in T_Y for the current part of K (*The constants*).**
- How it holds: the bounds below hold on the complexified post-R minimiser manifold 𝓜^c ((iii), *The scope*). The old insertions' post-R function is extended as a function of M(𝐔) alone, so its value at 𝐔 is its value at P(𝐔) := U^{post}(M(𝐔)) ∈ 𝓜^c. This extension is the note's own: Bałaban's takes (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the usual way" (LFR II p.374 l.38, p.375 l.11–12), and the two agree on 𝓜^c at 𝐉 = J^h(𝐔) ((iii), *The scope*). Every member of the class is taken composed with P, so the class's domain is M-factored ((iii), *The M-factored domain*, derived). The T-step's factorisation at s = 0 needs each member to be local. P is local, since U^{post} = U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), ·) is supported in Y₁, and each member takes the P of its own domain: a member that is a function of 𝐔 takes the localized projection P_Ω onto 𝐁_k″(Ω) (CRE (2.14)–(2.15)), as Bałaban uses local minimisers there (RG1 (3.23)). P_Ω is the identity on the minimisers ((iii), *The bounds*, derived). The transfer to step k+1 is derived ((iii), *The bounds*). In part (a), at a localized background, P of the T-step's point is compared with the background itself, which each member's P fixes, and the re-projection costs the response of the points at s ≠ 1, up to **Open (R-eval-curv)** there. In part (b), REG's step-k normalisation is met exactly by evaluating at another pair of the same G^c-orbit, chosen from the background alone through REG's normalised transformations at steps k and k+1, and equal at real parameters by the members' G-invariance; for the covariant members and the outer fields this holds up to **Open (R-eval-curv)**, and for the base current's frame term by l.173's bound on B_J (reviewed, packet JB). Its bounds there are those on 𝓜^c ∩ 𝒟^λ_k. The T-step's points land in it with no condition on L beyond SW (b3)'s. The factor K := O(C_dS_L) of VAR Proposition 9 and (190) costs only a factor 4K/(1−β) in T_Y and, for the current, an α₀-entry with constant C_K in T_Y, which D2.4 absorbs for g_K small (*The constants*). The other parts:
  - the current of the deformations is tracked covariantly at the configurations, J^h(V^G) = Ad_GJ^h(V), so the gauge transformation enters only through |λ̂|, and no Hessian of λ̂, 𝓗, or A_L enters ((iii), *The current of the deformations*);
  - the Landau representatives are VAR's, generated from the axial-gauge element as in REG, so 𝓗's argument is the configuration's own plain data, and the one gauge transformation G is bounded through REG (1.70)–(1.71) ((iii), *The Landau representatives*, *The bound on G*, *The reduced frame*);
  - the data's 𝐉: the domain is a set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉), and the pair's 𝐉, in the Landau frame, obeys 𝒟^λ_k's current condition and (2.36) about 0 by definition, with no transport ((iii), *The 𝐉 coordinate*);
  - (2.36) about 0 and the complex curvature conditions (2.34), (2.35), and (2.37) at the evaluation points are quoted from LFR II (1.65) and LFR I's chain statement (p.191 l.39–44), since 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k = Ũ_k^c ((iii), *The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points*). They are quoted at the u-free representative. At the reduced representative, where the class's bounds are taken, they are **Open (R-eval-curv)**.
- *The constants.*
  - CRE's C₀ and C₁. CRE (2.28) asks only that C₀ and C₁ be "sufficiently large" (p.259 l.50), but CRE p.262 l.43–46 imports the restrictions of RG1 on α_{0,j} and α_{1,j}, among them B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁ (RG1 p.277 l.54, the deformation 𝐇_j of (3.37) placed in the complex part). So C₁ = λ_RC₀ with λ_R = K_RLM + 1 (table row 53), α_{1,j} = λ_Rα_{0,j} under q₀ = q₁, and ᾱ = min(α₀, α₁) = α₀. No choice C₀ ≥ C₁ is available. The current part of K: T_Y carries an α₀-entry with constant C_K′ := max(C′, C_K) ≥ C_K := 128K/(1−β)², fixed after L and M ((iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*; the map's α₀-entry, l.929 and l.1498, with C′ ≥ 16). On the polydisc it gives sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ 4min(β,1)α_{0,k}/C_K + 8C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k. So the current change at s ≠ 1, at most 2K sup|𝐇| in the phase room's units, is at most min(β,1)(1−β)²α_{0,k}/16 + O(Kε_k) ≤ (1−β)²α_{0,k}/64 + O(Kε_k), since β ≤ 1/4 (CRE p.261 l.42; LFR I p.191 l.35), below (1−β)f_nα_{0,k}/64 + O(Kε_k) and so below ⅛(1−β)f_nα_{0,k} for g small (q₀ > p₀), and so is the 𝐇-part's own current at s = 1. C_K enters C_T, which is fixed after L and M, and 1/T_Y ≤ C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k with b_k/α_{0,k} ≤ C(A₀/C₀)(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q₀} → 0, so g_K small absorbs it, as it absorbs the factor 4K/(1−β). The other places where C₀/C₁ enters take C₁/C₀ = λ_R, fixed after L and M: the att constant of the reclassified dev insertions, Cρ_k·max(1, α_{0,k}/α_{1,k}) = Cρ_k (map l.1370; D2.6 closure §2.1); the new groups' complex-data clause, (1 + 2C₀/C₁)C_χ(C₁/min(C₀, C₁))(Σ_tw_t)ρ_{k+1} ≤ 3λ_RC_χ(Σ_tw_t)ρ_{k+1} (SW §6(6b)); (b2)'s 3λS₃₀λ_Rρ_k (l.55); and the covariance note's c_nc and m₀. T₁'s current at the start of the chain is not such a source term: it is absorbed by λ's factor λ_R (l.72) on the layers n ≤ k₀, and by M large on the layers k₀ < n ≤ k ((iii), *The starting values*). Each is a source term, not compounded from step to step, and multiplies an amplitude of order ρ ∝ g²·polylog, so g_K small absorbs it. The covariance note's former condition M ≥ 2C_avC₁/C₀ cannot hold, since λ_R grows with M; its disc is reduced instead (WILSON_IT1_COMPLEX_COVARIANCE_2026_10_29.md §3(b)). RG2 p.20 bounds (LM)⁴α₀ and (LM)⁴α₁ separately (map l.1252), which g small gives.
  - The phase and gradient part of K is derived on the M-factored domain ((iii), *The M-factored domain*), which agrees with Bałaban's T-step inside □₀ (RG1 p.275 l.2–5) and is the note's own extension outside it. Bałaban's generalized axial gauge (RG1 (3.26)–(3.29), p.275–276) keeps the phase away from the minimiser by a different device, which the M-factored domain does not need.
  - Where this sits: at complex external data the R-step's integrand evaluates the insertions at the old configuration U_k^{(0)}: Bałaban's U_k″ = (exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0)^{u_k″^{−1}} ((1.49)–(1.51)), further deformed by the N preliminary steps of LFR I (p.181 (1.21), p.191 l.39–41). There the Schwarz lemma in the background bounds the deformations' complex parts only up to a factor L^{k−m}, with k − m up to N = R_j. VAR Proposition 9's data derivatives remove that factor ((iii) below): for the phase in the top-layer weight, and for the gradient and the current in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights.
