  - the mixed configurations U^{𝒦}, for the phase condition;
  - the layer-weighted gradient and 𝐉 conditions of 𝒟^λ_k, with SW's consumers re-checked (*The dilated sub-domains*);
  - the reduced frame of the deformations, and their gradient and current on every layer ((iii));
  - the 𝐉 coordinate at the evaluation points, the configuration's part and the data's ((iii), *The 𝐉 coordinate*);
  - quoted: the complex curvature conditions (2.34), (2.35), and (2.37), and (2.36) about 0, at the evaluation points ((iii), *The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points*);
  - the phase and gradient part of K, on the M-factored domain ((iii), *The M-factored domain*), with T_Y reduced by 4K, the transfer to step k+1 included ((iii), *The bounds*, parts (a) and (b));
  - the gradient and the current at the start of the chain ((iii), *The starting values*), with λ enlarged by C_Λ′;
  - the current part of K, by T_Y's α₀-entry ((iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*; *The constants*).
- Where it enters: every bound of an R-step at complex external data.
  - For one loop: D2.7 item 5(b)'s uniform bound sup‖w_X‖ ≤ e^{2ΣA}, 15.3's activities, 15.6's |Ψ(S)| and |Ψ_g|, 15.6(c)'s B(g) through e^{2ΣA} and e^{A_k}, the Cauchy step of 15.6(d) on the extension domain of radius r^{ext}_k, 15.6(e)'s att charge, 15.6(f)'s members, 15.7, 15.8(b)'s member masses, and item 7 with 15.8(c)'s mass e^{3+2ΣA}. 15.1, at the flat reference, uses real data only, and with them the base-point conditions (*The remaining parts*, *The real base points*). So the continuum note's (4.5), (5.6), (6.3), and (8.1)–(8.3) need (R-eval) as well.
  - For the pair, in addition: in (6B), the cross members' factors (*15.5*, *15.6 for the cross groups of the R-gas*' ‖D_g^×‖ ≤ B(g), *The payment*'s member part of Ψ_g, *Second case: g has a carried cross member*, *The rooted sum*, *15.8*), and the parts with 𝒦 ≠ ∅ of the dev members and of the attached insertions (D2.7 15.5(i)); in (6C), the constant C_Ψ″ and E(Y_i) with item 5(b)'s mass (*Second case*), and the member sum x_g (*The single loop's R-made groups*). So the R-step part of the cross carry lemma, and with it (8.2), is derived only up to this input.
- The derivation: the mechanism of LFR I's proof of its (1.89) (p.197–200), which LFR II p.375 l.24–27 names as the proof of (1.65). There the M⁵ response is beaten by the age of Λ and by its distance from the newer layers. For the class it has three parts. The insertions are evaluated at a transport form Φ of U_k^{(0)}, whose complex part is the data's own plus a response sourced on Λ. The response lands on layers at least N₀ old, where dilated sub-domains have room for it. The class's bounds are carried on the dilated sub-domains. The bullets below are derived or quoted, except for T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), the conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative and at P of the re-projected points ((iii), *At s ≠ 1*, and *The bounds*, part (a)), CRE (2.39) at the axial form P(𝐔), and the conjugation factors of the covariant members and of the outer fields at part (b)'s pairs (**Open (R-eval-curv)**). The bound B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K, for the real base current in part (b)'s frame term, B_J the sup of (L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, is derived at l.173 and reviewed (packet JB). Their bounds hold on 𝓜^c ((iii), *The scope*), hence on the M-factored domain ((iii), *The M-factored domain*).
  - *The transport form (derived).* Let U^{post}(V) := U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), V) be the post-R minimiser map, with Bałaban's localized determining set 𝐁_k″(Y₁) = 𝐁(Y₁) ∪ 𝐁_k″ (LFR II p.373 l.15–17; the joining is CRE (2.14), p.257), so that U = U^{post}(M(U)) at every real post-R minimiser U (by nesting and LFR II (1.61); *The M-factored domain*, *At s = 1*). M is the averaging of 𝐁_k″(Y₁), which is finer than the post-R 𝐁_k on Z; U_k^0 ∘ M needs these finer averages, since (1.50) takes data on Z at the scales of 𝐁_k″. Let E(𝐔) := U_k^0(M(𝐔))·U^{post}(M(𝐔))^{−1} and Φ(𝐔) := E(𝐔)·𝐔, bondwise in G^c. Φ is analytic wherever U_k^0 ∘ M ((1.65)) and U^{post} ∘ M are. Its identity with Bałaban's configuration holds through P := U^{post} ∘ M: Φ(P(U)) = U_k^0(M(U)) at every real configuration U, since M(P(U)) = M(U) and U^{post}(M(P(U))) = P(U) (*The M-factored domain*, *At s = 1*). Off the real minimisers 𝓜 := {U real : U = P(U)}, Φ(U) itself need not equal U_k^0(M(U)). This includes the gauge transforms U = V^g of V ∈ 𝓜 with g not the extension g̃ that U^{post}'s gauge fixing gives to g's values at the block points: there P(U) = V^{g̃}, so Φ(U) = U_k^0(M(U))·(V^{g̃})^{−1}V^g bondwise. The class's bounds are therefore taken at Φ ∘ P. So the R-step's integrand at real data is unchanged when the insertions are evaluated at Φ ∘ P instead of at Bałaban's U_k^0 ∘ M, with 𝐇_k″(g_kB) and the N preliminary deformations applied as left factors, as in (1.51) and LFR I p.191 l.39–41 (the left factor exp iη𝐇^{(n)}_k at l.40), and with the later sources conjugated by the accumulated gauge transformation ((iii), *The reduced frame*). The gauge transformation u_k″ of (1.51) is dropped. Every word is the trace of an ordered product of insertions evaluated at one configuration, so it does not change, and each insertion changes by a real conjugation, which preserves its norm. At 𝐔 = e^{iηA′}U the base point of Φ(𝐔) is U_k^0(U). Its complex part is Ad(E(U))A′ plus the complex part of E(𝐔)E(U)^{−1}, up to second-order terms. No re-minimisation of the data enters off Λ.
  - *The response is sourced on Λ (derived).* Off Λ the determining set and the field of U_k^0 are those of the new configuration ((1.50), p.370 l.3–8). So U_k^0(M(𝐔)) and U^{post}(M(𝐔)) differ only through their data on Λ. The first takes the averages of U_0(𝐔) := U_0^{(AL)} at M^k(𝐔) on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} and the real V″ on the rest of Λ, Λ^{old}, where V″ are T′_k(X)'s integration variables V_h (p.370 l.4, p.378 l.31); the second takes 𝐔's own field. Here s_Z(𝐔) is the sup of 𝐔's phase per fine bond on Z, and L^kη = 1. The bound has four steps.
    - The scale-k response. U_0(𝐔) = U_{k,Z}(M^k(𝐔), V_Λ(M^k(𝐔))) has its data at the scale k on all of Z, since V_Λ is defined on Z^{(k)} (p.365 l.13). By (1.38) and (1.42) its data differ from those of U_0(U) by B̃′ = Q̃_k(ηA′) off Λ and by B′_Λ(B̃′) on Λ (p.366 l.28–32, p.367 l.19–23). B̃′ is an average of sums of L^k fine phases, so |B̃′| ≤ O(1)L^ks_Z. And |B′_Λ| ≤ 4d(100M)⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃²|B̃′| by (1.41) (p.367 l.15–17).
    - The fine field at the scale k. VAR (190) (p.308 l.37–44) bounds the derivative of the minimiser's field in the data by O(1)(L^jη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8}, for the target in layer j and the data at y′ in layer j′, with PRT2 (2.46)'s distance (p.231 l.11; VAR p.288 l.45 takes d(y, y′) from its [3], which is PRT2, VAR p.309 l.23), which measures each layer's part in that layer's block units. With j = j′ = k and the mean value theorem along the data, the fine phase of U_0(𝐔)U_0(U)^{−1} at x splits into the response to B̃′ and the response to B′_Λ. The first is at most O(1)L^{−k}sup|B̃′| ≤ O(1)s_Z, since the sum over the source blocks of e^{−δ₀d/8} is O(1). The second is at most C_{rsp}M⁵s_Z e^{−δ₀d(x,Λ)/16}, with C_{rsp} = O(1)4d·100⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃², the sum over the source blocks y′ ⊂ Λ of e^{−δ₀d(x,y′)/16} being O(1).
    - The averages. On Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} the data of U_k^0 are the averages of U_0(𝐔) at the scales j of 𝐁″_k there ((1.50)). A j-average's phase is at most O(1)L^j times the sup of the fine phase on its block. So the data of U_k^0 and U^{post} differ on a j-block y′ of Λ by a phase at most O(1)L^j(C_{rsp}M⁵ + 1)s_Z e^{−δ₀d(y′,Λ)/16}. The +1 bounds, up to O(1), the response to B̃′ (previous step) and 𝐔's own phase; the latter is also the difference on the rest of Λ, where U_k^0 takes the real V″.
    - Across the layers of 𝐁″_k(Y₁). VAR (190) again, now with the data on Λ's layers. A source of j′-block phase S at y′ gives at a target x of layer i a fine phase at most O(1)L^{−i}S e^{−δ₀d(x,y′)/8}. For i ≥ j′ this is at most O(1)L^{−j′}S e^{−δ₀d/8}. For i < j′ a contour from y′ to x crosses the layers i+1, …, j′−1 entirely. Consecutive layers are separated by at least one layer of M-cubes of their own scale: {Ω″_j} is an admissible sequence (LFR I p.179 l.40), and admissibility based on partitions into M-cubes in the corresponding scales means this separation ("Thus … separated by one layer of M-cubes", p.179 l.20–22). This covers the layers in Z and outside it. VAR's d is the distance (2.46) of its [3] (VAR p.288 l.45; PRT2 p.231 l.10–12), which counts a contour in blocks of each layer's own scale. So each fully crossed layer adds at least M to d, and L^{j′−i}e^{−δ₀d/16} ≤ L(Le^{−δ₀M/16})^{j′−i−1} ≤ L once Le^{−δ₀M/16} ≤ 1, which M large given L gives.
    - The mixed term. The steps above bound the response to the complex Λ-data difference, T₁ of (iii), *The starting values γ_Φ and κ_Φ*. Since E(U) ≠ 1, there is a second first-order term. At the real base U the two maps' real data on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} differ by δ^{re}, with |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd²)ε_k per block of 𝐁_k″(Y₁) and M₀ = O(1)B₅M⁶ (*The two minimisers*, (iii); LFR II p.356 l.37 and p.378 l.13–14), plus O(KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k at the T-step's real base U_{k+1} ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a), *The base near the support*). On Λ^{old} = Λ∖Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} they differ by T′_k(X)'s integration variables V_h against U's averages, which is not bounded. Its part T₂^{old} of the term is **Open (R-eval-old)** (top of the note). So the shared complex change Δd of the data passes through two minimisers with different real Λ-data. In the Landau representative relative to U, the part sourced on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} is T₂ := ∫₀¹[κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)]Δd dt with κ := δ𝓗/δB, as in (iii). Δd has phase at most O(1)L^{j′}λα_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ O(1)λα_{1,k} on a j′-block, since a j′-average's phase is at most O(1)L^{j′} times the fine phase (previous steps) and 𝐔 obeys 𝒟^λ_k's phase condition. So for g small given λ, Δd lies within half of VAR's ball |B| < r_B = C₁^{VAR}ε₁/2, whose radius does not depend on g (*The printed inputs*: VAR Proposition 9, p.309, and (172), p.305). w ↦ κ(tΔd ⊕ wδ^{re}) is then analytic for |w| ≤ r_B/(2|δ^{re}|), and (190) bounds it there, pointwise in (x, y′) with its decay, since (190) holds uniformly on the ball. By the Cauchy estimate in w, |κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)| ≤ (4|δ^{re}|/r_B) times (190)'s bound. A source y′ of layer n′ carries at most O(1)L^{n′}λ^{(k)}_{n′}α_{1,k}L^{−k} of Δd. At a target x of layer i, (190) gives at most O(1)L^{n′−i}λ^{(k)}_{n′}α_{1,k}L^{−k}e^{−δ₀d(x,y′)/8}. For n′ > i the previous step gives L^{n′−i}e^{−δ₀d/16} ≤ L, and λ^{(k)}_{n′} ≤ λ^{(k)}_i. For n′ < i, L^{n′−i} ≤ 1, and λ^{(k)}_{n′} ≤ c′^{i−n′}λ^{(k)}_i, while the i − n′ − 1 fully crossed layers give c′^{i−n′}e^{−δ₀d/16} ≤ c′(c′e^{−δ₀M/16})^{i−n′−1} ≤ L, since c′ < L. The sum over y′ of e^{−δ₀d/16} is O(1). So T₂'s phase at a bond b of layer i is at most C_{mx}ε_kλ^{(k)}_iα_{1,k}L^{−k}, with C_{mx} := O(1)L(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})/r_B, which depends on d, L, and M and is fixed before g. The same uniformity of (190) on the ball makes the mean-value steps above hold at complex intermediate data.
    - So, per fine bond b of layer i, the complex part of E(𝐔)E(U)^{−1} is at most C_ΛM⁵e^{−δ dist(b,Λ)}s_Z(𝐔) + C_{mx}ε_kλ^{(k)}_iα_{1,k}L^{−k}, up to second-order terms and up to T₂^{old}, which is open (**Open (R-eval-old)**), with C_Λ := O(1)L(C_{rsp} + M^{−5}) and δ := δ₀/32. dist is measured in PRT2's distance (2.46), which dominates the distance on the scale k since L^j ≤ L^k. C_Λ is bounded, uniformly in M ≥ 1, by a constant depending on d and L only, since B₃ depends on d and L only (VAR p.279 l.28) and γ₀, the constant of [10]'s (1.67) that LFR II (1.7) uses (p.358 l.3–5), on d only. LFR II's [10] is PRT1 (p.392 l.24), whose (1.67) and its γ₀ "dependent on d only" are at p.29 l.27–30. δ = δ₀/32 depends on d and L only, as VAR's δ₀ does: it is PBG Theorem 3.12's rate (VAR p.285 l.25; PBG p.423 l.39). In particular it does not depend on CRE's C₀ or C₁. Bałaban's (1.45) (p.367 l.40–45) bounds the same averages uniformly in j, which per fine bond would lose L^{k−j}. The steps above use (1.41) and (190) instead. The extension is taken on U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), the top-layer radius on Z (p.366 l.23–27), which holds at the points where it is used (*Where it lands*).
  - *Where it lands (quoted, with the bound on s_Z derived).* Before R, Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Ω^c_{k₀+1} (LFR I p.192 l.43), with k₀ = k − N₀ (p.181 l.40), so Λ is at least N₀ layers old. On Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1}, k₀ < m < k, the exponential is at most exp(−4δ(m − k₀)M) (p.200 l.12–19), and Bałaban makes the M⁵ term small there "for M large enough" (p.200 l.20). N₀ is fixed by L^{−N₀+1}R_{k−N₀+1} = 1 (p.200 l.30–31), so N₀ → ∞ as g → 0. After R all of Z lies in the new Ω_k, the components of both classes: the new determining set has Γ_k = Γ″_k ∪ Z^{(k)} ((1.33), p.365 l.2–7), and Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)} (CRE (2.2), p.255 l.15–16). A second-class component carries the scale-k large-field function χ^c_k(Y_i^{∼−6}) inside the new Ω_k ((1.72), p.379 l.17), not an older layer. So at the points where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause, s_Z ≤ ((1−β)/2 + h_k/α_{1,k})α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ α_{1,k}L^{−k}, by the family's bound on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} at step k+1 (next bullet) and (b3)'s 𝐇-part.
  - *The dilated sub-domains (derived).* For λ ≥ 1, let 𝒟^λ_k be the set of (A′, 𝐉) in 𝒟_k that obey three conditions on every Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n ≤ k. Here f_n := 1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}), λ^{(k)}_n := min(λ, c′^{k−n}), and c′ := (1−β)L/(2(1+β₀)) > 1, that is, L > 2(1+β₀)/(1−β), which the map's L ≥ L₀ gives.
    - The phase: |η_kA′(b)| < f_nλ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k}, SW (b1)'s condition times λ^{(k)}_n.
    - The gradient: (L^nξ)²|∇A′| < f_nλ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{n−k}.
    - The current: (L^nξ)³|𝐉 − J_k| < f_nλ^{(k)}_nα_{0,k}L^{n−k}.
    - The last two are CRE (2.39) and (2.36) with α_{i,n} replaced by λ^{(k)}_nα_{i,k}L^{n−k}. In PBG's norms, with L^kη = 1 and ξ = η, they read |∇A′|_{(−1)} < λα_{1,k} and |𝐉 − J_k|_{(−2)} < λα_{0,k}, up to the factors f_n and the cap. On Ω_k they are SW (b1)'s conditions. On an older layer they are weaker than (b1)'s top-layer weights, by L^{k−n} for the gradient and by L^{2(k−n)} for the current. These weights are (190)'s: its gradient entry carries one power of (L^iη)^{−1} more than its first entry, and its D*D entry two more.
    - 𝒟^1_k contains SW's 𝒟^{sub}_k and agrees with it on Ω_k. The clause is asserted on 𝒟^λ_k, with λ of *The R-step lands in the family*, from now on, which the consumers below allow. 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k: on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, m := k − n, 𝒟_k's radii are L^mα_{i,n}/α_{i,k} times f_nα_{i,k}L^{−m}. For i = 1 this factor is at least (L/(1+β₀))^m ≥ c′^m, by the one-step bound α_{1,j+1} ≤ (1+β₀)α_{1,j} (map l.696, from CRE (2.6) with R-cpl₀) iterated m times. For i = 0 it is at least L^m/((1+β₀)²√m) (map (6)(e), *Older layers*), which is ≥ c′^m since (2(1+β₀)/(1−β))^m ≥ (1+β₀)²√m for β₀ ≤ 1/2.
    - Data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} have phase per fine bond below λ^{(k+1)}_n((1+β₀)/L)α_{1,k}L^{−k} = min(λ(1+β₀)/L, ½(1−β)c′^{k−n})α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k}. This is (b3)'s margin times λ^{(k)}_n ≥ 1, so (b3)'s 𝐇-part fits as before, and (b3)–(b4) run on the family. The gradient transfers with the same ratio, since λ^{(k+1)}_nα_{1,k+1}L^{n−k−1} ≤ c′((1+β₀)/L)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{n−k}. The current transfers with α_{0,k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)²α_{0,k} (the same bound at m = 1), so its ratio is at most ½(1−β)(1+β₀) ≤ ¾(1−β). This leaves ¼(1−β) of the room for the 𝐇-part's current.
    - (b2)'s size: j* (map l.318) is taken with r_J(b) := f_nα_{0,k}L^{n−k}(L^nξ)^{−3}, the current's radius above. Then |𝐉 − J_k|/r_J < λ, so j* ≤ 2λS₃₀α_{1,k}, while a* uses the phase only. So ρ̃_k(a* + j*) ≤ 3λS₃₀(C₁/min(C₀, C₁))ρ_k = O(λρ_k), with ρ_k = C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ (map l.366). For fixed λ, g_K small absorbs it.
    - *The consumers of the gradient and current conditions (derived).* In SW, the gradient condition enters (6b)'s Schwarz radius R′ (SW l.93), and the current condition enters j* and the same radius. (b3)'s 𝐇-part, (b3)–(b4)'s data transfer, D2.6's att bounds, (6c)'s covariance-note inputs, and map l.1498's c₀-rotation charge are the other consumers (the sub-bullets below). R′ needs z(A + a″) and z(𝐉 − J_{k+1}) in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain for |z| < R′, and that domain has Bałaban's layer radii.
      - Let ‖a″‖_{sub} be the larger of sup L^{k+1}ξ|a″| and sup over layers of L^{k+1}ξ·L^nξ|∇a″|. Let ‖𝐉 − J‖_{sub} be the sup over layers of (L^nξ)³L^{k+1−n}|𝐉 − J_{k+1}|. On the family these are at most λ times their (6b) values, α_{1,k+1} and 2α_{0,k+1}.
      - For |z| < R′ the layer-n gradient and current are then below c₀ᾱ_{k+1}L^{n−k−1} in the weights (L^nξ)² and (L^nξ)³. This is inside α_{1,n} and α_{0,n}, since L^{k+1−n}α_{i,n} ≥ α_{i,k+1}. So (6b) and (6c) run on the family, with the factor λ in a* and j*, absorbed as in (b2). This places the points in that domain through the gradient and the current only; its conditions (2.34)–(2.37) need the margin of **Open (R-eval-curv)**.
      - (b3)'s 𝐇-part: (6)(e)'s *Older layers* bounds the deformation's gradient and current in SW (b3)'s top-layer weights, which imply the layer weights. (b3)–(b4)'s data transfer is the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (l.54).
      - D2.6's att bounds. D2.6 states them as sup bounds on 𝒟^{sub} (map l.308, l.1445). The class asserts them on the family, as the cross clause ((R-eval-cr), *Production at the T-steps*).
        - Production at step j on 𝒟^λ_j. A new att(j, ·) group's vertex sups are Bałaban's activities, bounded on 𝒟_j ⊃ 𝒟^λ_j, and old insertions bounded by their clauses on the family. Its transports satisfy map l.1407's bound with λα_{1,j} in place of α_{1,j} once c_πλα_{1,j}M ≤ ν/64, and map l.1496's and l.1498's conditions once c_πλα_{1,j}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16. (S-w)'s right side is 2C_winε^{new} + C_win(ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k})²/ε_* (map l.1574), with ε^{new} ≤ C_Mρ_{k+1}·pl + K_β(b_k/ᾱ_k)ε^{old} (map l.1577). Its source term grows on the family by at most λ², through the dev sups' λ ((b2)) and the transports' conditions above, so the att production constants are multiplied by λ², source terms fixed after L and M. The old masses do not compound across the steps. Map l.1581's recursion C^{(k+1)} ≤ 2C_winC_M + θ_kC^{(k)} closes uniformly in k only for the real-point part (map l.1580). The complex-data part is bounded from the production constants, Σ_{j≤k}E_j ≤ Ĉ·max_{j≤k}N^{(j)}ρ_{k+1}pl_{k+1} (covariance note §4, l.141), and the recursion used is C^{(k+1)} ≤ (1+Ĉ)max_{j≤k+1}N^{(j)} + L^{−1}(1+β₀)^{15}C^{(k)}, which gives C^{(k)} ≤ C_S′ := max(2(1+Ĉ)C^{(1)}, 8(1+Ĉ)C_winC_M) once (1+Ĉ)s̄ ≤ 1/4 (covariance note l.149–157; map l.1582). On the family:
          - The production constant N^{(j)} ≤ 2C_winC_M + s_{j−1}C^{(j−1)} (j ≥ 2; covariance note l.131, map l.1581) gains λ² in its source term and at most λ⁴ in s_{j−1}, through the (β) term (λ²) and the quadratic term, which carries C^{(j−1)} again (λ⁴).
          - The carry term L^{−1}(1+β₀)^{15} is unchanged, since a carried insertion's sup bound does not grow on the family ((R-eval-cr), *The carry*).
          - Ĉ is unchanged: its inputs are the covariance note's top-layer bounds, which the family leaves as they are ((6c)'s inputs below).
          - C^{(1)} gains at most λ², as a production constant.
          - So the recursion closes with C^{(k)} ≤ λ²C_S′ for all k ≤ K, once (1+Ĉ)λ⁴s̄ ≤ 1/4. The term j = 1 of the max is (1+Ĉ)N^{(1)}, with N^{(1)} = C^{(1)} at most λ² times its 𝒟^{sub} value (previous sub-bullet), so it is at most λ²C_S′/2. For j ≥ 2, with C^{(j−1)} ≤ λ²C_S′ by induction: (1+Ĉ)2λ²C_winC_M ≤ λ²C_S′/4, (1+Ĉ)λ⁴s̄·λ²C_S′ ≤ λ²C_S′/4, and L^{−1}(1+β₀)^{15}λ²C_S′ ≤ λ²C_S′/8. In either case the total is at most 5λ²C_S′/8. s̄ → 0 for g_K small ((R-m′), map l.1580), so the condition holds for g_K small given λ. C^{(k)} stays at most λ² times its 𝒟^{sub} bound C_S′, uniformly in k, and the λ⁴ is not compounded.
          - These bounds use the polymer conditions with the masses read on the family. The old masses grow by at most λ² there: the dev sups by λ ((b2)), and the att masses by the λ² of C^{(k)}. So (S-w)'s condition ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ ε_*/2 (map l.1571), (12)(e)'s window counts, and D2.6 §5.2–5.3's (T0) condition (163 + C_win)M(ε^{new} + ε^{old} + ε^{ref}) ≤ ν_r/8 (D2.6 l.89) are needed with λ²ε^{old} in place of ε^{old}. D2.6 l.89 reads (T0) as a smallness of g under (R-m′), and the map's l.1571 condition is one as well, since ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ 2L⁴C(M/ν_e²)C^{(k)}ρ_k·pl_k (map l.1580) vanishes with g. The factor λ² is fixed before g, so all three hold for g_K small given λ.
        - The carry, D2.6 §5.4. §5.4 compares the transports of an insertion made at step j with their production value, over the carry length k + 1 − j (D2.6 l.96). On a layer n the family's phase room per fine bond is λ^{(k+1)}_nα_{1,k+1}L^{−(k+1)} at step k + 1 and λ^{(j)}_nα_{1,j}L^{−j} at production. Since min(λ, c′^{k+1−n}) ≤ c′^{k+1−j}min(λ, c′^{j−n}) and α_{1,k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)^{k+1−j}α_{1,j} (map l.696), their ratio is at most (c′(1+β₀)/L)^{k+1−j} = ((1−β)/2)^{k+1−j} ≤ 1, on every layer, the layers older than j included. So the transports do not grow, and §5.4's condition reads 4c_πMλα_{1,k+1} ≤ 1, which holds for g small given λ.
        - D2.6 §5.1 uses 𝒟^{sub} as the clause's domain and the chain condition, which the transfer above gives on the family.
      - (6c)'s inputs from the covariance note (SW l.103). That note reads a carried dev insertion W only on X_W ⊂ (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1} (covariance note §0, l.20), and its data bounds there, a, the per-step change, and |𝐉| < r_J^{(k+1)} (l.26–29), are top-layer bounds. On the top layer λ^{(k+1)}_{k+1} = min(λ, 1) = 1, so the family agrees with 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} there, and these bounds, the disc radii R_A, R_J (l.87), R-av in its top-layer uniform form (l.13), and c_πα_{1,j}·4096M ≤ ν (l.110) are used as the note states them, with no factor λ.
      - The c₀-rotation charge (map l.1498, *The charge*). The map bounds the imaginary part of 𝐔_pd per fine bond by α_{1,k} in η_k-units. On the family it is at most λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k} on the layer n: min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 from 𝐇(s, tB′), and λ^{(k+1)}_nα_{1,k+1}/L ≤ ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k} from a″ (the transfer bullet). Since λ^{(k)}_n ≤ λ, the telescoping gives |log c₀^{(k)}| ≤ λC_{c₀}α_{1,k}L^{j−k}(1 + o(1)) on the layer j, and the same for c₀^{(k+1)}(𝐔) at 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^λ_{k+1}. The map's condition becomes 32c_GC_{c₀}λ(2/L)^{k−j}α_{1,k}(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 1. By l.963's estimate its left side is at most λ times a quantity that is small uniformly in k, K, and j for L ≥ 4 and g_K small, so it holds for g_K small given λ. At j = 0, λ^{(k)}_n ≤ λ^{(k)}_0 = min(λ, c′^k) on every layer, since λ^{(k)}_n does not increase with n. With c′/L = (1−β)/(2(1+β₀)) ≤ 1/2 and α_{1,k} ≤ (1+β₀)²k^{1/2}α_{1,1}, this gives Σ_k2C_{c₀}λ^{(k)}_0α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ 2C_{c₀}(1+β₀)²α_{1,1}Σ_kk^{1/2}2^{−k} ≤ 4C_{c₀}(1+β₀)²α_{1,1}, because Σ_kk^{1/2}2^{−k} < 1.35. So on the family, D2.6 §5.0's ρ_ω (and the O4 note §4(f)'s, l.74) is taken with 4C_{c₀} in place of 2C_{c₀}, and the O4 note's C_ω with C_w + 3d + 4C_{c₀} in place of C_w + 3d, since its tilt entries are bounded through ρ_ω. C_{c₀} = 2d + 24d(d+1)B₀e^{16κ_s} is free of λ and fixed before the coupling, so ρ_ω ≪ r_{ω,0} and the O4 note's tilt absorption stand as before.
  - *The R-step lands in the family (derived from the bullets above, except for T₂^{old}: **Open (R-eval-old)**).* Take λ := 8max(C_Λ, C_Λ′λ_R)M⁵/(1−β), with C_Λ′ of (iii), *The starting values*, and λ_R = C₁/C₀ (*The constants*), since T₁'s D*D is measured against the current room α_{0,k} = α_{1,k}/λ_R. λ is fixed after L and M.
    - On a layer n ≤ k₀ the response, apart from T₂^{old}, is at most (C_ΛM⁵ + C_{mx}ε_kλ)α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ¼(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k}, once c′^{N₀} ≥ λ and C_{mx}ε_k ≤ ⅛(1−β) (g small given M).
    - On k₀ < n ≤ k it is, apart from T₂^{old}, at most (C_ΛM⁵e^{−4δM} + C_{mx}ε_kλ^{(k)}_n)α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ¼(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k}, once C_ΛM⁵e^{−4δM} ≤ ⅛(1−β), which M large given L gives (δ depends on d and L only), and C_{mx}ε_k ≤ ⅛(1−β).
    - The transported part lies in the post-R family with (b3)'s margin. Before R a bond's layer is no newer than after R, since R moves bonds only to more recent layers: Γ_j = Γ″_j ∩ Z^c for j < k and Γ_k = Γ″_k ∪ Z^{(k)} ((1.33), p.365 l.2–7). LFR I's preliminary integrations, from step k's original layering, in which Λ's age, p.200's decay, and 𝒟_k are stated, to 𝐁″_k, move bonds the same way: each replaces a part of Γ_j by its version at the scale j + 1 (LFR I (1.12)–(1.16), p.179 l.32–p.180 l.25). So the original layering, 𝐁″_k, and 𝐁_k are ordered from older to newer at every bond. So λ^{(k)}_n is no smaller before R. The factor f_n is no larger, since it decreases as k − n grows, but f_n ≥ 1 − β. The transported part is the data's, at most ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k} (the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}), plus (b3)'s 𝐇-part, at most h_kL^{−k} with h_k ≤ min(β, 1)α_{1,k}/2 (SW (b3); smaller on the older layers). With the response (at most ¼(1−β)λ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k} by the two bullets above) and the deformations (at most ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k}, (iii)), the pre-R phase is at most ((7/8)(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n + β/2)α_{1,k}L^{−k}. This is within the pre-R room f_nλ^{(k)}_nα_{1,k}L^{−k} once β/2 ≤ ⅛(1−β), that is, β ≤ 1/5, the worst case being λ^{(k)}_n = 1. CRE's β is 1/8 (the table's κ row; LF *The choice β = 1/8*).
    - Hence, up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), Φ(𝐔) obeys 𝒟^λ_k's phase condition (pre-R) at every point of 𝓜^c where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause. At the other points the bound goes through P ((iii), *The M-factored domain*). For the gradient and current conditions, the starting values are derived ((iii), *The starting values*), and the deformations' gradient and current are derived ((iii), *The current of the deformations*). Off Λ's decay range the response, apart from T₂^{old}, is at most C_{mx}ε_k times the room, which vanishes with g, so h_k's condition is (b3)'s and T_Y is unchanged by the Λ-response for g small (it is reduced by 4K for the phase and gradient part of K, (iii), *T_Y reduced by 4K*). The same holds for U^{𝒦}, with U^{𝒦}(M(𝐔)) in place of U_k^0(M(𝐔)): its data differ from the post-R data only on the Λ's of the components that keep their old data.
  - *The remaining parts.*
    - (i) The class's other bounds at the points Φ(𝐔) (derived on 𝓜^c for the phase condition, except for T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**); the gradient and current conditions as in (iii); the conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative **Open (R-eval-curv)**). These are the cross members' weights ((R-eval-cr) below), the attached masses inside e^{2ΣA}, and the leading word's transports. Each is asserted on the family 𝒟^λ_k itself, and the points Φ(𝐔) lie in 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*) up to T₂^{old} and up to (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative, so none of them needs a growth factor there. The masses and weights are the sub-bullet *The masses and weights* below. The transports are bounded through A_k, a sup over the evaluation points with A_k(S) ≤ c_πλα_{1,k}ℓ_k(S) at every point of 𝒟^λ_k (*A_k* below), so they are bounded at Φ(𝐔) as at the post-R points. What remains is the size of A_k where the R-step changes the data. Per fine bond, Φ(𝐔)'s phase differs from the transported part by the deformations and by the Λ-response ((iii); *The response is sourced on Λ*). The deformations are at most ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} on every layer ((iii)), and the pre-R budget of *The R-step lands in the family* counts them. The part T₂ of the Λ-response's mixed term, sourced on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, is at most C_{mx}ε_kλα_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} everywhere, for g small given λ (*The mixed term*); its part T₂^{old} is open. Its other part exceeds ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} only where e^{−δ dist(b,Λ)} > (1−β)/(8C_ΛM⁵). So the Λ-response, apart from T₂^{old}, exceeds ¼(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k} only where dist(b, Λ) < δ^{−1}log(8C_ΛM⁵/(1−β)). This lies inside the component's neighbourhood X^∼, which lies in a cube of side 100MR_k + O(1).
      - *The transports.* The loop's two squares meet that cube in O(d) segments of length at most its side, so the leading word's transport exponent there is at most O(d)·100MR_k·λα_{1,k}. With α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} (CRE (2.28)), this is g_k times a polylog, and it is below 1 for g small.
      - *The masses and weights.* The bounds that hold on the family 𝒟^λ_k itself need no growth factor at the points Φ(𝐔), which lie in 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*) up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**) and up to (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative (**Open (R-eval-curv)**).
        - The dev clause is asserted on 𝒟^λ_k (*The dilated sub-domains*), with the factor λ in a* and j* absorbed for g_K small.
        - D2.6's att bounds, and with them the attached masses inside e^{2ΣA}, hold on the family: D2.6 §5.4's condition there is 4c_πMλα_{1,k} ≤ 1 (*The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*).
        - The cross insertions' weights hold on the family: the cross clause (ii^cr), its production in (6), and the cross carry lemma are stated with sup bounds on 𝒟^λ ((R-eval-cr) below).
      - *The payment.* The transports' factor is paid by the margin of (1.89) for a first-class component. For a second-class component Y_i it joins E(Y_i) in the quarter of exp(−p₀(g_k)) ((6C), *The payment of Π_iE(Y_i)*), since 3 + p₀(g_k)/16 + 1 + 7.3's costs at Y_i ≤ ¼p₀(g_k) for g small.
    - (ii) Analyticity on the whole post-R 𝒟_k (derived, given the readings already used at U_k^0). It is taken at Bałaban's configuration, not at Φ.
      - By (1.65), U_k^0 maps the post-R space into Ũ^{(N)c}, the space (1.64)–(1.69) [IV] for n = N (LFR II p.375 l.24). (1.60) evaluates the old terms at exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0 (p.372 l.23–25). It is printed for the terms with X not contained in □^{∼2} and for the second group's boundary terms (p.372 l.21–22), while the terms with X ⊂ Z^c and X ⊂ □^{∼2} (p.372 l.2–4) take (1.59). Bałaban says only that the analyticity domain "is restricted on the cube □₀, or on the domain X, by the analyticity properties of the original term" (p.372 l.33–34). That exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0 lies in Ũ^{(N)c}, where the chain starts, is therefore a reading (the third reading of l.17, *R-chain-start*).
      - If 𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n+1)c}, then exp iηℍ^{(n)}𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n)c}, with no gauge transformation (LFR I p.191 l.39–41). For n = 0 the space is Ũ_k^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.191 l.33–34), CRE's space for the 𝐁-terms ((2.41)(ii), CRE p.261 l.59–60). The old terms' space is RG I's U^c_j (CRE p.259 l.41–42), which is G^c-invariant. With p.375's inclusion read as an inclusion of spaces and R-far″(β), it lies in 𝒟_k ((6C), *The insertions*).
      - So the u-free chain applied to exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0(𝐔) lies in 𝒟_k for every 𝐔 in the post-R 𝒟_k, and every insertion and word is analytic there. The same holds for U^{𝒦}, component by component, since (1.65) is proved for each component separately.
      - At a real post-R minimiser this point equals the transport-form evaluation point, since Φ(U) = U_k^0(M(U)) on 𝓜 (*The transport form*). The real minimisers are a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of 𝓜^c, not of the T-step's open set, so the two functions agree on 𝓜^c only ((iii), *The scope*). The bounds are taken at Φ on 𝓜^c and reach the other points through P. The analyticity is taken at Bałaban's configuration on the whole domain.
    - (iii) The complex parts of the N preliminary deformations and of 𝐇_k″(g_kB) as left factors: the phase, the gradient, and the current derived on every layer, on 𝓜^c, up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**) wherever the smallness of ε comes from ε_Φ (from *One step* on).
      - *Their sources are real.* The source of the n-th factor is the fluctuation V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} of (1.44), with |V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| < 2δ′_j by χ′_j (LFR I p.182 l.53) and δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) (p.183 l.2). The source of 𝐇_k″ is g_kB, with g_k|B| < δ′_k, localized in Λ (LFR II p.360 l.41–43, p.372 l.14–15). So the factors are real at real data, and their complex parts come from the complex background only. (1.45), sup_{B^i(y)} L^iη|ℍ^{(n)}|, sup_{B^i(y)} (L^iη)²|∇_{U_k^{(n+1)}}ℍ^{(n)}| ≤ 4B₃δ′_j exp(−δd(y, Ω^c_{j+1}∖Z″_{j+1})), is printed at the real U_{k,Z}^{(n+1)} (LFR I p.186 l.5–11). The u-free chain's source is s_n = (1/i)log V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1}, relative to V^{(j)} (p.188 l.53), with |s_n| ≤ 2c_χδ′_j (*χ′_j and χ′^{(j)}*), so (1.45) reads O(c_χ)B₃δ′_j for it. That this bound holds on Bałaban's complex spaces is the reading *R-chain-cx*: Bałaban states the complex step's inclusion (p.191 l.39–42) with a proof "almost identical" to (1.44)–(1.52), which rest on (1.45), and VAR's analytic 𝓗 (Proposition 9, p.309 l.2–4) gives the form of the estimate. The gauge transformations u of (1.44) are dropped as u_k″ is.
      - *On the layers k₀ < m ≤ k (derived).* At the points where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause, the data's complex part is s_Z ≤ ((1−β)/2 + h_k/α_{1,k})α_{1,k}L^{−k} ≤ ½α_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (*Where it lands*, with h_k ≤ min(β, 1)α_{1,k}/2). Bałaban's post-R spaces have radius at least α_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond on every layer, so the complex part can be multiplied by 2 inside their phase, gradient, and current conditions; their conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the scaled points need the margin of **Open (R-eval-curv)**. The Schwarz lemma in that factor gives |Im ηℍ^{(n)}| ≤ O(c_χ)B₃δ′_jL^{−m}e^{−δd} per fine bond on layer m. Each exponential is at most 1; for points of Z on a layer m > k₀ the distance is 0 for up to N₀ steps, and the bounds cumulate (p.191 l.54–55). δ′_j ≤ (1+β₀)²N^{β₀}δ′_k (p.192 l.2–3, with k − j ≤ N), and N ≤ R_k (LFR II p.361 l.14). So the sum over the N steps is at most O(c_χ)B₃(1+β₀)²R_k^{1+β₀}δ′_kL^{k−m}·L^{−k}. Here L^{k−m} ≤ L^{N₀} = L·R_{k−N₀+1} (p.179 l.9), R_m ≤ L(log g_m^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)), and log g_m^{−2} ≤ 2log g_k^{−2} for k − m ≤ N₀ (CRE (2.6)). So the sum is at most O(1)g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₁+(2+β₀)r}L^{−k}, against α_{1,k} = λ_RC₀g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} with q₁ = q₀ > p₀ + 3r ≥ p₁ + 1 + 3r (LFR I p.198 l.55). For g small it is below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k}, inside the room that 𝒟^λ_k leaves after the transported part and the response.
      - *On Z's layers m ≤ k₀: the phase, the gradient, and the current (derived on 𝓜^c).* The bound of the previous bullet carries the factor L^{k−m} there, and k − m runs up to N = R_j (LFR II p.384 l.17). The deformations are bounded instead through their dependence on the external data, whose derivatives VAR Proposition 9 bounds. Here i is the layer of the point x, y ∈ Λ_i the block with x ∈ Δ(y), and j the layer of step n's collar.
        - *The printed inputs.* VAR Theorem 1 (p.279) asks (7), |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε₁ on 𝔅_k with one ε₁ for all layers, and ε₁ ≤ a₁, with a₀, a₁, B₃ depending on d and L only; uniqueness needs B₃ε₁ ≤ a₀. So ε₁ is a fixed constant with M′ε₁ ≤ a₁ (p.305 l.6), and the real stage data meet (7) with room for g small. By Proposition 9 (p.309) and (172) (p.305), U_k(V′V_0) extends analytically to V′ = e^{iB} on |B| < r_B := C₁^{VAR}ε₁/2, a radius independent of g. Here C₁^{VAR} is the C₁ of VAR (172), VAR's absolute constant (p.305 l.44), not CRE's C₁; in the rest of this bullet C₁ is C₁^{VAR}. (172) assumes |V′ − 1| < C₁ε₁ and infers |B′| < 2C₁ε₁ (p.305 l.31–33), so the ball of B is the one inside the assumption: for |B| < C₁ε₁/2 ≤ log 2 (ε₁ small, as (172) takes it, l.36), |e^{iB} − 1| ≤ |B|e^{|B|} < C₁ε₁. On this ball U_k(V′V_0)U_k(V_0)^{−1} in the Landau gauge is exp iη𝓗(B), with 𝓗(0) = 0. B is dimensionless: H₀B minimises on L^{j(·)}ηQA′ = B (p.297 (129)). On the ball, (190) bounds δ𝓗_μ(B, x)/δB_ν(y′) by O(1)(L^iη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}exp(−δ₀d(y, y′)/8), y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, and its x-gradient by the same with (L^iη)^{−2}. Sums over y′ carry the weights (L^{j′}η)^d ((130)). (190) follows from (189), which Bałaban states as "a final result only", the calculation not performed (p.308 l.28–30), with (188), Lemma 2.1, Proposition 2, and (181) (l.36); it is accepted from Bałaban.
        - *The stages are VAR minimisers.* LFR I represents its stage configurations by VAR's 𝓗 on their determining sets: (1.30), (1.34) ("in the standard way"), (1.44)–(1.45), with B₃ of VAR Theorem 1 (p.191 l.28). This application is accepted from Bałaban. The u-free chain's step n is exp iηℍ^{(n)}(s_n)𝕌 with the real source s_n = g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j) of the integration variable B_j (p.191 l.39–41). Its complex part comes only from the background 𝕌.
        - *The data.* Fix a real base point U of 𝐔 = e^{iηA′}U. The R-step's integration variables are real ((6C), *The integration variables are real*), so the stage data at 𝐔 and at U differ by Δd, the complex part of the external data. The phase per fine bond is at most λα_{1,k}L^{−k}, and a layer-j′ block bond averages over L^{j′} fine bonds, so |Δd(y′)| ≤ C_dλα_{1,k}L^{j′−k}, with C_d depending on d. Every stage is a function of the same Δd.
        - *One step.* Let U₀ be the real stage-(n+1) configuration at U, and write the u-free chain's stage-(n+1) configuration at 𝐔 as 𝕌 = e^{iηA_Φ}U₀, bondwise. The norms are PBG's (3.41), |A|_{(α)} := sup_i sup_{Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}}(L^iη)^{−α}|A|. ε_{n+1} := sup|A_Φ|_{(0)} is the stage-(n+1) deviation from the real chain, the sup taken over the segment 𝐔_t := e^{itηA′}U, t ∈ [0, 1] (*The sum* bounds it there). Let d_{n+1} = (Δd_L, B*) be 𝕌's plain block data relative to those of U₀, with Δd_L on the external blocks and B* on step n's collar, and 𝕎 := e^{iη𝓗(d_{n+1})}U₀ its VAR representative (*The Landau representatives*, below). A layer-j′ block variable is a product of at most O(1)L^{j′} fine bond variables along a path, each with phase at most η|A_Φ|, so |d_{n+1}(y′)| ≤ C_GL^{j′}ηε_{n+1} on y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, with C_G = O(1) depending on d, far below r_B once ε_{n+1} ≤ 2ε_Φ (*The induction*). With the stage-n 𝓗 at the base U₀, put F(d, s) := η𝓗(Δd_L, s ⊕ B*) − η𝓗(Δd_L, B*), where e^{i(s⊕B*)} = e^{is}e^{iB*}. F(·, 0) = 0, since 𝓗 at s = 0 is the stage-(n+1) minimiser, which meets the collar constraints: 𝓗(Δd_L, B*) = 𝓗_{n+1}(d_{n+1}) =: A_L. F is analytic for |s| < r_B/2, and its d-derivative obeys (190) uniformly there.
        - *The Schwarz bound.* At real integration variables |s| ≤ 2c_χδ′_j (*χ′_j and χ′^{(j)}*). The Schwarz lemma in s gives |∂_dF| ≤ (12c_χδ′_j/r_B)·O(1)L^{−i}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8} for |s| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j. So |F(d_{n+1}, s) − F(0, s)| ≤ O(δ′_j/r_B)L^{−i}Σ_{y′}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8}|d_{n+1}(y′)| ≤ O(δ′_j)C_GS_L·ηε_{n+1} per fine bond at x, with S_L := sup_y Σ_{y′}L^{j′−i}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/16} ≥ 1.
        - *S_L is finite.* VAR's d is the distance (2.46) of its [3] (VAR p.288 l.45), PRT2 p.231 l.10–12: the length of a contour counted in blocks of each layer's own scale. A fully crossed layer is at least one layer of M-cubes of its own scale wide (*Across the layers of 𝐁″_k(Y₁)* in packet A), so it adds at least M ≥ M/M₁ to d. The newer layers' factor L^{j′−i} and the older layers' block count L^{d(i−j′)} are therefore beaten once δ₀M/(16M₁) ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1. This is a condition on M given d, L, and M₁, of the kind of LFR I's δM/M₁ ≥ 2 (p.186). A localized set 𝐁(Y) ∪ 𝐁_k″ (CRE (2.14)) adds the strip Y∖Y⁰ of width at most 2M₁ (LFR II p.373 l.26–27), whose layers satisfy dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}^c) ≥ L^nξM₁ (CRE p.256 l.61). A fully crossed strip layer is therefore at least M₁ blocks of its own scale wide, adds at least M₁ to d, and its factor L·e^{−δ₀M₁/16} is beaten as above once δ₀M₁/16 ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1. This holds, since M₁ = K_{CRE}M′, with CRE's integer K_{CRE} ≥ 12d + 1 (not VAR's factor K below), and δ₀M′ ≥ 64 log L (table rows 34 and 44), so δ₀M₁/16 ≥ 4(12d + 1)log L.
        - *The gauge representatives (derived).* The words do not depend on the representative, but the bounds do. Membership in Bałaban's spaces, and in 𝒟^λ_k, is a condition on a representative 𝐔 = U′U (CRE p.261, (i)–(iii) and l.60–61). The transported part and the response are bounded in Φ's frame, and F at VAR's representatives. One G^c gauge transformation G relates a stage configuration to its representative. The bullets below bound G by O(1)L^iη times ε_{n+1}, run the chain in a frame where only G enters a step (*The reduced frame*), and run the induction over the steps.
          - *The base.* U₀ obeys VAR (9)–(10) (p.279), which are PBG (3.35)–(3.36) with α₀ = B₃ε₁, and M′B₃ε₁ ≤ a₅ (VAR p.305). VAR applies PBG at its minimisers in the same way (pp.285, 295, 297). So PBG's bounds hold at U₀ for γ ∈ [−4, 4]: |G′f|_{(γ+2)}, |∇G′f|_{(γ+1)}, |G′∇*f|_{(γ+1)} ≤ B₀|f|_{(γ)} ((3.47)), the kernel bound (3.48) for C = (Q′G′²Q′*)^{−1}, and (3.49) for P.
          - *The Landau representatives (derived).* A configuration V near U₀ is in the Landau gauge relative to U₀ if R(U₀)D*_{U₀}(1/iη)log(VU₀^{−1}) = 0 (REG (1.27)). REG's representative is not normalised from an arbitrary element of the orbit. The normalisation \overline{R_0u}^j = 1 ((1.29)) does not respect the group structure, so the set {V^{u^{−1}}} "depends on an element U chosen to generate" it (p.80 l.28–30). REG generates it from the element in the axial gauge Ax_k(𝔅_k, U₀) ((1.28), p.81), "for deeper reasons connected with properties of the averaging operations" (p.82 l.25–26). The transformation u from the axial element is unique (Theorem 4, p.88). It is u = u′u₁ (p.89 l.18), with u₁ the transformation of the inductive hypothesis (p.88 l.18), which "is determined uniquely in terms of U₁" (p.88 l.36). With this generator the representative's double-bar averages are the data: \bar{\bar U}_1^j = e^{iB} with B given by the plain data V through (1.31) (p.82). This is VAR's constraint (20), and VAR Proposition 2 (p.281) takes these representatives.
            - The representatives used here are VAR's. For a stage configuration 𝕌 with plain data V, relative to the base U₀ = U_k(V₀), let 𝕎 := e^{iη𝓗(B)}U₀, with B given by V. By VAR Proposition 9 (p.309) 𝕎 is, by definition, the Landau-gauge form of the axial-gauge minimiser U_k(V), with REG's normalisation from the axial element. At real data 𝕌 is a critical configuration with the data V ((iii), *The stages are VAR minimisers*). The critical orbit is unique (VAR Theorem 1, p.279), and the axial gauge meets the orbit at U_k(V). So 𝕌 = 𝕎^G for a gauge transformation G, which is u_L, taking 𝕎 to U_k(V), followed by w, taking U_k(V) to 𝕌. VAR's orbits are those of the group (4) of gauge transformations with u(y) = 1 on 𝔅_k (VAR p.278 l.16–19, l.32–33), and 𝕌 and U_k(V) lie on the same orbit, so w can be taken in this group: w(y) = 1 at the block points y ∈ 𝔅_k.
            - So 𝓗's argument is 𝕌's own plain data, with no conjugation by G. At step n, 𝕎 = e^{iη𝓗(d_{n+1})}U₀ =: e^{iηA_L}U₀ and 𝕎_n := e^{iη𝓗(d_n)}U₀, where d_{n+1} = (Δd_L, B*) and d_n = (Δd_L, s_n ⊕ B*) are the plain data of 𝕌 and 𝕌^{(n)} relative to U₀'s (*One step*). 𝕌^{(n)} = 𝕎_n^{G_n} in the same way. u_L, w, 𝕎, and 𝕎_n are explicit analytic functions of the configurations, and the identities 𝕌 = 𝕎^G and 𝕌^{(n)} = 𝕎_n^{G_n} hold at real post-R minimisers, so they hold on 𝓜^c (*The scope*). At a real non-minimiser the stages of Φ's chain are not minimisers, and the identities fail there ((iii), *The scope*).
          - *The bound on G (derived).* Write G = e^{iλ̂}. (190) gives |A_L|_{(0)}, |∇A_L|_{(−1)} ≤ O(C_GS_L)ε_{n+1}.
            - At the block points. At a block point y of a layer-i block, w(y) = 1, so G(y) = u_L(y), REG's transformation u = u′u₁ from the axial element U_k(V) to 𝕎 (p.89 l.18). REG bounds it by |u′u₁ − 1| < 16dB₁(α₀ + α₁), "reasoning as in (1.70)–(1.72)" (p.89 l.35). There (1.70)–(1.71) (p.88) bound the transformation at x ∈ B^j(Λ_j) by 2Σ_n max|log(\bar R\bar{\bar U}_1^n)(Γ)|, and these logarithms by the averages |Q_n(U₀, ηA)| over paths of at most dL^{n+1} bonds. With A_L in place of REG's A this gives |λ̂(y)| ≤ 16dL^iη|A_L|_{(0)}.
            - The gradient. Bondwise, 𝕎 = 𝕌^{G^{−1}} reads A_L = N(λ̂) + D₀λ̂, with N(λ̂) := e^{−i ad λ̂}A_Φ + (Φ(i ad λ̂) − 1)D₀λ̂ + R₂/η, Φ(z) := (1 − e^{−z})/z, and R₂ the BCH remainder, at most O(η|A_Φ|·η|D₀λ̂| + |λ̂|(η|D₀λ̂|)²). While |λ̂| and η|D₀λ̂| are at most a small constant c₀, this gives |D₀λ̂|_{(0)} ≤ 2(|A_L|_{(0)} + |A_Φ|_{(0)}) ≤ C_DS_Lε_{n+1}, with C_D = O(C_G).
            - Off the block points. Every point x of a layer-i block is joined to its block point by a path of at most dL^i bonds inside the block. Along a bond b, |λ̂(b₋)| and |λ̂(b₊)| differ by at most 2η|D₀λ̂(b)|, since the real U₀ transports by a norm-preserving Ad. So |λ̂(x)| ≤ |λ̂(y)| + 2dL^iη sup|D₀λ̂|, and |λ̂|_{(1)} ≤ B_Gε_{n+1} with B_G = O(d)(C_G + C_D)S_L.
            - Continuity. Along 𝐔_t, λ̂ is continuous (u_L and w are analytic in the configuration, VAR p.307), and λ̂ = 0 at t = 0, where 𝕌 = U₀. The bounds make |λ̂| and η|D₀λ̂| at most O(S_Lε_{n+1}) < c₀/2 for g small, with ε_{n+1} ≤ 2ε_Φ (*The induction*), so they hold at t = 1. G is not determined by a fixed-point equation, and no bound on G′, R, or C at the complex point enters.
          - *The reduced frame (derived).* Let 𝕌^{(n)} := exp iηℍ^{(n)}(s̃)𝕌 be the u-free step from 𝕌, with the source s̃ of this frame (below), and 𝕌^{(n)} = 𝕎_n^{G_n} as in *The Landau representatives*. Put 𝕌̃^{(n)} := 𝕎_n^G with this step's G, which is 𝕌^{(n)} transformed by a G^c gauge transformation ũ_n made of G_n^{−1} and G.
            - The chain is run on 𝕌̃^{(n)}, with the later sources conjugated by the accumulated ũ at the collars' block points. Its words equal those of the u-free chain, and the class's bounds are taken at this representative. The proof uses no continuation in the gauge variable, because 𝒟_k, like CRE's Ũ^c, is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (CRE p.261 l.42–43). It is the identity theorem in 𝐔 on 𝓜^c, as for the identities of *The Landau representatives*.
              - The base U of *The data*, and with it each U₀, is held fixed, so the reduced chain is a function of 𝐔 alone. At a real post-R minimiser, ũ is then G-valued: 𝕌, 𝕎, 𝕌^{(n)}, and 𝕎_n are real, so G and G_n are (at U itself G = 1, below). The u-free chain's steps are covariant under G-valued gauge transformations: they are built from gauge-covariant operators on Bałaban's spaces, which are G-invariant (LFR I p.191 l.32–33). So the reduced chain is the u-free chain transformed by the real ũ, and the words, which are G-invariant, take the same value on 𝓜.
              - Both words are analytic functions of 𝐔. The u-free chain's words are analytic on the post-R 𝒟_k ((ii)). The reduced chain's words are analytic wherever its data stay in VAR's ball |B| < r_B and its final stage lies in 𝒟_k, since G, u_L, w, 𝕎, and 𝕎_n are analytic in the configuration (*The Landau representatives*). Along each path P(𝐔_t), t ∈ [0, 1], the data are at most C_GL^{j′}ηε_{n+1}, far below r_B (*One step*, with the induction below run over the segment 𝐔_t), and the final stage lands in 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k (*The sum*; *The R-step lands in the family*), both up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), and the landing also up to (2.34)–(2.37) there (**Open (R-eval-curv)**).
              - Each such path in 𝓜^c starts at the real minimiser P(U) ∈ 𝓜. 𝓜 is totally real of maximal dimension in 𝓜^c (*The scope*), so two functions analytic near P(U) that agree on 𝓜 agree on a neighbourhood of P(U) in 𝓜^c. By uniqueness of analytic continuation along the path, they agree at P(𝐔).
            - At U the same construction gives the chain of the real stages' VAR representatives, with G = 1 and ũ_n(U) = G_n(U)^{−1} real. This is a real gauge transform of the real chain, which preserves every norm. U₀ and ε_{n+1} of *One step* refer to this chain and to the reduced chain at 𝐔, and the next step starts from 𝕌̃^{(n)} with its own plain data and its own G.
            - The sources. The reduced real source is s^{re} := Ad_{ũ(U)}s, with ũ(U) the accumulated real ũ, so |s^{re}| = |s| ≤ 2c_χδ′_j. At 𝐔, s̃ := Ad_ũs. At a block point of layer j, ũ_n differs from its value at U by at most O(B_G)L^jη(ε_n + ε_{n+1}), by *The bound on G* for G, and for G_n after the real gauge transformation G_n(U). ũ_n is formed after step n's source is used, so s̃ carries only the ũ_m with m ≥ n + 1, and |s̃ − s^{re}| ≤ O(B_G)L^jηΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1})|s| on step n's collar. With the induction's ε ≤ 2ε_Φ ≤ 4λα_{1,k}, up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), this is at most O((N+1)B_Gλα_{1,k})|s| ≤ ½|s| for g small, since (N+1)α_{1,k} is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}. So |s̃| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j, inside *The Schwarz bound*.
          - *One step's deviation.* The factor of step n in the reduced frame is f := 𝕌̃^{(n)}𝕌^{−1}, and bondwise f_b = (𝕎_n^G)_b(𝕎^G)_b^{−1} = Ad_{G(b₋)}(e^{iηX_b}) exactly, with X := (1/iη)log(𝕎_n𝕎^{−1}). 𝕎_n𝕎^{−1} = e^{iη𝓗(d_n)}e^{−iηA_L}, so X = 𝓗(d_n) − A_L + β = F(d_{n+1}, s̃)/η + β, with the BCH remainder β at most η|𝓗(d_n) − A_L||A_L|. By (190) and |s̃| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j, |X| ≤ O(c_χS_L)δ′_j(L^iη)^{−1}. At U, G = 1 and f(U) = e^{iF(0, s^{re})}, since F carries η. So the step's deviation dev_n := sup_b η^{−1}|log f_b − log f(U)_b| is at most the sum of three terms.
            - (a) η^{−1}|F(d_{n+1}, s̃) − F(0, s̃)| ≤ O(δ′_j)C_GS_Lε_{n+1}, by the Schwarz bound.
            - (b) η^{−1}|F(0, s̃) − F(0, s^{re})| ≤ O(c_χB_G)S_Lδ′_jΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}), by (190) in s and the bound on |s̃ − s^{re}|.
            - (c′) |β| + (e^{2|λ̂|} − 1)|X| ≤ O(c_χ)(C_GS_L + B_G)S_Lδ′_jε_{n+1}, since |Ad_GX − X| ≤ (e^{2|λ̂|} − 1)|X|, |λ̂| ≤ B_GL^iηε_{n+1}, and |A_L| ≤ O(C_GS_L)ε_{n+1}.
            - Hence dev_n ≤ C_*S_L²δ′_jΣ_{m≥n+1}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}), which involves only the stages before n, with C_* depending on d, L, B₃, c_χ, and r_B. No transformation other than G enters, and G enters only through |λ̂|.
          - *The induction.* 𝕌̃^{(n)} = f e^{iηA_Φ}U₀ and the reduced real chain's U₀^{(n)} = f(U)U₀, so 𝕌̃^{(n)}(U₀^{(n)})^{−1} = (f f(U)^{−1})·Ad_{f(U)}(e^{iηA_Φ}). Ad_{f(U)} is a real conjugation, which preserves |A_Φ|, so ε_n ≤ ε_{n+1} + dev_n(1 + O(δ′_j)). Starting from Φ's deviation ε_Φ, over 𝐇_k″(g_kB) and the N deformations, suppose ε_m ≤ 2ε_Φ for every stage before n. Then ε_n ≤ ε_Φ + 2C_*S_L²(N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j·4ε_Φ ≤ 2ε_Φ for g small, since Σ_nδ′_j ≤ (N + 1)(1+β₀)²N^{β₀}δ′_k (p.192 l.2–3) and (N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}. This keeps ε small at every stage, as the bullets above assume.
        - *The sum.* The steps add at most Σ_n dev_n ≤ C_*S_L²(N + 2)Σ_nδ′_j·4ε_Φ to the deviation. ε_Φ ≤ (λ + C_ΛM⁵)α_{1,k}(1 + o(1)) ≤ 2λα_{1,k}, from the transported part and the response (*The R-step lands in the family*), up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**). This bound uses only the phase of A′, which along 𝐔_t is t times that at 𝐔 up to a relative error O(|B|/r_B) (*The scope*), so it holds on the whole segment. So per fine bond the sum is at most C_*O(N^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²λα_{1,k}L^{−k}. With N ≤ O((log g_k^{−2})^ν) (LFR I p.198 l.54, under (1.94)) and δ′_k = g_kA₁p₁(g_k), the factor N^{2+β₀}δ′_k is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}. So for g small the sum is below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k}L^{−k}. 𝐇_k″(g_kB) is the step at the stage of U_k^0 with source g_kB, g_k|B| < δ′_k on Λ (LFR II p.360 l.42, by LFR I (1.82)), and is one step of the induction. The BCH terms between factors are inside the induction, since ε_n is the deviation of the composed configuration.
        - *The scope (derived).* Let 𝓜 := {U^{post}(V): V real} be the real post-R minimisers, and 𝓜^c := {U^{post}(V): V complex, within VAR's radius} its complexification. V ↦ U^{post}(V) is analytic and injective (M(U^{post}(V)) = V), so 𝓜 is a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of 𝓜^c. An identity between analytic functions that holds on 𝓜 therefore holds on 𝓜^c.
          - Φ(U) = U_k^0(M(U)) and the identities of *The Landau representatives* hold on 𝓜, hence on 𝓜^c. On 𝓜^c the segment 𝐔_t of *One step* is taken as U^{post}(e^{itB}V₀), with B := (1/i)log VV₀^{−1}, which stays in 𝓜^c. Since 𝓗(0) = 0 and 𝓗 is analytic on the ball of radius r_B, the complex part along it is t times that at t = 1, up to a relative error O(|B|/r_B). So *The sum*'s bound holds along it.
          - The T-step's points e^{iη𝐇_𝐕(s,tB′)}𝐕, 𝐕 ∈ 𝓜^c_{k+1} (*The M-factored domain*, *The bounds*), include real non-minimisers: real 𝐕 and B′ with s ∈ (0, 1). There Φ(U) ≠ U_k^0(M(U)), and the stages of Φ's chain are not minimisers.
          - The old insertions' post-R function is taken as the word at the u-free chain applied to exp iη𝐇_k″(g_kB)U_k^0(M(𝐔)), a function of M(𝐔) alone. This extension is the note's own. Bałaban introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the usual way into the localized function 𝐇_k … hence into u_{k,Λ}", replaces "M·(U_k), V″ by the corresponding M·(𝐔), and V_Λ^{(A)} by the function V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔))" (LFR II p.374 l.37–40), and introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) into the other expressions "in the usual way" (p.375 l.11–12). So his extension depends on 𝐉, and on 𝐔 beyond M(𝐔) through 𝐇_k and u_{k,Λ}. At a real minimiser U ∈ 𝓜 with its current, (U, J(U)), both are the real post-R function, since P(U) = U and the old terms take the configuration's current (RG1 p.262 l.61–p.263 l.2). Both are analytic, and {(V, J(V)) : V ∈ 𝓜} is a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of the graph {(𝐕, J^h(𝐕)) : 𝐕 ∈ 𝓜^c}, so the two agree on that graph, as far as it lies in the domain Ũ_k^c(Y₄) of Bałaban's extension (LFR II p.375 l.17–20), which at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))) needs the margin of **Open (R-eval-curv)**, including CRE (2.39) at P(𝐔). (1.50)'s determining set and field off Λ are those of the new configuration (p.370 l.4–8), and the regularity off Λ^∼ is "almost the same as for the configuration U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), M·(𝐔))" (p.375 l.12–13). The value of the note's extension at 𝐔 is its value at P(𝐔) := U^{post}(M(𝐔)) ∈ 𝓜^c, and its bounds at 𝐔 are those at P(𝐔).
          - By Proposition 9, P(𝐔)'s complex part in the Landau gauge relative to U^{post}(M(U)) is 𝓗^{post}(Δd). Here Δd is M(𝐔)'s complex part, with |Δd(y′)| ≤ C_dL^{j′}η·sup|A′|. By (190) its phase and gradient are at most K := O(C_dS_L) times 𝐔's room, and its D*D is at most K times the phase room. K's printed constants depend on d and L: B₃ (VAR Theorem 1, p.279 l.28), δ₀ and B₀ (the common rate and constant of PBG Theorem 3.12, "depending on d and L only", PBG p.423 l.39–p.424 l.3, which VAR uses through "Theorem 3.12 from [5]", p.285 l.25, its [5] being PBG, p.309 l.25), and (190)'s O(1), and the multi-layer free constants depend on L through γ₀′ = π²/L² (PRT II p.242 l.42–43). So K is fixed after L and M, and is never compared with L.
        - *The M-factored domain (derived).* Let 𝒟^{λ,M}_k be the set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉) with M(𝐔) within VAR's radius such that, for the localized projection P of each member (*The bounds*), with V̂ = P(𝐔)^{u_L} the VAR representative of P(𝐔) and u_L the G^c gauge transformation from P(𝐔) to it, both (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) and (V̂, J^h(V̂)) lie in 𝒟^λ_k ((𝐔, 𝐉)^u = (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉), RG1 (1.10)). P acts on 𝐔 alone, and 𝐉 is the pair's own coordinate, which the new terms take. J^h(V̂) is the current that the old terms take at P(𝐔) (*The 𝐉 coordinate*). Bałaban's T-step is M-factored only inside □₀: "in the interior averages M·(U_{k+1}) we replace the configuration U_{k+1} by 𝐔", 𝐉 replaces J_{k+1} "as usual", and in 𝐇_k and the Q_j the configuration U_{k+1}(□₀, M·(𝐔)) is used, "extended as equal to 𝐔 outside □₀" (RG1 p.275 l.2–5). The full M-factoring is the note's.
          - The bounds. Every member of the class is taken composed with P: on 𝒟^{λ,M}_k it is Y∘P, with Y its function of the configuration at the pair's 𝐉. For the old insertions' post-R functions this is their extension (*The scope*). For the members that are functions of 𝐔 itself (SW closure (b2)'s Y_W, the att, lw, and wrap members, and D2.6 closure 5.4's transports) it changes their values off 𝓜^c. On 𝓜^c, P is the identity (*At s = 1*), so the values at the real minimisers, where the effective density is evaluated at s = 1, are unchanged. The values at the real s-decoupled points do change. The cluster expansion uses the members at s = 1, at s = 0 through the factorisation over the decoupled components, and analytically in s in between. Locality. P is local: U^{post} = U(𝐁_k″(Y₁), ·), with 𝐁_k″(Y₁) = 𝐁(Y₁) ∪ 𝐁_k″ CRE's localized set (2.14) (l.39), and a determining set's layer Γ₀ = Ω₁^c carries M⁰, the bonds themselves (CRE (2.2), (2.11), p.255 l.12–16, p.256 l.36–41). So for 𝐁(Y₁) every bond off Ω₁(Y₁) is pinned, Y₁^c included, and dist(Ω₁(Y₁), Y₁^c) ≥ ξM₁ (p.256 l.61). The free bonds and the plaquettes containing them lie in Y₁, and P(𝐔) depends on 𝐔 on Y₁ only. Each member takes the P of its own localization domain. An old insertion takes that of its R-step component Y₁ and step j (l.39, l.135). A member that is a function of 𝐔 itself, with domain X, takes P_Ω := U(𝐁_k″(Ω), M·(·)), 𝐁_k″(Ω) := 𝐁(Ω) ∪ 𝐁_k″, for a neighbourhood Ω of X chosen as CRE p.257 l.8–11 and l.22–26 require (∂Ω in the layers, at distance ≥ 2M₁ from their boundaries). This is how Bałaban's T-step evaluates the old terms, at local minimisers 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j(□₀, exp iB̃_□M·(U_{k+1}))) (RG1 (3.23), p.274), with □₀ = □̃⁵ and X ⊂ □̃² (RG1 p.273 l.37–45). So every Y∘P is local, and at s = 0 it factorises over the decoupled components as a member localized in its Ω or Y₁, as Bałaban's terms localized in □₀ do. The identity on minimisers. A localized minimiser map U(S, ·), pinned off Ω₁ as above, is the identity on the minimisers of every determining set S′ that equals S or is coarser than S on S's support. For such a minimiser U, every W admissible for S with U's data equals U off Ω₁. W has U's S′-averages, since the averages are iterated (M_S = M^j on Γ_j, CRE (2.11), with M^j the j-fold average) and S′'s blocks meeting the support are there unions of aligned S-blocks, down to the pinned bonds (the layers are unions of L^nξM₁-cubes, CRE p.256 l.59–61). So W is admissible for S′, A(W) ≥ A(U), and VAR Theorem 1's uniqueness gives U(S, M_S(U)) = U up to a gauge transformation. This is the nesting argument of *At s = 1*, and Bałaban prints the instances RG1 p.273 l.45 and LFR II (1.61). It covers the 𝐁_k-minimisers, the 𝐁_k″-minimisers (𝐁_k″ is finer than 𝐁_k on Z, LFR II (1.33)), and the points of 𝓜 for a domain Y₁′ with Ω ⊂ Y₁′⁰, where 𝐁_k″(Y₁′) = 𝐁_k″ (LFR II p.373 l.26–27). The identity principle of *The scope* extends it to their complexifications. So the values at the real minimisers, where the effective density is evaluated at s = 1, are unchanged. At s ≠ 1, P_Ω of the point differs on Ω from the s = 1 point by at most 2K sup|𝐇|, by VAR Proposition 9 for 𝐁_k″(Ω). CRE takes VAR's theory for every determining set (p.256 l.48–53) and applies it to the localized minimisers U_{p,X} (p.261 l.10, (2.35)), and K is derived for 𝐁_k″(Ω) as for 𝐁_k″(Y₁), whose strip *S_L is finite* covers (l.99). The fine bonds of the pinned layer differ by at most 2 sup|𝐇| as well. sup_{𝒟^{λ,M}_k}|Y∘P| is the sup of |Y| at the points P(𝐔) of 𝓜^c whose representative V̂ has (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) ∈ 𝒟^λ_k, for each member's own P, which is what the bullets of (iii) bound ((R-eval-cr)). These bounds hold up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**) and up to (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative (**Open (R-eval-curv)**). At step k+1 the members are Y′∘P_{k+1}, with P_{k+1} the step-(k+1) member's own localized projection, local as above. So the T-step needs Y′ only at 𝐕 := P_{k+1}(𝐔) ∈ 𝓜^c_{k+1} for that member's domain Y′, which is taken to contain each constituent's own domain (its Ω or Y₁) in Y′⁰. Both parts of this transfer are derived below, part (a) up to **Open (R-eval-curv)** at the re-projected points, as at s ≠ 1, and part (b) up to **Open (R-eval-curv)** for the covariant members and the outer fields. (a) At a localized background (derived up to **Open (R-eval-curv)**). The real points of 𝓜_{k+1}(Y′) are localized minimisers U(𝐁_{k+1}″(Y′), M·(·)), pinned off Ω₁(Y′), and RG2's identity (map l.108 (ii)) holds at the global minimisers only, which are not a uniqueness set in 𝓜_{k+1}(Y′). So at a localized background 𝐕 the s = 1 point e^{iη𝐇_𝐕(1,tB′)}𝐕 is not shown to be a step-k minimiser, and the transfer does not use it as one. Bałaban's T-step does not either: it deforms the localized background U_{k+1}(□₀, M·(𝐔)) by 𝐇_k and re-projects with U_j(□₀, M·(·)) (RG1 (3.24)–(3.25), p.275). The comparison of *At s ≠ 1* (l.137) is made instead against 𝐕 itself, at every s, relative to the real base U_{k+1}. Each member's P fixes 𝐕. The step-(k+1) set 𝐁_{k+1}″ is Bałaban's R-operation set at step k+1 (LFR I (1.12)–(1.13), p.179 l.32–45, at k+1). That R-operation starts from 𝐁^T_{k+1}, the T-step's coarsening on Ω_{k+1} of the post-R set 𝐁_k (CRE (2.2)) ((1.14), l.59–62). Its preliminary integrations replace parts of Γ_j by their versions at the scale j + 1 ((1.12)–(1.16); l.75), so 𝐁_{k+1}″ is 𝐁^T_{k+1} or coarser, and it equals 𝐁^T_{k+1} if that R-operation has no region Z ((1.12) with Z = ∅). 𝐁_k is coarser than 𝐁_k″ (LFR II (1.33), p.365). So every average of 𝐁_{k+1}″ is an iterated average of those of 𝐁^T_{k+1}, hence of those of 𝐁_k″, and of an old insertion's 𝐁_j″ (l.135). *The real base.* U_{k+1}, the real base of 𝐕, at a global and at a localized background, is a real point of 𝓜_{k+1}. Every real point of 𝓜_{k+1} is a 𝐁_{k+1}″-minimiser at its own averages, and 𝐁_{k+1}″ is 𝐁^T_{k+1} or coarser (above) and is every localized 𝐁_{k+1}″(Y′) or coarser. By the nesting of *The identity on minimisers* (above), every real point of 𝓜_{k+1}, U_{k+1} among them, is fixed by every P_{k+1}, and it is the 𝐁^T_{k+1}-minimiser at its own averages, a T-step background at its own data, whether or not the (k+1)-th R-operation has a region Z. Bałaban's post-R background at step k+1, with the post-R set 𝐁_{k+1} (LFR II (1.33)–(1.35), p.365, at k+1), is one of these points, since that set equals 𝐁_{k+1}″ off Z and is Z^{(k+1)} on Z, so it is coarser than 𝐁_{k+1}″. The nesting argument of *At s = 1* (l.133) then applies as for the points of 𝓜 for a domain Y₁′ with Ω ⊂ Y₁′⁰ (above), with 𝐁_{k+1}″(Y′) in place of 𝐁_k″(Y₁′): each real 𝐕 is a minimiser of the member's problem with its own averages, and P(𝐕) = 𝐕 by VAR Theorem 1's uniqueness of the minimal orbit, on 𝓜^c_{k+1}(Y′) by the identity principle of *The scope*. M of the point differs from M(𝐕) by the data of 𝐇_𝐕(s,tB′), of sup at most sup|𝐇|, the pinned bonds included, since the layer Γ₀ carries the bonds themselves (CRE (2.11)). By Proposition 9 relative to the real base U_{k+1}, as in *The scope*, P of the point is the minimiser with the data d_𝐕 ⊕ δ, with d_𝐕 := M(𝐕) and δ this difference, and its Landau field relative to U_{k+1} is that at d_𝐕 plus 𝓗 := 𝓗_S(d_𝐕 ⊕ δ) − 𝓗_S(d_𝐕) = ∫₀¹κ(B_w)∂_wB_w dw, κ := δ𝓗_S/δB, with B_w the coordinates (1/i)log of d_𝐕 ⊕ wδ relative to the base's data, so |∂_wB_w| ≤ (1 + O(α_{1,k}))sup|δ| by BCH. (190) bounds κ uniformly on the ball, pointwise with its decay and with its gradient and D*D entries, as at l.164–165. So |𝓗| is at most (1 + O(α_{1,k}))K sup|𝐇| in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights, for phase, gradient, and current. This is below the response 2K sup|𝐇| of *At s ≠ 1*, and l.137–138's budget holds with the whole of 𝓗 counted as response. On every layer Kh_k^{(K)} ≤ 2Kh_k^{(K)} ≤ ¼(1−β)α_{1,k} (l.138), inside SW (b3)'s uniform margin ½(1−β)α_{1,k}, which is the room that 𝐕's own data leave, since data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} use at most ½(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n of the step-k phase room (l.54), and likewise for the gradient. 𝓗's current is at most the response's (1−β)²α_{0,k}/64 + O(Kε_k), which replaces both the response's and 𝐇_𝐕's shares of l.137's current budget. Part (b)'s pair for the point has the representative e^{iη(a″+𝓗^h)}U_{k+1} exactly, with 𝓗^h the same integral at d_{𝐕_X} ⊕ δ^h, at most (1 + O(α_{1,k}))e^{2a_c}K sup|𝐇| ≤ 2K sup|𝐇| for g small, within the same budget. Its real base is 𝐕's, U_{k+1}. *The base near the support.* At a global and at a localized background the real base of the T-step's points is U_{k+1}, and part (b)'s pair is built relative to it. l.159's bounds use the support of the R-step's indicators at the real base only through the bound on δ^{re} (l.160). U_{k+1}, the T-step point at B′ = 0, is not shown to lie on that support, but its data lie within O(ε_k) of a datum that does. Let B′ be the T-step's real integration variable and V the T-step data of U_{k+1}. U^{B′} := U_k(e^{iB′}V) is Bałaban's post-R background at the datum e^{iB′}V (l.132). It equals e^{iη𝐇(1,B′)}U_{k+1} up to a G-valued gauge transformation (RG1 (2.6), p.266; l.131 at the real point U_{k+1}). The T-step's integrand carries the post-R function at e^{iB′}V with its indicators as factors (RG1 (2.1), (2.9), p.265 l.30–39). So wherever the integrand is nonzero, e^{iB′}V lies on their support, and there sup|B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_k (l.138; map (6)(e)). At a real base U, δ^{re} is the difference, on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, between U's data and the averages of U_0^{(AL)} = U^{(L)}_{k,Z}(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}, V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c})), a function of U's data off Λ (l.160). Along the real segment from the data e^{iB′}V to V, the first term changes by at most O(K)sup|B′|: on Z, U's 𝐁_k″(Y₁)-data on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} are averages at scales j ≤ k, namely h on the part outside Ω″_{h+1}, where Γ″_h = (Ω″_h∖Ω″_{h+1})^{(h)}, and h+1, …, k inside it (LFR I (1.10)–(1.13), p.179 l.44), of the minimiser at the T-step's 𝐁_k-data, which are scale-k on Z (LFR II (1.33)), and VAR Proposition 9 and (190) bound their change. The critical extension changes by at most C_{rsp}M⁵sup|B′|, by LFR II (1.38)–(1.42) (p.366 l.30–p.367 l.24), in the axial gauge of (1.38): (1.41) gives |B′_Λ(B̃′)| ≤ 4d(100M)⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃²|B̃′| for a small data change B̃′ at a regular real base (table row 128's C_{rsp}). The averages of the minimiser U_{k,Z} change by at most K(1 + C_{rsp}M⁵)sup|B′|, by VAR Proposition 9 and (190). So at U_{k+1}, |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k per block, with a constant fixed before g, far below r_B for g small. l.159's bounds then hold at the real base U_{k+1}, with C_{mx} carrying this constant (l.45, l.160). The conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at P of the point need the margin of **Open (R-eval-curv)**, as at s ≠ 1. (b) The step-k normalisation (derived, up to **Open (R-eval-curv)** for the covariant members and the outer fields, with λ_w := (1/i)log w for a gauge function w, and U^w the left action, (U^v)^w = U^{wv} (RG1 (1.10); REG p.78 l.14)). The step-k Landau representative V̂ of a point is generated from that point's axial element and normalised by (1.29) on the step-k layers (REG p.81; l.102–103), and the normalisation "depends on an element U chosen to generate" the set (p.80 l.29–30). The step-(k+1) representative X := e^{iηa″}U_{k+1} of 𝐕 is generated from 𝐕 and normalised on the step-(k+1) layers, and 𝒟^λ_k is G-invariant only. So at the evaluated point 𝐏, an axial-gauge minimiser (VAR Proposition 9; l.156), V̂ need not be the point that SW (b3) computes. The step-(k+1) member therefore evaluates each step-k member at another pair of 𝐏's G^c-orbit, chosen from 𝐕 alone, and equal to the T-step's value at real parameters. The construction is made for each member's own P, with that P's determining set: its block points, its axial conditions (1.19), and its normalisation (1.29); the bound is read member by member, as l.129 defines it for each member's own P. The normalised axial element. For a configuration U₁ near U₀, real or G^c-valued, CMP98's gauge conditions (72) for U₁^u fix u from its values at the top block points ((77) and p.30 l.13), and every such u has \overline{R₀u}^j(x_j) = u(x_j)\overline{R_{0,x_j}U₁}^{(j)} ((84), p.30). So exactly one of them has REG's normalisation, u(y) = (\overline{R_{0,y}U₁}^{(k)})^{−1} ((86)–(87), p.31): it "is uniquely determined by all the conditions" (p.31 l.20), as an explicit analytic function of U₁. REG takes this over layer by layer, with (1.29) at the top points of each Λ_j, for its u₁, "determined uniquely in terms of U₁" (p.88 l.36), and (1.70)–(1.72) bound it at every point of Ω₀. Let v_{k+1} and v_k be this transformation for U₁ = X, with 𝐕's step-(k+1) set and with the member's step-k set, and 𝐕_X := X^{v_k}, axial for the member's set. 𝐕 = X^{v_{k+1}}, since 𝐕's own normalised transformation is such a u for U₁ = X. So 𝐕_X = 𝐕^c with c := v_kv_{k+1}^{−1}. By (1.70)–(1.71) at both steps, as l.106 bounds λ̂ by A_L, |λ_c| ≤ a_c at every point, with a_c := 33d times the sup over the layers of L^jη|a″| on Ω_j, which is O(α_{1,k}) on 𝒟^λ_{k+1}. At real 𝐕, X is real and v_k, v_{k+1}, and c are G-valued, whatever s, t, and B′ are. The pair. Let h := c at the block points of the member's set, ĥ the gauge transformation with these values that keeps the conditions (1.19), and 𝐔″ := 𝐏^ĥ. A transformation keeps (1.19) if and only if u(x_n) = R(Ū₀ⁿ(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}))^{−1}u(x_{n+1}) (p.78 l.14, p.79 l.6–8), so ĥ is fixed by h and entire in h, and since R(Ū₀ⁿ) conjugates by the real base's averages, |λ_ĥ| and |Im λ_ĥ| are at most a_c at every point. In fact ĥ = c. At every point the member's layer index j is at most 𝐕's (the member's set is finer, part (a)), and the chains x_0, …, x_j of the member's conditions (1.19) are the first levels of 𝐕's chains at that point, with the same averages relative to the same base U_{k+1} (REG p.79 l.4–9). So 𝐕, axial for its step-(k+1) set, is axial for the member's set too, and so is 𝐕_X = 𝐕^c. So c keeps (1.19), and it is the transformation with the block values h that keeps (1.19). Plain averages transform with the values at the block points (REG p.78 l.14), and the axial gauge picks one element in each orbit of the group (1.14) (p.79 l.15). So 𝐔″ is axial and critical with the data M(𝐏)^h, within VAR's radius since a_c → 0 with g, and P(𝐔″) = 𝐔″ by VAR Theorem 1's unique critical orbit (l.103) at real parameters, and on the complex parameters by the identity principle of *The scope*. Its representative. M(𝐏) = d_𝐕 ⊕ δ, with d_𝐕 := M(𝐕) and δ the data of the deformation (part (a)), so M(𝐏)^h = d_{𝐕_X} ⊕ δ^h, with d_{𝐕_X} = M(𝐕^c) = d_𝐕^h, since c = h at the block points, and δ^h(b) := Ad(h(b₋))δ(b), so |δ^h| ≤ e^{2a_c}|δ|. X is generated from 𝐕_X with REG's normalisation, so it is 𝐕_X's representative by the uniqueness of Theorem 4 (p.88 l.2–6), once X meets (1.37), (1.38), and (1.62) at step k. (1.38) is R_kD*a″ = 0 (the representatives, below). CRE (2.36) (p.261 l.19) at the real base pair (U_k, J_k), at which D(k)(iii) evaluates the factors (map l.316), would give B_J ≤ (1+β₀)α_{0,k}, since α_{0,n} ≤ (1+β₀)α_{0,k} for n ≤ k (g_n ≤ (1+β₀)g_k by CRE (2.6), and x(log x^{−2})^{q₀} is increasing for small x). Neither this bound nor that membership is among the real-U base-point conditions of map l.315. B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K, holds: *The real base current* (l.173, reviewed as packet JB) derives B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ with ε₁′ := max(2L²ε_0, 2(1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}) at D(k)(iii)'s real base points, by VAR Theorem 1 and its uniqueness clause for the problem with support Ω_1^∼, and B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ + O(KC₁^{RG2})ε_k at this part's real base U_{k+1}, with B_J there over the post-R term's Ω_1 = Ω_1 ∪ Z, from the post-R term's base-point clause (LFR II Theorem 1; D2.7 item 15.8, derived, not reviewed). A fixed factor L between a bond's and its endpoint's layer index at the layer boundaries is harmless, since B_J → 0 with L fixed. Both terms fit inside the current room of l.54 for g small. So 𝒟^{λ,M}_k's bound for the member applies at (𝐔″, R(ĥ)𝐉) once V̂″ meets 𝒟^λ_k's conditions, which is SW (b3)'s computation below. The T-step uses the complex parameters only through the analytic extension of its integrand, for the Cauchy bounds of the cluster expansion. RG1 introduces 𝐔 into 𝐇_k through U_{k+1}(□₀, M·(𝐔)), which "has approximately the same regularity properties as 𝐔, hence all necessary theorems are valid for operations with this configuration" (RG1 p.275 l.6–7), and VAR (190) is stated for G^c-valued V′ (Proposition 9, p.309). So e^{iη𝐇(1,tB′)}P_{k+1}(𝐔) is the step-k minimiser of the complex data it carries: at every real point 𝐕 of 𝓜_{k+1} and real B′ it is RG2's U_k(e^{iB′}V) = e^{iη𝐇}𝐕 (map l.108 (ii)), with V the T-step data of 𝐕, and both sides are analytic, so the identity extends from 𝓜_{k+1}, a totally real submanifold of maximal dimension of 𝓜^c_{k+1}, to 𝓜^c_{k+1} and complex t as in *The scope*. A minimiser of the coarser constraints is admissible for the finer ones with its own averages, and every configuration admissible for the finer ones is admissible for the coarser ones, since the coarser averages are iterated averages of the finer ones, so it is also a minimiser of the finer problem. K does not enter.         - *The gradient of the deformations (derived on 𝓜^c).* With L^kη = 1, let γ_n be the (−1)-norm of the gradient of the reduced stage-n configuration's complex part, at the reduced real stage. Per step:
          - (a)'s gradient is at most O(δ′_j)C_GS_Lε_{n+1}, by *The Schwarz bound* applied to (190)'s gradient entry. That entry carries (L^iη)^{−2} where the first carries (L^iη)^{−1}, and the (−1)-norm absorbs the extra power. (b)'s gradient is bounded in the same way, with (b)'s factor.
          - (c′)'s gradient. (190)'s entries bound the gradients of 𝓗 and of A_L, β's gradient is bilinear in these and their values, and ∇(Ad_GX − X) involves only |λ̂|, D₀λ̂, X, and ∇X. D₀λ̂ and |λ̂|_{(1)} are bounded in *The bound on G*.
          - The base change between consecutive stages is of order zero for the gradient, with coefficients ηℍ^{(n)} and ∇(ηℍ^{(n)}), bounded by (1.45). It adds O(B₃δ′_j)ε_{n+1}.
          - Hence γ_n ≤ γ_{n+1} + C_*S_L²δ′_jΣ_{m≥n}(ε_m + ε_{m+1}), with C_* depending on d, L, B₃, c_χ, and r_B. Over the N + 1 steps, γ_0 ≤ γ_Φ + O(N^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²ε_Φ. With ε_Φ ≤ 2λα_{1,k}, the deformations' part is below ⅛(1−β)α_{1,k} for g small, on every layer. No factor L^{k−i} appears, since (190)'s entries carry exactly the weights of the norms.
        - *The current of the deformations (derived on 𝓜^c).* The current is tracked covariantly, at the configurations themselves. Let C_n := J^h(𝕌̃^{(n)}) − J(U₀^{(n)}), the holomorphic current of the reduced stage-n configuration minus the current of the reduced real stage, and κ_n its (−2)-norm.
          - Covariance. J^h(V^G) = Ad_GJ^h(V), with Ad_G := Ad_{G(b_−)} at each bond b, which is the action R(u) of RG1 (1.10), (𝐔, 𝐉)^u = (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉) = (u_−𝐔u_+^{−1}, R(u_−)𝐉). So J^h(𝕌̃^{(n)}) − J^h(𝕌) = Ad_G ΔJ with ΔJ := J^h(𝕎_n) − J^h(𝕎), and at U, where G = 1, J(U₀^{(n)}) − J(U₀) = ΔJ^{re} := J^h(𝕎_n(U)) − J^h(U₀). Hence C_n − C_{n+1} = (Ad_G − 1)ΔJ + (ΔJ − ΔJ^{re}).
          - The expansion. For a field a, J^h(e^{iηa}U₀) = J(U₀) + D*_{U₀}D_{U₀}a + 𝒬_{U₀}(a). Here 𝒬 collects the rest: (D*_V − D*_{U₀})F_V, of order zero in the plaquette field F_V with coefficient O(|a|), and D*_{U₀} of the plaquette field's nonlinear part and its commutators with the real curvature. The nonlinear part is a local analytic function of a on each plaquette, vanishing to second order, and D* is a first-order difference operator. So D* of it is bounded by its first differences, which are bilinear in a and its first differences (with the real curvature as a coefficient). In the (−2)-norm this is (L^iη)²|a||∇a| ≤ εγ on layer i, uniformly in k. So 𝒬 and its differences are bounded by a, its first differences, and the real curvature ((2.34)), and no second difference of a enters.
          - The rotation term. Pointwise on layer i, |ΔJ| ≤ O(c_χS_L)δ′_j(L^iη)^{−3}, by (190)'s D*_UD_U entry, (L^iη)^{−3}, with |s̃| ≤ 3c_χδ′_j, and the expansion. With |λ̂| ≤ B_GL^iηε_{n+1} pointwise (l.120), |(Ad_G − 1)ΔJ| ≤ (e^{2|λ̂|} − 1)|ΔJ| ≤ O(c_χB_G)S_Lδ′_jε_{n+1}(L^iη)^{−2}, so |(Ad_G − 1)ΔJ|_{(−2)} ≤ O(c_χB_G)S_Lδ′_jε_{n+1}. Only |λ̂| enters.
          - The mixed difference. ΔJ − ΔJ^{re} is D*D of [F(d_{n+1}, s̃) − F(0, s^{re})]/η plus the mixed difference of 𝒬 at A_L, 𝓗(d_n), and their real values. The first is bounded by (190)'s D*D entry with *The Schwarz bound* for (a) and with (190) in s for (b), as for the phase. The second is first order, bounded by |A_L|, |∇A_L|, the values and gradients of 𝓗(d_n), and γ_{n+1}.
          - Hence κ_n ≤ κ_{n+1} + C_*S_L²δ′_jΣ_{m≥n}(ε_m + ε_{m+1} + γ_{m+1}). No Hessian of λ̂, of 𝓗, or of A_L enters, and neither does a base change of the current. Over the N + 1 steps, κ_0 ≤ κ_Φ + O(N^{2+β₀}δ′_k)S_L²(ε_Φ + γ_Φ), and the deformations' part is below ⅛(1−β)α_{0,k} for g small, on every layer.
          - The starting value. κ_Φ is the current deviation of Φ(𝐔). In the decomposition of *The starting values*, 𝓗(Δd)'s current is 𝒟^λ_k's current condition at the representative V̂, T₁'s and T₂'s are bounded by their D*D there, and the 𝒬 terms are at most O(sup_nα_{0,n} + ε_Φ + γ_Φ)(ε_Φ + γ_Φ).
        - *The 𝐉 coordinate (derived).* At real data the old terms are evaluated at a configuration together with its current: RG1 substitutes (U_n(M·(𝐔)), J_n(M·(𝐔))) for (𝐔, 𝐉) (p.262 l.61–p.263 l.2, under (1.16)), with J = D*ξ^{−2}π Im ∂U and the real-data identity (1.8)–(1.9). The analytic continuation of this current is the holomorphic current J^h(V) := D*_Vξ^{−2}π(∂V − ∂V^{−1})/(2i) of the evaluation configuration. So the 𝐉 coordinate at the evaluation point is J^h of the representative. It follows the representative by RG1 (1.10), (𝐔, 𝐉)^u = (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉), and by the E-terms' G^c-invariance ((1.19)). The old terms also take the derived configurations' currents J_n(M·(𝐔)), which are CRE (2.37)'s 𝐉_{p,X}(M·𝐔). These are quoted at the u-free representative (*The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points*), given (1.65)'s application at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))) (**Open (R-eval-curv)**, l.156 and l.158). The old terms' own space U^c_j is RG I's, a union of orbits of the G^c-valued gauge transformations (RG1 p.262 l.13; CRE p.259 l.40–45; table §11), and the old terms are G^c-invariant (RG1 (1.19)). So the passage from the u-free to the reduced representative needs no transfer for the old terms: they take the same values there. In that passage (2.34)–(2.37) at 𝕌̃, as conditions of the class's 𝒟_k, are part of **Open (R-eval-curv)** (l.158). *The current of the deformations* bounds a different object, the evaluation configuration's own current J^h, which is (2.36)'s. The data's own 𝐉 enters the new terms: "The variable 𝐉 replaces the function J_{k+1} as usual" (RG1 p.275 l.4), and LFR II introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) into the other expressions "in the usual way" (p.375 l.11–12).
          - The data's 𝐉 is not transported. 𝒟^{λ,M}_k is a set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉), and P acts on 𝐔 alone (*The M-factored domain*). The members are evaluated at (P(𝐔), 𝐉), whose Landau form is (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) by RG1 (1.10), with u_L the G^c gauge transformation from P(𝐔) to V̂. So R(u_L)𝐉, the pair's 𝐉 in the Landau frame, obeys 𝒟^λ_k's current condition and, since 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k (l.53), 𝒟_k's (2.36) about 0, by the definition of 𝒟^{λ,M}_k. The new terms take this 𝐉 where J_k appeared, with no transport beyond the gauge transformation of the pair. For CRE's B-terms, which are G-covariant only, the bounds at (P(𝐔), 𝐉) follow from the membership of the G^c transform (V̂, R(u_L)𝐉) only through the transfer of **Open (R-eval-curv)**, as at the reduced representative (l.6).
          - At the T-step's points the new 𝐉 lands by the transfer bullet (l.54: the 𝐇-part takes ¼(1−β) of the room, the rest ¾(1−β)) at s = 1, and by *At s ≠ 1* with T_Y's α₀-entry otherwise.
