The landing of R(u_L)𝐉 uses the step-k frame u_L, so at the next step's points it is taken in part (b)'s frame t, which differs from the data's step-(k+1) frame by a factor e^{2|Im λ_f|} = 1 + O(α_{1,k}) and by the term (R(f) − 1)J_k, at most 3C_fdλλ_RB_J/(1−β) times the room, which is o(1) once B_J → 0 as g → 0 (derived at l.173 and reviewed as packet JB; (iii), *The bounds*, part (b)), and at s ≠ 1, and at s = 1 at a localized background (part (a)), it also uses (2.36) at P of the point, which is **Open (R-eval-curv)** (l.137).
        - *The curvature conditions and (2.36) at the evaluation points (quoted).* 𝒟_k, D(k)(iii)'s domain, is CRE's full complex part of Ũ_k^c about the base point U_k: (2.34)–(2.35), (2.36)–(2.37), and (2.39) (SW closure (b1)), and 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k (l.53).
          - LFR II (1.65) (p.375) maps (𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ Ũ_k^c(Y₄) to U_k^0(𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ Ũ_k″^c(Y₁), the space (1.64)–(1.69) of LFR I for n = N. (1.65) is stated for Bałaban's extension, which depends on 𝐉 (*The scope*). The note's U_k^0(M(𝐔)) equals Bałaban's U_k^0 at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))), by the identity principle on the graph over 𝓜^c (*The scope*). This point is on the graph, and V̂ is not. P(𝐔) is the axial-gauge minimiser (VAR Proposition 9), and its Landau form V̂ is a G^c gauge transform of it, complex and ≠ 1 at the block points (l.106). (V̂, J^h(V̂)) lies in 𝒟^λ_k ⊂ 𝒟_k by the definition of 𝒟^{λ,M}_k. Ũ_k^c is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (LFR I p.191 l.32–33; CRE p.261 l.42–43), so (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))) ∈ Ũ_k^c(Y₄) needs the margin of **Open (R-eval-curv)**, the same transfer through a G^c gauge transformation as at the reduced representative (l.6), and also CRE (2.39) at the axial form P(𝐔) (CRE p.261 l.35–40), which a G^c gauge transformation changes additively, by D(Im λ̂) at first order; this too is part of **Open (R-eval-curv)** (l.6). Given it, (1.65) applies there, read as an inclusion of spaces as on l.17.
          - Along the chain, LFR I p.191 l.39–44: if 𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n+1)c}, then exp iηℍ_k^{(n)}(g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j))𝕌 ∈ Ũ^{(n)c}, "and the corresponding statement for 𝕁", with the proof "almost identical to the proof of a similar statement for the configurations U_k^{(n)}, given in (1.44)–(1.52)". For n = 0 the space coincides with Ũ_k^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.191 l.33–34). (1.68) prints c = 3 on Ω_k (p.191 l.13–14), which at n = 0 gives 3α_{0,k} there, so taking CRE's radii at n = 0 is the reading *R-coincides* (l.17). LFR I (1.68) lists |∂𝕌 − 1|, |∂U_{p,X}(M·𝕌) − 1|, |𝕁|, and |𝕁_{p,X}(M·𝕌)|, which are (2.34)–(2.37).
          - So, given (1.65)'s application at (P(𝐔), J^h(P(𝐔))) (**Open (R-eval-curv)**, above, including (2.39) at P(𝐔)), the u-free representatives of the evaluation points obey the complex curvature conditions (2.34), (2.35), and (2.37), and (2.36) about 0, with Bałaban's radii under *R-coincides*. These are Bałaban's presented-as-true claims, with the proofs omitted as "almost identical", and are accepted. The reduced representative 𝕌̃, at which the class's bounds are taken, is a G^c transform of the u-free one, and the spaces are G-invariant only, with no margin stated. So these conditions at 𝕌̃ are **Open (R-eval-curv)** (top of the note).
        - *The starting values γ_Φ and κ_Φ (derived at the R-step's evaluation points).* The bounds at 𝐔 are those at P(𝐔) (*The scope*), so take 𝐔 ∈ 𝓜^c with 𝐔 = P(𝐔), whose representative V̂ lies in 𝒟^λ_k. The bounds below are derived where the real base is one of Bałaban's post-R backgrounds on the support of the R-step's indicators, and where the real base is the T-step's U_{k+1}, at a global and at a localized background, whose data lie within O(ε_k) of that support, with the larger constant of δ^{re} given there ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a), *The base near the support*). At s ≠ 1, and at s = 1 at a localized background, V̂'s membership at the T-step's points is subject to (2.34)–(2.37) at P of the point (**Open (R-eval-curv)**, l.137). REG's step-k normalisation is arranged by the choice of pair (part (b), derived; for the covariant members and the outer fields up to **Open (R-eval-curv)**). At a general real point of 𝓜 the data on Λ are free, and |δ^{re}| = O(ε_k) is not claimed there. On 𝓜^c, Φ(𝐔) = U_k^0(M(𝐔)), so the chain starts at a VAR minimiser, and no transport by E enters.
          - The two minimisers. 𝐔 = U^{post}(M(𝐔)) and U_k^0(M(𝐔)) are minimisers for 𝐁_k″(Y₁) (*The M-factored domain*, nesting) whose data agree off Λ (*The response is sourced on Λ*). On Λ^{old} = Λ∖Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, U_k^0 takes T′_k(X)'s real integration variables V_h (LFR II p.370 l.4, p.378 l.31), and their difference from U's averages is not bounded (**Open (R-eval-old)** at the top); the bounds below concern Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}. There U_k^0 takes the averages of U_0^{(AL)} = U^{(L)}_{k,Z}(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c}, V_Λ(V_k⌈_{Z∩Λ^c})) (LFR II p.366 l.23), and the post-R configuration is the global minimiser U_k = U_{𝐁_k}(V) ((1.35)) for the data V_k′V_Λ of (1.34). U_{k,Z}(V_k′V_Λ) is LFR I (1.74)'s minimiser, defined in each component of Z separately (LFR I p.192 l.52–55). So the real data differ on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} in two ways. The first is through V_k′ = exp ig_kB′, and the R-step's integrand restricts |g_kB′| < M₀ε_k (LFR II (1.1), p.356, and the display below it, l.37). The second is the replacement of U_{k,Z} by U_k, which Bałaban makes "in the same way as above, i.e., with the configuration U_{k,Z} replaced by U_k, and with some new small terms" (LFR II p.365 l.35–36). "Above" is (1.36)'s representation, an 𝐇-function whose argument "has a support in the boundary layer of the width 2M₁ at the boundary ∂Z^{∼−3}, and it is bounded by 44d²ε_k" (p.365 l.29–30). Bałaban prints neither the representation nor that bound for this replacement. Reading it as the same representation with the same bound (Bałaban's "small terms", accepted) is the reading *R-small-Z* (l.17), and under it the 𝐇-function's linear bound (VAR Proposition 9 and (190), with factor K, as in the next sub-bullet) puts this part at most O(Kd²ε_k) per block. Let δ^{re} be the real data difference there at the real base U, so |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd²)ε_k per block of 𝐁_k″(Y₁), and at the T-step's real base U_{k+1}, whose data lie within O(ε_k) of the support, |δ^{re}| ≤ O(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a), *The base near the support*), far below r_B for g small.
          - The real base. Take U_k^0(M(U)) as its Landau representative relative to U, U_k^0(M(U)) = e^{iηR₀}U with R₀ := 𝓗(δ^{re}). This holds where the real data differ only by δ^{re}, that is, apart from the real data difference on Λ^{old}, which is **Open (R-eval-old)**. The words are gauge invariant (*The gauge representatives*), so this choice is free, and (1.51)'s gauge u_k″ plays no part. By (190), (L^iη)|R₀| ≤ O(S_L)|δ^{re}| on layer i, which is all that passing from ∇_U to ∇_{U_k^0(M(U))} uses: a base change of order zero for the gradient with coefficients O(S_L(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k), as in *The gradient of the deformations*. The current is compared at the configurations themselves (*The current of the deformations*), with no base change.
          - The complex part. Relative to its real base, Φ(𝐔)'s complex part is 𝓗(Δd^0 ⊕ δ^{re}) − 𝓗(δ^{re}), where Δd^0 is the complex data: Δd off Λ, 0 on Λ^{old}, where U_k^0 takes the real V_h, and the critical extension's complex part on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}. Write it as 𝓗(Δd) + T₁ + T₂, plus the open part T₂^{old}.
            - 𝓗(Δd) is 𝐔's own complex part. Only its complex part enters, so its phase, gradient, and current are 𝒟^λ_k's conditions at the representative V̂, which hold by the definition of 𝒟^{λ,M}_k. Its current is the configuration's (*The 𝐉 coordinate*).
            - T₁ is the response to Δd^0 − Δd, which is supported on Λ: −Δd on Λ^{old}, at most 𝐔's own room per block (packet A's "+1", l.43), and on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} at most O(1)L^j(C_{rsp}M⁵ + 1)s_Z per j-block, with C_{rsp} = O(1)4d·100⁵γ₀^{−1}B₃², by (1.41) and (190) (*The response is sourced on Λ*, *The fine field at the scale k* and *The averages*, l.42–43). Its phase is *The response is sourced on Λ*'s. By (190)'s gradient and D*D entries, uniform on the ball, its gradient and D*D are at most C_Λ′M⁵e^{−δ₀dist(x,Λ)/16}s_Z in the layer weights, with C_Λ′ = O(K)C_Λ. K is bounded uniformly in M once M meets *S_L is finite*'s condition, since a larger M only increases the distances.
            - T₂ := ∫₀¹[κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)]Δd dt, with κ := δ𝓗/δB. The map w ↦ κ(tΔd ⊕ wδ^{re}) is analytic for |w| ≤ r_B/(2|δ^{re}|), and (190) bounds it there, pointwise in (x, y′) with its decay. By the Cauchy estimate in w, |κ(tΔd ⊕ δ^{re}) − κ(tΔd)| ≤ (4|δ^{re}|/r_B) times (190)'s bound. The same holds for the gradient and D*D entries. So T₂'s phase and gradient are at most O(K²(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k/r_B) times 𝐔's room on every layer, and its D*D at most O(λ_RK²(B₃M₀ + Kd² + KC_{rsp}M⁵C₁^{RG2})ε_k/r_B) times the current room, since Δd is bounded through the phase room and α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k}. This tends to 0 with g.
          - Landing. On a layer n ≤ k₀, λ^{(k)}_n = λ, since k − n ≥ N₀ and c′^{N₀} ≥ λ for g small (N₀ → ∞ as g → 0, *Where it lands*). There T₁'s gradient is at most ⅛(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n times the gradient room once λ ≥ 8C_Λ′M⁵/(1−β). Its D*D is at most ⅛(1−β)λ^{(k)}_n times the current room once λ ≥ 8C_Λ′M⁵(C₁/C₀)/(1−β), with C₁/C₀ = λ_R fixed after L and M (*The constants*). l.72's λ meets both. On k₀ < n ≤ k, crossing n − k₀ layers of the Ω_m costs at least (n − k₀)M/M₁ in the scaled distance (*S_L is finite*), so T₁ is at most C_Λ′M⁵e^{−δ₀M/(16M₁)} times the gradient room and C_Λ′λ_RM⁵e^{−δ₀M/(16M₁)} times the current room. Both are ≤ ⅛(1−β) for M large given d, L, and M₁, since λ_R = K_RLM + 1 is linear in M (table row 53). T₂ on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} and the base change tend to 0 with g, and are below (1−β)/32 of the room for g small; T₂^{old} is open (**Open (R-eval-old)**). 𝐔's own part at the T-step's points carries (b3)'s margin (*The R-step lands in the family*, l.72–75, with P of the point by *The M-factored domain*, and at s ≠ 1, and at s = 1 at a localized background (*The bounds*, part (a)), up to **Open (R-eval-curv)** at P of the point, l.137). The gradient budget is that of the phase (l.75), with l.137's response at s ≠ 1 inside SW (b3)'s margin (l.138). On the older layers this uses λ^{(k)}_n ≥ c′ ≥ 2, which L ≥ 12d + 6 (table row 12) gives with β ≤ 1/4 and β₀ = 1/7. The current budget, in units of λ^{(k)}_n times the current room's unit, which the room f_nλ^{(k)}_n exceeds by f_n ≥ 1−β, runs as follows. The data's current transfers with ratio at most ½(1−β)(1+β₀) = (4/7)(1−β) at β₀ = 1/7 (l.54). 𝐇_𝐕's current is at most (1−β)/64 (l.14, with β ≤ 1/4), the quadratic terms are at most (1−β)/32 for g small (l.136), and at s ≠ 1 the response of l.137 adds at most (1−β)/64 + o(1). T₁ is at most ⅛(1−β), T₂ and the base change at most (1−β)/32, and the deformations at most ⅛(1−β) for g small (*The current of the deformations*). The total is at most (4/7 + 1/64 + 1/32 + 1/64 + 1/8 + 1/32 + 1/8)(1−β) + o(1) < 0.92(1−β), inside the room.
    - (iv) D2.7 15.6(c), (d), and (f)'s extension domain of radius r^{ext}_k (derived, up to the opens marked at its end). Its points are the post-R data V_k within 2r^{ext}_k per scale-k bond, in the gauge-fixed deviation, of a real datum V_k⁰ on the support of the R-step's indicators: the 2r^{ext}_k-neighbourhood of the real support. The insertions are evaluated at their transport form as above. r^{ext}_k := min(r^{an}_k, c_{ext}α_{0,k}), with c_{ext} := (1+β₀)/(2LK), K the factor of VAR Proposition 9 and (190) (l.8), and r^{an}_k := g_kC₁p₁(g_k), the radius of CRE (3.43), |A| < C₁p₁(g_k), for the unscaled field g_kA (a scaling not stated at (3.43), as in D2.7 15.7). CRE does not define p₁(·), which occurs in CRE only in (3.43). It is either LFR I's p₁ < p₀ (p.183 l.2) or a slip for q₁ (READINGS R43; TRANSCRIPTION_FINDINGS, LFR II p.368). Under either reading r^{ext}_k ≤ c_{ext}α_{0,k} and r^{ext}_k is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}, which is all that (iv) and D2.7 15.7 use. The post-R top-layer radii are α_{0,k} = g_kC₀(log g_k^{−2})^{q₀} and α_{1,k} = λ_Rα_{0,k} per scale-k bond (CRE (2.28), table row 53). The Cauchy step of 15.6(d) at a real datum V_k⁰ on the support of the R-step's indicators, with deviation ε₀, evaluates D_g at the data of deviation tε₀/|ε₀|, |t| < r^{ext}_k (when |ε₀| ≥ r^{ext}_k, (c)'s bound at V_k⁰ itself suffices, and V_k⁰ is in the domain). Each of these points is within 2r^{ext}_k of V_k⁰ per scale-k bond, so it is in the domain. At every point of the domain, V_k⁰ is taken as the real base, so the base is on the support, as l.159 and *The real base points* require. At such a point the configuration is U_k(V_k) = U_{𝐁_k}(V_k), the post-R data being at scale k on Z. It lies in 𝓜^c up to a gauge transformation by l.133–135: U^{post}(M(U_k(V))) = U_k(V) on Y₁ at real data, continued analytically. VAR Proposition 9 and (190) apply to the 𝐁_k-minimiser directly, which is VAR's own setting, with K taken as the largest of VAR's factors for 𝐁_k, 𝐁_k″(Y₁), the 𝐁_k″(Ω) of l.130, and U_{k,Z}'s 𝐁_k(Z) (LFR II p.367 l.11), and c_{ext} uses this K. So the configuration's whole deviation from U_k(V_k⁰), real and imaginary directions together, is at most 2K r^{ext}_k in phase, gradient, and D*D in 𝒟^λ_k's layer weights, up to a relative error O(r^{ext}_k/r_B). The deviation counts as the g^c-valued complex part, as in CRE (2.39). So per scale-k bond it is below ((1+β₀)/L)α_{0,k} ≤ ((1+β₀)/L)α_{1,k}, which is the bound used above at the T-step's points, for 𝒟^λ_k's phase and gradient conditions and for the current condition, whose room is α_{0,k}. The pair's 𝐉 is J^h of the point (*The 𝐉 coordinate*). So the bullets above apply to these points as at the T-step's points, and they obey 𝒟^λ_k's phase, gradient, and current conditions. The bullets carry their opens: T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), and (2.34)–(2.37) at the point's representative V̂, which (iv) puts into 𝒟_k from its phase, gradient, and current alone, the conditions at the reduced representative, and CRE (2.39) at P of the point (**Open (R-eval-curv)**, l.6). D2.7 15.6(d) and 15.7 take r^{ext}_k in their Cauchy factors.
    - *A_k (derived).* D2.7 15.6(c)'s e^{A_k} bounds the transports of the words on span(g); the slots' insertions are bounded separately, by e^{2ΣA} each.
      - For a set S of loop bonds, A_k(S) := c_π Σ_{b∈S} sup|Im ηA_b|, the sup over the evaluation points, where U_b = e^{iηA_b}u_b with u_b ∈ G, and c_π := sup_{|X|≤1}‖dπ(X)‖.
      - π is unitary on G, and the Hermitian part of iηdπ(Re A_b) − ηdπ(Im A_b) is −ηdπ(Im A_b). So ‖π(U_b)‖ ≤ e^{c_πη|Im A_b|}, the transports along S have norm at most e^{A_k(S)}, and the word is at most dim π·e^{A_k(S)} times its slots' bounds. This holds for every compact G and unitary π.
      - On 𝒟^λ_k the complex part is at most λα_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond, and a scale-k bond has L^k fine bonds. So A_k(S) ≤ c_πλα_{1,k}ℓ_k(S), with ℓ_k(S) the length of S in scale-k units. With α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} (CRE (2.28)), this is g_k times a polylog per unit length.
      - The evaluation points of 15.6(c) obey 𝒟^λ_k's phase condition: U^R at the T-step's points, the transport-form points ((i) and (iii)), and the extension-domain points of (iv), on which 15.6(d) uses B(g). This holds up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), and at the next step's points, at s ≠ 1 and at s = 1 at a localized background, up to **Open (R-eval-curv)** at P of the point ((iii), *The bounds*, part (a); l.137). The sup is taken at the representative at which 𝒟^λ_k's conditions hold, which the words' gauge invariance permits (*The gauge representatives*). At the transport-form points that representative is the reduced 𝕌̃, and the equality of the words there rests on l.111's identity theorem, which holds up to (2.34)–(2.37) at 𝕌̃ (l.113). At U^R the slots' CRE B-terms reach V̂'s bounds only through the transfer of l.153. Both are **Open (R-eval-curv)**. The phase bound itself uses no curvature condition, and A_k uses no other condition of 𝒟^λ_k.
    - *The real base points (derived, given D(k)(iii)'s pre-R clause, for the transfer to U_{k+1} the post-R clause of D2.7 item 15.8, and Bałaban's printed implications).* At real external data, at every point of the R-step's integration support, the preliminary variables included, U_k^{(0)} is a G-valued gauge transform of one of D(k)(iii)'s real base points. So it satisfies their conditions, the real-U part of CRE (2.34) and (2.38) (map l.313, l.315). *The real base current (derived and reviewed as packet JB; the transfer to U_{k+1} given D2.7 item 15.8).* At every such point B_J := sup(L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, with n the bond's layer index, satisfies B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′, with ε₁′ := max(2L²ε_0, 2(1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}), which tends to 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K. This is the bound that l.130's frame term (R(f) − 1)J_k needs. On the other bonds that term vanishes: every REG-normalised transformation is 1 at the layer-0 points, since REG (1.70) at a layer-0 point is an empty sum (p.88 l.38–44), so f = 1 there, and those bonds have both endpoints in Λ_0 = Ω₁^c. Let Ω := Ω_1^∼. The bonds off Ω_1 are first-step variables. Γ₀ = Ω₁^c carries the bonds themselves (CRE (2.2)), and they are the first step's V_0 = U⌈_{Ω₁^c} (CRE (1.12), p.248). An R-operation's region Z is a union of whole components of Λ_k^c (LFR I p.177 l.17–33), with the set Z^{(k)} on Z and unchanged off Z (LFR II (1.33), p.365 l.2–7; LFR I (1.12)). Since Ω₁^c ⊂ Λ_k^c, each component of Ω₁^c is removed whole or keeps its first-step boundary, and later large fields change only Ω_{j+1} ⊂ Ω_1. So every remaining part of ∂Ω_1 has its first-step margin: Ω_1 lies at distance at least 2LMR_1 from every large-field region (CRE p.248 l.8–9). The first-step bounds used below are statements about the step-1 integrand. There χ_0 localized in Ω_1^∼ is implied by the other step-1 functions and dropped (p.248 l.9–12), and p.246 l.21–22 bounds V on the support of χ_0(P_0^c). The variables V_0 and V⌈Γ_1 are not integrated at later steps, so later integrations only shrink the support in them, and every bound below that involves only V_0 and V⌈Γ_1 holds at every point of the support of the step-k integrand. D(k)(iii)'s base points are taken in that support (map l.313). The restricted problem. The free bonds of 𝐁_k lie in Ω_1, and every plaquette that contains one lies in Ω. The bonds off Ω are at distance at least LMR_1 from Ω_1, so a configuration admissible for the problem with support Ω (VAR's Ω_0 := Ω, with 𝐁_k's layering on Ω, as for the localized sets of CRE p.256 l.58–61) extends by U_k off Ω, with the same action change and the same conditions (2) off Ω. The sequence Ω ⊃ Ω_1 ⊃ … ⊃ Ω_k meets VAR's geometric conditions (p.277 l.11–17). Ω_1 is a union of LMR_1-cubes (CRE (1.10), p.248 l.8), and Ω adds one layer of them (CRE p.245 l.40–43), so both are unions of M_1-cubes, the partitions being compatible (p.245 l.38–40), and η^{−1}dist(Ω^c, Ω_1) ≥ LMR_1 > R_1^VM_1, VAR's (1) at j = 0, for g small: CRE's scale R_1 ≥ (log g_1^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), p.255 l.36–39) with g_1 ≤ (1+β₀)g_k, while VAR's R_1^V and M_1 are fixed (map l.174 distinguishes the two). For j ≥ 1 the conditions are those of 𝐁_k's own sequence {Ω_j}, to which VAR applies at the T-step, and the restriction leaves Ω_j unchanged for j ≥ 1. So U_k on Ω is a minimal configuration of that problem. Its data meet VAR (7) at ε₁′ (VAR p.278 l.34–45, mixed plaquettes included), as follows. Layer 0 (the bonds V_0 in Ω∖Ω_1): |V_0(∂p) − 1| < ε_0, since χ_0 (CRE p.246 l.4–6), localized in Ω_1^∼, equals 1 on the domain of integration (p.248 l.9–12). Layer 1: |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < 2L²ε_0 by CRE p.246 l.21–22, since (∂V)(p′) is the first step's V(∂p′) (V = Ū on Ω_1^{(1)} by CRE (1.11), and V̄ on the layer-0 bonds is the average of V_0), and p′ ⊂ Ω_1^∼ ⊂ (P_0^c)^{(1)}. Layer j ≥ 2: the four blocks of p′ lie in Ω_{j−1} ⊂ Ω_1, where the real-U part of (2.34) gives |∂U_k − 1| < α_{0,n}L^{−2n} ≤ (1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}L^{−2j} on layer n ≥ j − 1 (α_{0,n} ≤ (1+β₀)α_{0,k} for n ≤ k, as at l.130). V = Ū_k^j there by VAR (3), so CMP98 Proposition 2 in its local form (p.26 l.46–53, with its k := j and α₀ := (1+β₀)L²α_{0,k} ≤ c₂) gives |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < 2(1+β₀)L²α_{0,k}. For g small, ε₁′ ≤ ε₁, the fixed constant of l.94. So VAR Theorem 1 gives a minimal orbit in 𝔘(B₃ε₁′) (VAR (8), p.279). U_k's orbit lies in the space with ε₀ := B₃ε₁ (l.94), restricted to Ω. On U_k's own support, a small neighbourhood of Ω_1 including a layer of M_1-cubes (CRE p.255 l.21–23), the conditions (2) are those of l.94. On the rest of Ω, in layer 0, the plaquettes satisfy |∂U_k − 1| < ε_0 < B₃ε₁ by the layer-0 bound above, and at each bond the problem's current is a sum of at most 2(d−1) such plaquette terms, so it is at most C(d)ε_0η^{−1} < B₃ε₁η^{−1} for g small. So by the uniqueness clause (p.279 l.15, with B₃ε₁′ ≤ B₃ε₁ ≤ B₃a₁ ≤ a₀) U_k's orbit is that minimal orbit. The second line of VAR (2) (p.278) and J = Im ξ^{−2}D*∂U (VAR (28), p.282 l.34), with the current gauge covariant, give (L^nξ)³|J_k| < B₃ε₁′ at every bond with an endpoint in Ω_1: every plaquette containing such a bond lies in Ω, so there the problem's current is J_k's. At part (b)'s base. Part (b) uses J_k at its real base U_{k+1}, which is not shown to lie on the support (l.130 (a), *The base near the support*). There some U^{B′} = U_k(e^{iB′}V), with real B′ and sup|B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_k, lies on the support of the post-R function's indicators (l.130 (a)). U^{B′} is the post-R background U_{𝐁_k}(V) of LFR II (1.35) at the datum e^{iB′}V (l.132), not one of D(k)(iii)'s pre-R points, and part (b)'s layers at U_{k+1} are those of this post-R 𝐁_k (l.132). The post-R density is of the CRE Sect. 2 form with new regions (LFR II Theorem 1, p.355 l.17–21, accepted). Off Z the preliminary steps leave the regions unchanged (Ω_j″ = Ω_j ∩ Z^c there, and Γ_0″ = Ω_1^c; LFR I (1.12)–(1.13), p.179 l.32–47), and LFR II (1.33) (p.365 l.2–7) sets Γ_j′ = Γ_j″ ∩ Z^c for j < k and Γ_k′ = Γ_k″ ∪ Z^{(k)}. So the new regions are Ω_j′ = Ω_j ∪ Z for 1 ≤ j ≤ k, and Γ_0′ = Ω_1^c ∖ Z. The real base points of the post-R term satisfy the real-U part of CRE (2.34) and (2.38) on these regions: map l.174 derives that clause from the printed small-field functions of a term of the CRE Sect. 2 form, and D2.7 item 15.8 (`WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md`, derived, not reviewed) applies it to the post-R term, as l.175 records. So the derivation above runs at U^{B′} with the post-R term's objects in place of the pre-R ones: Ω_1′ for Ω_1, Ω′ := Ω_1′^∼ for Ω, the post-R term's own support (CRE p.255 l.21–23 for that term) for U_k's, and B_J taken over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1′. Its inputs carry over as follows. f = 1 at the layer-0 points of the post-R 𝐁_k by the same empty sum. Z is a union of whole components of Λ_k^c ⊇ Ω_1^c, so Ω_1′^c = Ω_1^c ∖ Z is a union of whole components of Ω_1^c, and no component of Ω_1′^c is adjacent to Z. Its bonds are first-step V_0, not integrated by R, every part of ∂Ω_1′ is a remaining part of ∂Ω_1 with the first-step margin, and layers 0 and 1 of Ω′ are bounded as above. VAR's geometric conditions hold for Ω′ ⊃ Ω_1′ ⊃ … ⊃ Ω_k′: at j = 0, Ω′∖Ω_1′ is the layer of LMR_1-cubes along those remaining parts of ∂Ω_1, so η^{−1}dist(Ω′^c, Ω_1′) ≥ LMR_1 > R_1^VM_1 as above; for j ≥ 1 they are those of the post-R 𝐁_k's own sequence, to which LFR II (1.35) applies VAR. The R-operation acts only at k > N (D2.7 item 13: the 𝐑^{(j)} terms vanish for j ≤ N), so the data on Z lie in layer k ≥ 2, and the four blocks of a layer-k plaquette on Z lie in Ω_{k−1}′ ⊇ Z. There, and on every older layer, VAR (7) at ε₁′ comes from the real-U part of (2.34) by CMP98 Proposition 2 as for layers j ≥ 2 above, with the coupling g_k on Z (p.365 l.19). Hence B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ at U^{B′}. U^{B′} equals e^{iη𝐇(1,B′)}U_{k+1} up to a G-valued gauge transformation (RG1 (2.6), p.266), and the current is gauge covariant. VAR Proposition 9 and (190) bound the current of the field 𝐇(1,B′) by O(K)sup|B′| in the weights (L^nξ)³, as l.130 (a) bounds 𝓗's, with K of l.167. So at U_{k+1}, B_J ≤ B₃ε₁′ + O(KC₁^{RG2})ε_k, which also tends to 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K. Bałaban's own instance is the first step: CRE p.248 l.37 applies VAR Theorem 1 ("By Theorem 1 [15] there exists exactly one critical point of (1.12)") to "U = V_0 on Ω_1^c, Ū = V on Ω_1^{(1)}", at the data level O(L²ε_0).
      - *The pre-R support.* Before R, D(k)(iii) is posed for real U_k in the support of the term's indicators (map l.313).
        - Its base-point clause there is the T-step's: map l.174 derives it from the printed small-field functions of a term of the CRE Sect. 2 form. The term that reaches the R-step has that form. Before the first R-step this comes from the T-steps alone. After an earlier R-step the post-R density is of the CRE Sect. 2 form with the new {Ω_j, Λ_j} (LFR II Theorem 1, p.355, accepted), and the base points enter the next T-step as at k ≤ N (D2.7 15.8, (e)). This bullet uses the pre-R clause, and l.173's transfer to U_{k+1} uses the post-R one from D2.7 item 15.8; the note derives neither.
        - The indicators of the term (1.2) of LFR I are χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}), the indicators among the seven groups (1.3)–(1.9) of the steps j = h, …, k−1 (p.178; (1.6) is a density), χ_h(Ω_h∖Ω^∼_{h+1}), the indicators of 𝐓_h(Z_h), and those of 𝕋_k(Z_k∩Z^c), which the R-step does not change (LFR I p.177 l.42). Let S be the set of original field variables on which every one of them equals 1.
      - *The operations keep the support.* Up to (1.75) Bałaban's transformations "preserve the k-th density" (LFR I p.193 l.36); after it they are equivalences, with equal integrals over V_k. They are of six kinds.
        - Decompositions of unity 1 = χ + (1 − χ), which multiply the integrand by an indicator.
        - Changes of variables, which are bijections at fixed external data:
          - (1.53), V′_j = V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1}, with the representation (1.44) of U_{k,Z}^{(n)};
          - (1.81), V″ = V′V_0 on Λ_0 (p.195), restated in the AL gauge as (1.92);
          - the change of variables (1.22) [III] with ℍ_Z^{(j)} on the components with 1 − χ′^{(j)} (p.188 l.35–37);
          - the scaling B′_j = g_jB_j (p.187 l.54);
          - the integration of the δ-functions of (1.72), which parametrises their constraint surface.
        - Gauge fixings: the Faddeev–Popov insertions (LFR I p.182 l.43, p.196 l.33), the reversed procedure on first-class components (LFR II p.378 l.17–19), the average over the 2^dd! graph families, and the compensating gauge transformations of V_h and V′ that accompany the AL gauge of U_0 (LFR I p.197 l.39–42). A forward insertion multiplies by a gauge-fixing factor, and the others move points along their gauge orbits.
        - The R′-replacement (1.100), (1.102): the integration of V_k over Λ, followed by a normalised fraction in dummy variables. The integrated variables V″ = V′V_0 and V_h stay in pointwise correspondence with the original V_k↾Λ, and the insertions do not depend on the dummy variables.
        - Bałaban's exact integrations of the preliminary fluctuation fields, with ℂ_k^{(n+1)} the logarithm of the integral in (1.60) ((1.62)) and its expansion (1.63), and the localisation expansions of his action terms (LFR II p.372 l.16–20, p.377). They act on his own terms only.
          - In the integrand that carries the insertions, the preliminary variables (A_j, B_j) stay integration variables, with χ^{(j)}, χ′^{(j)}, and the densities (1.6) as factors. U_k^{(0)} depends on them ((6C), *The insertions*), and the insertions are evaluated at U_k^{(0)} of the original variables, B included, with nothing subtracted from them (D2.7 closure l.6).
          - That integrand is Bałaban's times the insertions' average over (A_j, B_j), normalised by his integral of (1.60). No interpolated expansion acts on the insertions.
        - Removals of a factor that equals 1 on the support of the remaining ones (next bullet).
        - So every point of the final support corresponds to a point of S, up to a G-valued gauge transformation v of the variables. U(𝐁_k, V^v) is then a G-valued gauge transform of U(𝐁_k, V): the averages are gauge covariant (RG I p.265 l.14), the Wilson action is gauge invariant, and the minimiser is unique up to gauge (VAR Theorem 1 with map l.174's uniqueness identification; on Λ, LFR I Proposition 1, p.194, gives the critical orbit from which U_0's V_Λ is taken). The words are traces of ordered products of insertions at one configuration, so they are gauge invariant, and the gauge-fixing identities hold with them.
      - *Every removal is an implication.*
        - p.181 l.20–22 and l.32–33: χ_k^{(0)} gives (1.3) for j = h and □ ⊂ (Ω^∼_{h+1})^c∖Z_h, and χ_k^{(1)} gives (1.4) for j = h. On Z_h the function stays as χ_h(Z_h∩Ω_h) until (1.89).
        - (1.29) (p.183–187), for every n = j − h: on the support of χ_k^{(n+1)}, the completed (1.9) product over Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}, and χ′_j, the functions (1.3), (1.4), (1.5), (1.7), (1.8), and χ_k^{(n)} equal 1 (p.183 l.22–24; (1.31), (1.42)–(1.43), (1.48)–(1.52)). So the left-hand side's χ(Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}), ζ(Ω^c_{j+1}), and χ_k^{(n)} are removed, and the densities (1.6) extend to the whole region. For j + 1 = k, (1.4) is kept as χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}).
        - *χ′_j and χ′^{(j)} (derived).* Bałaban introduces χ′^{(j)} of (1.55) in the B_j-integral and excludes from Z the components with 1 − χ′^{(j)} (p.188 l.4–10), where χ′_j stays in the form (1.59). (1.60) carries χ′^{(j)} and not χ′_j. B_j is the scaled field (p.187 l.54, p.188 l.2), and Bałaban's large-field estimates take the threshold on the unit scale, |g_jB_j| < δ′_j (LFR II p.383: "|B(b)| ≥ g_j^{−1}δ′_j = A₁p₁(g_j)"; `TRANSCRIPTION_FINDINGS.md`, LFR II p.380–383). χ′_j is not a pre-R indicator, so S does not need it. What is needed is that the removals of (1.29) also hold on supp χ′^{(j)}.
          - On supp χ′^{(j)}, V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1} = exp i[g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j)] (p.188 l.53), and D̃ "has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms" (RG I p.267). So |V_j(V^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| ≤ c_Cδ′_j(1 + O(δ′_j)), with c_C the sup norm of C on (Ω^c_{j+1}∖Z″_{j+1})^{(j)*}, which depends on d and L. By (1.58), |V^{(j)}(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| ≤ O(1)e^{−R_j}ε_j ≤ δ′_j for g small. So |V_j(V_Z^{(j)})^{−1} − 1| ≤ c_χδ′_j, with c_χ := c_C + 2.
          - Bałaban's proof of (1.29) uses χ′_j only through the bound 2δ′_j on this quantity. It enters in (1.5), as O(1)δ′_j < ε_j (p.184 l.17–19); in ℍ's argument, bounded by 4δ′_j ((1.38), p.186 l.8, (1.45)); in (1.43), as 2δ′_j + ½δ_j < 2δ_j; and in (1.54).
          - With c_χδ′_j in its place each step holds for g small given d and L. Here δ′_j/δ_j = p₁(g_j)/p₀(g_j) → 0 ((1.38)) and δ′_j/ε_j = (A₁/A₀)p₁(g_j)/p₀(g_j) → 0, and p.186 l.40's factor becomes 4c_χB₃γA₁p₁(γ), small for γ small. So the functions (1.3)–(1.8) and χ_k^{(n)} equal 1 on supp χ′^{(j)} as well.
        - (1.89) (p.198–200): on the support of χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4})χ_{k,Λ}χ_{h,1/2}χ′, the functions χ_h(Ω_h∩Z_h) and χ_k″ equal 1. Bałaban sketches the proof ("we will sketch only main points", p.198 l.17), and (1.96), (1.98), and p.200 l.12–42 check each line of (1.24) at n = N. It assumes β ≤ 1/4 (p.198 l.16), and its proof needs (1.94)'s two conditions on N (p.198 l.54–57) and O(1)B₃B₅M⁵L₀^{−2(N₀−1)} ≤ 1/4 (p.200 l.30).
        - LFR II p.378 l.9–14: on first-class components χ_{k,Λ} and χ equal 1, on the support of the new small-field function χ_k. On second-class components χ_{k,Λ_i}χ_iδ_{G_i} are kept (l.20–22). So (1.89) applies on both classes.
        - On a component excluded by a large-field function the old functions stay, since such components "are not changed by the subsequent operations" (LFR I p.181 l.18).
        - The factors left only shrink the support. They are:
          - in 𝐓′_k(X) and (1.100): χ′(X), χ_{h,1/2}, σ(g_kB), δ_{rT}(B), χ(Λ_i), χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}), χ_k(X^{∼−6}), and 𝐓_h(Z_h∩X);
          - on second-class components: χ^c_k(Y_i^{∼−6}), χ_{k,Λ_i}, χ_i, and δ_{G_i};
          - for the preliminary variables: χ^{(j)}, χ′^{(j)}, and the densities (1.6).
        - Bałaban "combine[s]" χ_k(X_i^{∼−6}) with χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}) (LFR II p.378 l.15–16), read here as their product.
      - *The L₀-weighted layers.* On Z's layers i > k₀ the powers of L₀ weaken only the new functions χ_k^{(n)} ((1.24), (1.49), (1.51)), χ_k″, and the analyticity spaces (1.64)–(1.67), and no old function is replaced by them.
        - At step j, (1.3) and (1.4) follow from the last line of (1.24), on Ω_{j−1}∖Ω_{j+1}, which carries no power of L₀ ("with c = 1", p.183 l.38).
        - χ_k^{(n)} follows from χ_k^{(n+1)} by (1.48)–(1.52). (1.48) needs δM/M₁ ≥ 2 and γ or A₁/A₀ small (p.186 l.35–42), and (1.50)–(1.52) need β₀, β ≤ 1/2, L₀ < L/2, and α = 1/8 (p.187).
        - χ_k″ follows from (1.89), under β ≤ 1/4 and L₀² ≤ L/3 (p.191 l.35, p.198 l.16).
        - So the old functions on those layers equal 1 on the final support.
      - *Conclusion.* At real external data, at every point of the R-step's integration support, for every class component and with the preliminary variables, U_k^{(0)} is a G-valued gauge transform of U_k(V) for some V ∈ S. The term's indicators (1.5)–(1.9) are not gauge invariant (LFR I p.179 l.2–3), so this places U_k^{(0)} on a gauge orbit through S, not in S. CRE's spaces "are invariant with respect to G-valued gauge transformations" (p.261 l.43). So the real-U part of (2.34) and (2.38) hold there, by the pre-R clause. D2.7 item 5(a) and the real parts of the evaluation points in (i)–(iv) and *A_k* rest on this.
    - With the bullets of (iii) and the readings of l.17, (R-eval) holds: the deformations' current, the Landau representatives, the data's 𝐉, (2.36) about 0 at the evaluation points, and the curvature conditions are derived or quoted there, except for T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**), the conditions (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative and at P of the re-projected points ((iii), *At s ≠ 1*, and *The bounds*, part (a)), CRE (2.39) at the axial form P(𝐔), and the conjugation factors of the covariant members and of the outer fields at part (b)'s pairs (**Open (R-eval-curv)**). The bound B_J → 0 as g → 0, uniformly in k and K, for the real base current in part (b)'s frame term, B_J the sup of (L^nξ)³|J_k| over the bonds with an endpoint in Ω_1, is derived at l.173 and reviewed (packet JB).
  - *Why Bałaban's U_k^0 itself does not do.* Without the composition with P_{k+1} (*The bounds*), the next T-step's points have a″ a general element of 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}, and U_k^0, with (𝐔, 𝐉) taken in the usual way, re-minimises the data off Λ as well. Its response is then of the order of a″'s full room, which T_Y does not reduce.
    - The re-minimisation's constant is multi-scale, and every multi-scale constant in the prints depends on d and L: PRT II p.242–248, PBG p.406, p.416, p.423–424, and p.432, and VAR Theorem 1's B₃ (p.279). Only single-scale constants are "dependent on d only": PRT I's (p.28–29), and the decay rate of PRT II's single-scale H_j′ (p.246 l.28–29).
    - On Λ, (1.41) and (1.45) carry the factor M⁵ with M = L^m (RG I p.257 l.3). So on the uniform sub-domain, a response constant C with C(1+β₀)/L ≤ (1−β)/2 is not available.
    - The Schwarz lemma on (1.65)'s own spaces does not reach the older layers. On the layers k₀ < m ≤ k their phase radius is L₀^{2(k−m)}α_{1,k}L^{−k} per fine bond (LFR I (1.66), p.190 l.40–48, at n = N), so the bound is (4/π)((1+β₀)/L)L₀^{2(k−m)}α_{1,k}L^{−k}. With L₀² ≤ L/3 (p.191 l.35) it is at most (4/π)(1+β₀)/3·α_{1,k}L^{−k} < ½α_{1,k}L^{−k} on layer k − 1, inside 𝒟^{sub}_k, but on layer k − 2 it can reach (4/π)(1+β₀)(L/9)α_{1,k}L^{−k}. On a layer n ≤ k₀ the radius is α_{1,n}L^{−n} (p.190 l.24–29), so the bound there is (4/π)((1+β₀)/L)α_{1,n}L^{−n}, outside 𝒟^{sub}_k.
    - The transport form removes the re-minimisation, and the dilated sub-domains take the M⁵ where Bałaban's spaces take it: on the old layers. The composition with P keeps a″ on 𝓜^c_{k+1}, so at a global background a re-minimisation enters only at s ≠ 1 (at a localized one, also at s = 1: (iii), *The bounds*, part (a)), through P or the localized P_Ω, where it is of the order of sup|𝐇| and T_Y absorbs it (*T_Y reduced by 4K*).

**(R-eval-cr), derived, not reviewed: the cross insertions' weights at the R-steps' evaluation points.**
- The issue: the points Φ(𝐔) leave 𝒟^{sub}_k near the components ((R-eval)(i)), and the bound of an R-made cross insertion is a product over its slots and members ((6B), D2.7 15.6(f)), whose number is not bounded uniformly in k. So a growth factor per insertion at Φ(𝐔) does not bound it.
- How it holds: the cross clause (ii^cr) is stated with sup bounds on the dilated sub-domains, as the dev clause and D2.6's att bounds are (*The dilated sub-domains*). After a T-step into step k, and before the R-step at k, ‖Y_W‖ in w(W) is the sup over 𝒟^λ_k. After the R-step at k it is the sup over 𝒟^{ev}_k, the set of the points where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause: the s = 1 points of data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*, the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}), and the s ≠ 1 points, which P brings to them ((iii), *At s ≠ 1*, *T_Y reduced by 4K*). The class's domain is M-factored ((iii), *The M-factored domain*). The R-step's own evaluation points Φ(𝐔), 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^{ev}_k, lie in 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*) up to T₂^{old} (**Open (R-eval-old)**) and up to (2.34)–(2.37) at the reduced representative (**Open (R-eval-curv)**), where the pre-R clause holds. Every constituent of a product bound is then bounded at the evaluation points by its own clause, and no factor per constituent arises. The clause is derived at the three places where it is made or used.
  - *Production at the T-steps ((6)).* A cross(k+1) group passes SW §1(a1)'s test (ii): its components and the traversals of its hull lie in Ω_k. At step k+1 these are the layers Ω_{k+1}, where 𝒟^λ_{k+1} and 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} coincide (λ^{(k+1)}_{k+1} = 1), and Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, where the phase room is λ^{(k+1)}_k = min(λ, c′) ≤ c′ times 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}'s. ‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ S_𝒢, the product of the vertex sups and the transports (map l.1575).
    - The vertex sups on 𝒟^λ_{k+1}. A group with a parent that is not dev takes the first species of its parents, with cross(j) after wrap(j) (*The species of a new cross group*). So a cross(k+1) group's old members are dev, and its vertices are fluctuation activities and dev insertions. The fluctuation activities are Bałaban's, bounded on 𝒟_{k+1} ⊃ 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*). The dev insertions are bounded by the dev clause on the family (*The masses and weights*), at evaluation points that (b3)–(b4) keep in the family (*The dilated sub-domains*, the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}). Their complex-data size there is O(λρ_kpl_k), λ times their size on 𝒟^{sub} ((b2)'s size, *The dilated sub-domains*; map l.1498).
    - The transports: map l.1407's bound e^{c_πα_{1,k+1}n_{k+1}(𝒢)} ≤ e^{νσ_{k+1}(𝒢)/64} holds with c′α_{1,k+1} in place of α_{1,k+1} once c_πc′α_{1,k+1}M ≤ ν/64. Map l.1496's condition 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1, and map l.1498's a′-cost e^{(ν_r/16)σ_k(W)} per touched W, hold once c_πc′α_{1,k+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16 (map (12)(d)'s condition with c′α_{1,k+1}). Both hold for g small, since c′ depends on β, L, and β₀ only, fixed before g.
    - (S-w)'s right side is 2C_winε^{new} + C_win(ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k})²/ε_* (map l.1574), with ε^{new} ≤ C_Mρ_{k+1}pl + K_β(b_k/ᾱ_k)ε^{old} (map l.1577). The old sums enter linearly or quadratically and are at most λ times their values on 𝒟^{sub}. So for λ ≥ 1 the right side is at most λ² times its value on 𝒟^{sub}, and the KP condition ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ ε_*/2 (map l.1571) holds for g small given λ. So A^cr_{k+1} obeys (6)'s bound on 𝒟^λ_{k+1} with C_cr^λ := λ²C_cr in place of C_cr, a constant fixed after L and M.
    - The first step: 𝒟^λ_1 ⊆ 𝒟_1 = 𝒟^{sub}_1 (SW §3(b1), one layer), so (6)'s first-step bound holds on 𝒟^λ_1 as written.
  - *The carry ((6), the cross carry lemma).* The lemma is stated on 𝒟^λ_{n+1}. Its 5.1 step: a cross insertion carries no deviation, and its bound is a sup bound on 𝒟^λ_n, or on 𝒟^{ev}_n after an R-step at n. The step-(n+1) evaluation points of data in 𝒟^λ_{n+1} are the points of 𝒟^{ev}_n ⊆ 𝒟^λ_n: at s = 1 by the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1} (*The dilated sub-domains*), and at s ≠ 1 through P ((iii), *At s ≠ 1*, *T_Y reduced by 4K*). So its norm does not grow there. Its 5.4 step: the transports do not grow, under D2.6 §5.4's condition on the family, 4c_πMλα_{1,n+1} ≤ 1 (*The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*). 5.5's reserve budget spends (ν_r/16)σ on the a′-transports of a touched or merged fixed member (map l.1498). A carried cross(j) insertion can lie on a layer where the room is λ times (b1)'s, so this needs c_πλα_{1,n+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16, a smallness of g given λ, under which the budget is unchanged. The rest of the proof uses only the exponents. The constant c̄ is unchanged: c̄_* = max(2K_β, C″) (D2.6 closure 5.0) comes from the sum rooted at D, linear in D's weight, and the group's other vertices enter it only through the KP condition ε̄^{old} + ε̄^{ref,k} ≤ ε_*/2 (map l.1571), which holds on the family for g small given λ.
  - *The R-steps ((6B), (6C)).* For 𝐔 ∈ 𝒟^{ev}_k, Φ(𝐔) ∈ 𝒟^λ_k (*The R-step lands in the family*: the points of 𝓜^c where the next T-step evaluates the post-R clause; the s ≠ 1 points through P). So a carried cross insertion W has ‖W(Φ(𝐔))‖ ≤ ‖W‖, its pre-R sup on 𝒟^λ_k, with no growth factor. This is the ‖W‖ that 15.6(c)'s bound B(g) uses for each cross member (*15.6 for the cross groups of the R-gas*), and the factor w_{k−1}(W) of *Second case*. B(g)'s other factors are (R-eval)'s. So an R-made D_g^× obeys B(g) at every point of 𝒟^{ev}_k, which is its post-R clause, the one the next T-step's carry and production use. Neither uses a point of 𝓜^c ∩ 𝒟^λ_k outside 𝒟^{ev}_k.
  - The same holds for the cross insertions in the blocks of second-class components (*15.8*, the third class). Their factor is the sup over the evaluation points of 𝐓′_k(Y_i), which are points Φ(𝐔) of the same form ((1.71) and 𝐓′_k(X) for X "any component of the first, or of the second class", p.378 l.27). (R-eval)'s landing holds there: Y_i lies in the new Ω_k ((1.33); *Where it lands*), so s_Z ≤ α_{1,k}L^{−k} on its component of Z, and Λ_i = Λ ∩ Y_i is at least N₀ layers old before R. This is also the radius of Bałaban's own extension, U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}) (p.366 l.25–27), and of his first-order bound O(1)B₃²A₁²p₁²(g_k)|𝐁₀|(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k}) for both classes (p.379 l.27–31). So the factor is at most ‖W‖ ≤ w_{k−1}(W).
- Constants added: c_πc′α_{1,k+1}M ≤ ν/64, c_πc′α_{1,k+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16, and c_πλα_{1,n+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16 (smallness of g given λ), and C_cr^λ = λ²C_cr, fixed after L and M. λ is (*The R-step lands in the family*)'s dilation, not the rate factor λ_n of (6B).
- Where it enters: (R-eval)(i), *The masses and weights*; the carried cross total at the R-steps, and through it (8.2).

**(R-round), derived, not reviewed: the rounding of the carried members.**
- The issue: the members in a first-class component's slots are not bounded in number uniformly in k, since members made at a step j < k can have L^{k−j} disjoint intervals in one step-k bond (D2.6 closure 5.2 (T0) *Positions*). So no per-member constant can be paid by the margin of (1.89).
- How it holds: the rounding is counted per cube ((6B), *The units of the members*, *Big and small members*, *The rounding*).
  - A big member (σ(D) ≥ 40 when j = k, σ(D) ≥ 22 when j < k) has cubes, traversals, and diameter at most (21/20)σ(D) in π_k-units, with no constant, paid from its own weight. For j = k the diameter is that of T_D, which holds the π_k-cubes of the base Y′₀ over D's intervals.
  - A small member (the others: fewer than 40 cubes, fewer than 40M interval bonds, and diam_∞(T_D) < 42M) whose T_D meets X⁺ for a component X of its polymer, every arc-slot one included, has T_D inside X^{(44)}, the set X⁺ widened by 44 layers of π_k-cubes. All these small members of X are counted through #π_k(B_P) and the constant 4R_k + 92 of D_P, once per cube and per bond, whatever their number. Any other small member is joined to its polymer by a tree of length at least 2MR_k or a piece with at least 2R_k − 1 cubes outside its base, and half of that tree's or piece's term of D_P covers its count, with no constant ((6B), *Big and small members*). #π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ (105R_k)^d for R_k ≥ 88, and the margin pays it as it pays X⁺ (*The volume*).
  - (6B)'s *The payment*, *Joins*, *Join count*, and *First case*, and (6C)'s *The single loop's R-made groups*, its extent, and its re-run of D2.7 15.7 use only these bounds for the slot members 𝔅(g). The span members 𝒲_g of a single-loop group keep a constant 17 each, paid through their masses, not by the margin ((6C), *The members' weights*).

**(R-member), derived, not reviewed: the merge rule for shared cross members.**
- The issue: a carried cross insertion W can have intervals in the spans of several groups of one loop (D2.7 15.6's members 𝒲_g), on C₁ and on C₂ or on one loop, and in a slot of a cross group of (6B) as well. It is then a member of several groups, the groups' insertions are not separate factors of the word, and the (H2) set and the payment of the resulting insertion are not those of one group.
- Which sharings occur.
  - A single-loop insertion is a member of at most one group: its arc is one arc holding no slot bond (D2.7 15.6, the definition of 𝒲_g; map l.1314's single arc).
  - A cross insertion is a slot member of at most one polymer (*Second case*: the polymers of its slots overlap), hence of at most one group. A polymer with a cross slot member has no span (*The groups of 15.4*). The groups of (6B) with slots on both loops contract only their slots' bonds (*The arc set*), so they have no span members.
  - So the sharings are: W ∈ 𝒲_g ∩ 𝒲_{g′} for single-loop groups g ≠ g′, and W ∈ 𝒲_g ∩ 𝔅(h) for a single-loop group g and a cross group h.
- *The rule.* On a term of 15.6's expansion with marked set S, link g ∈ S and a carried cross insertion W when W ∈ 𝒲_g ∪ 𝔅(g). Each connected component of the links that contains a cross insertion, with its groups G ⊆ S and its cross insertions 𝒲^×(G), is one new insertion D_G: the contraction in loop order of the groups' insertions D_g (D2.7 15.6, 15.6(f)) and D_h^× ((6B)), with every interval of each W ∈ 𝒲^×(G) in its place. The intervals of the W that lie in no span of G and no slot of G stay open: they lie in the word at U^R, possibly in the span of an unmarked group, whose term c_{g′}Π_{P∈g′}F(P)·𝒲^{sf}_{span(g′)}(U^R; 𝒲_{g′}) carries them as ordered factors. A group of S linked to no cross insertion keeps its D_g or D_h^×. A cross insertion linked to no group of S is untouched. Each cross insertion lies in at most one D_G.
- *The identity.* D2.7 15.6's expansion is unchanged as an identity. Pointwise in the integration variables, both terms of a group's expansion are linear in each interval factor that lies in the group's span or slots: each member is an ordered factor on its interval in both terms (D2.7 15.6(f)), and a slot's insertion is a factor of the slot's word. So the identity holds for elementary tensors ⊗_iW_i of interval factors, and by linearity for W. On each term it is the product of the D_G, the other groups' insertions, and the word at U^R.
- *The bound.* B(g) bounds the integrand pointwise: 15.6(c)'s bound is a sup over the integration domain, and 15.6(f) and (6B) use no Cauchy step for groups with members. Contraction does not raise the projective norm on ⊗End(V_π) (map l.911), and at complex data the transports are in B(g)'s e^{A_k}. So ‖D_G‖ ≤ Π_{g∈G}B(g)·Π_W‖W‖, over the members of all groups of G, each cross insertion once.
- *Species and lineage.* D_G is a cross insertion: it carries no deviation bound and is never contracted. It is in the lineage of its first cross insertion W₁ in D2.0(3)'s order and then position, with the rate λ_k(D_G) ((6B), *The rates*) and θ(G) := Σ_{W∈𝒲^×(G)}θ(W), as in *Second case*: in M^cr, or, when W₁ is a two-loop att insertion, in W₁'s register of D2.6 5.3 ((6C), *The members' weights*). When a group of G is att(k, ·) by 15.6(e), D_G stays in W₁'s lineage, and that group's polymer part is paid in (6C), as for the cross groups (*15.6(e)'s scope rule*). A member att(·, Z) or lw keeps its factor in its species' total ((6C), *The members' weights*). A D_G with a wrap member is wrap(k), and its mass enters (ii)'s wrap sums through its first wrap member ((6C), *Wrap members*).
- *The arc set and the units.* ℐ(D_G) := ∪_{g∈G}ℐ(g) ∪ ∪_{W∈𝒲^×(G)}ℐ(W), with ℐ(g) := span(g) for a single-loop group and the slots' bonds for a cross group. X_{D_G} is the union of the groups' counted domains and the cross insertions' X_W, each cross insertion once. Put
  σ(D_G) := Σ_{g∈G}π(g) + Σ_{W∈𝒲^×(G)}σ(W) + Σ_{D}c_Dσ(D) + Σ_{W′}(½σ(W′) + 17),
  with π(g) := Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P), D over the single-loop slot members, c_D = ⅓ in a single-loop group (σ̃) and ½ in a cross group (σ_g), and W′ over the single-loop span members.
  - The pieces of *The units of the members* and *The count* are counted once each. A cross insertion contributes #_k(X_W) + n_k(W)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(W) + 17 ≤ 18.05σ(W), and diam_∞(T_W) ≤ 18.05Mσ(W), since σ(W) ≥ 1. A single-loop group's parts are at most 16 times its part of σ(D_G) (*Units* in (6C)), and a cross group's at most 6 times ((6B), *The count*). So D_G has the units of (6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor* with u = 19.
  - (H0) and (H1). (H0): D_G depends on the configuration through its groups' insertions and its cross insertions. Their (H0) puts their Cov domains in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_{D_G} ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(D_G)), since the groups' counted domains and the X_W lie in X_{D_G}, and the groups' arcs ℐ(g) and the ℐ(W) lie in ℐ(D_G). (H1): S_{D_G} is the union of the groups' sets S_{D_g}, S_g, and the T_W. It is closed-connected, since each T_W is closed-connected and meets the span or slot of each group linked to W, through ℐ(W).
  - (H2): E_{D_G} := X_{D_G} ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D_G) ∪ H₂(D_G)) ∪ ∪_{W′}E_{W′}, with W′ over the span members made at step k. For a group h ∈ G, the π_k-cubes meeting each dom P, P ∈ h, lie in h's counted domain ⊆ X_{D_G} or meet ℐ(h) ⊆ ℐ(D_G), and form a closed-connected set holding P's slot bonds and its slot members' X_W and intervals ((6A)(i), *R-made insertions*, for a cross group; (6B), *The count*, D_g's (H2), for a single-loop one, with ℐ(h) = span(h)). Each W ∈ 𝒲^×(G) has T_W closed-connected, containing ℐ(W), and inside X_W ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(W)) ⊆ E_{D_G} ((6B), *The units of the members*). Each E_{W′} is closed-connected by W′'s (H2), contains X_{W′}, and meets a cube meeting ℐ(W′) ⊆ ℐ(D_G). A span member made at a step j < k has T_{W′} = X_{W′} ⊇ ℐ(W′) ((6B), *The units of the members*). Each H_i(D_G) is an arc containing ℐ_i(D_G), with the members' intervals outside the spans. So E_{D_G} is closed-connected.
  - The count of E_{D_G}: X_{D_G} has ≤ 19σ(D_G) cubes. |H_i(D_G)| ≤ 4diam(S_{D_G}) ≤ 76Mσ(D_G) (*Filled hulls*), so the arc cubes number ≤ 1216σ(D_G) + 160 (*Arc cubes*). E_{W′} has ≤ 65σ(W′) + 160 ≤ 225σ(W′) ≤ 450(½σ(W′) + 17) cubes. In all ≤ 1685σ(D_G) + 160. So D_G's domain one step later has at most 16 + 20480/L + 215680σ(D_G)/L cubes, and the share needs (219024ν_f + 1672ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4. This holds for L ≥ L₀^× = 10⁶, since (219024·17/16 + 209)/10⁶ ≤ 0.233.
  - (T0)'s condition at u = 19 is (3230 + 70756/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8, a smallness of g under (R-m′). The transport condition holds since 1408·19 ≤ 4096L.
- *The weight and its payment.* D_G carries w_k(D_G) := e^{λ_k(D_G)ν_rσ(D_G)}e^{θ(G)}‖D_G‖, with D_G's shift of (6B), *The rates*.
  - The polymer parts π(g) are paid group by group: at the rate 2ν_r or (5/2)ν_r for a cross group ((6B), *The payment*; (6C)), and as in (6C), *The payment*, for a single-loop group. These payments are per polymer, and λ_k ≤ 1.
  - e^{|Ψ|}: the clusters incompatible with a group of G are rooted at that group's cubes and bonds, so |Ψ| ≤ Σ_{g∈G}|Ψ_g|. Its member part is over the slot members (*The payment*), and a cross insertion is a slot member of at most one group, so it pays its part once.
  - A single-loop slot member pays as in (6B), *The payment*, or (6C), *D's budget*.
  - A single-loop span member pays ½σ(W′) from its reserve, and its 17 through e^{17ν_r} in x_g or in its species' normalisation ((6C), *The members' weights*). The dev ones are summed in 15.7's factor e^{x_g} of their group, with x_g ≤ n_g, absorbed by a third of e^{−κ(size)} as in D2.7 15.7. So the dev members' masses are counted inside D_G, in M^cr, and not in (ii).
  - A cross insertion W ∈ 𝒲^×(G) enters with e^{λ_k(D_G)ν_rσ(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖W‖ ≤ w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k + ϑ_k)ν_rσ(W)}, since λ_k(D_G) ≤ λ_k(W) = λ_{k−1}(W) − ε_k − ϑ_k. Of the released (ε_k + ϑ_k)ν_rσ(W), ε_k/2 pays its member part of Ψ, ε_k/4 pays its join positions and its link positions (below), and ϑ_k/4 pays its parts' factor 1 + η(W) ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*). That leaves at least ε_k/4.
- *The link sum.* A group linked to W contains a π_k-cube of X_W (a slot, *Second case*) or holds a bond of ℐ(W) in its span. So W has at most #_k(X_W) + n_k(W) ≤ 19Mσ(W) link positions (M ≥ 100).
  - At a bond b, the groups whose span holds b contain a polymer whose span holds b. *Joins* roots these polymers at b, at total ≤ C_jne^{−p₀(g_k)/2}, and the rest of the group adds at most a factor 2 for small g_k (*The rooted sum*). At a cube, the groups are rooted by *The rooted sum*, at total ≤ C_gre^{−p₀(g_k)/2}.
  - So, with C_lk := 2max(C_gr, C_jn), the link factor of W is Π_{positions}(1 + C_lkε_k²) ≤ e^{19MC_lkε_k²σ(W)}. The join positions cost e^{(21/4)MC_jnε_k²σ(W)} (*Join count*). Together these are ≤ e^{(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} under 100MC_lkε_k ≤ ν_r, a smallness of g_k. The other cross insertions of G pay their own link factors, so the sum over the structures of G factorises over the cross insertions.
- *The lineage growth.* For W₁ the first cross insertion of G, at least one group is linked to it. So Π(1 + C_lkε_k²) − 1 ≤ 19MC_lkε_k²σ(W₁)e^{19MC_lkε_k²σ(W₁)}, and with the remaining e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W₁)} and σe^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ} ≤ 4/(eε_kν_r),
  Σ_{G: W₁ first}w_k(D_G) ≤ y^R_k·w_{k−1}(W₁), y^R_k := C_yε_k, C_y := 77MC_lk/(eν_r).
  - The other cross insertions of G enter with w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W), as in *Second case*. This C_y contains *Second case*'s 73C_gr/(eν_r), and it is the constant of (H^R).
- Constants added: u = 19 for the merged insertions, L₀^× = 10⁶, 100MC_lkε_k ≤ ν_r, and C_y = 77MC_lk/(eν_r).
- Where it enters: (6B)'s *The arc set*, *Second case*, and *15.8*; (6C)'s *The single loop's R-made groups*; *The units of the members*' cross members of single-loop groups; and the cross carry lemma at the R-steps.

**(R-att-ext), derived, not reviewed: the extent of an attached D_g.**
- The issue: D_g can be att(k, ·) through its box, a polymer, or a non-dev member. A member's own collar distance is measured from the collar of its production step (D2.6 closure 5.0), which an intervening R-step can remove. And the non-dev members' factor e^{17ν_r} in σ̃ is not in D2.7 15.7's member sum x_g.
- How it holds ((6C), *The single loop's R-made groups*, *The attached extent*, *The members' weights*):
  - The members enter through their domains: a dev member through ext(W), a non-dev one through diam_∞(T_W) ≤ (21/20)Mσ(W) or < 42M. Any two points of D_g's Y-set are within Δ_g, the hull's diameter plus 2R_k + 1 plus twice the largest member term.
  - When D_g's Y-set meets the post-R collar Coll_k (a polymer in a component, or the box near Λ_kᶜ ∪ S_k ∪ Ω_kᶜ or outside the new small-field region), D_g is att(k, ·) and ext^{coll}_k(D_g) ≤ Δ_g. Otherwise D_g continues the lineage of its first non-dev member W₁, as D2.6 closure 5.5 does for T-step merges, with ext^{coll}(D_g) ≤ ext^{coll}(W₁) + Δ_g and growth at most e^{−p₀(g_k)/4} at the R-step. A D_g with a wrap member is wrap(k).
  - A member enters σ̃ at half its σ, and the other half of its reserve pays its extent term. The non-dev members' e^{17ν_r} goes into D2.6 closure 5.0's species normalisation, as e^{c_W} does, once per insertion. It at most doubles A_j(Z) and ε_A, and the masses e^{2ΣA} stay polylogs of order r < p₀.

The cross groups of the R-gas whose polymers meet a second-class or unchanged component are paid in (6C), and so are the single loop's R-made groups, at the factor u = 16 when a slot holds a carried insertion, and the groups that share a cross member are merged by (R-member), at u = 19. Nothing else is needed beyond the derived and not reviewed (R-eval), (R-eval-cr), (R-member), (R-round), and (R-att-ext), and the inherited inputs below.

The bound also uses these conditions on constants:
- the smallness conditions (H^cr) ((6)) and (H^R) ((6B)) of the couplings;
- (T0)'s smallness of g for the multi-interval insertions, (3230 + 70756/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8 ((6A)(i), (6C), (R-member) at u = 19), of the kind the single loop imposes under (R-m′);
- the smallness of g_k at the R-steps in (6B) and (6C), among them 182C_Ψ′Mε_k ≤ ν_r, R_k ≥ 17/ν_r, R_k ≥ 46 ((6B), *Big and small members*), R_k ≥ 88 ((6B), *The volume*), e3^d(1 + C_α)e^{−δMR_k/2^{d+5}} ≤ ½ and 4c_re^{−δMR_k/16} ≤ ν_r ((6B), *15.5*, *The rates*), C_δc̄S_R + (1 + C_s + C_attC_y)S^R ≤ log 2 and y^s_k ≤ 1/32 ((6C), *The members' weights*), and the polylogs of order r < p₀ and the cost e^{5p₀(g_k)/64} of the sets X^{(44)}, the small members' rounding included, that the margin of (1.89) pays ((6B), *The volume*);
- δM ≥ 5·2^{d+3}ν_r ((6B), (6C)) and M ≥ 100 ((6B), *The units of the members*), lower bounds on M of the kind the map already imposes (l.973), with δ fixed before M by the reading of δ under *Inherited*;
- L ≥ L₀^× := 10⁶ ((6A)(i), (6C), (R-member)), a lower bound on L of the kind of the map's L₀ = 5000 (l.1551).

The conditions 5·2^{d+2}ν_r ≤ 1 and (5/2)ν_r ≤ ¼ of (6B) and (6C) hold by the map's ν ≤ 1/1000 (l.1614), with ν_r = ν and ν_e = ν/(2C_box) ≤ ν/8 (l.1531, l.1549). The span bound of (6B) uses a torus period of at least twice each side, N_s ≥ 2max(ℓ₁, ℓ₂), as V4's tori have (L1565).

*Inherited, not new:* the inputs of the single-loop (6.3). These are:
- (S-w) with its review status (map D2.4(12)–(13));
- D2.6's inputs (for the carry lemma: the D2.6 closure 5.3's list, with O6 and the base points of 5.1);
- D2.7 given the reading R-far″, with its pieces' domains in 𝐃_k as printed, in place of D2.7 15.5(ii)(β)'s "connected union of MR_k-cubes" ((6B));
- the large-field payment behind B_LF (continuum note (4.5));
- the reading of V4's step-1 factors in D(1)'s weights (map l.1350), applied to the cross groups ((6));
- LFR II's δ (p.376) as CRE's decay rate δ of the 𝐇-bounds B₃exp(−δ·), which CRE fixes before M₂R₁ is taken large (p.250 l.47–48), so that δ is fixed before M ((6B), *The payment*);
- the evaluation at U^R of the insertions that R leaves unchanged (D2.7 15.8, as for one loop);
- the bounds |μ^R_n|, |ζ^R_n| ≤ C_R𝔞_n on the coefficients of the R-steps (map l.1146, D2.7 note 15.4), which the continuum note's (6.1) needs. They control κ, which (8.2) divides out.

The continuum note's (2), (5.2), and (6.3) bound E_const by C_Σ𝔞̄ alone, which omits the R-steps' remainders E°^R_k(C) of D2.7 15.4(ii). (7) adds them for the pair as C^{R°}_E, and the single-loop bound needs the same term. That note is not changed here.

## What the Objects Are

- The pair observable W_{χ₁}(C₁;U)W_{χ₂}(C₂;U) is the normalised trace of π₁(Hol_UC₁) ⊗ π₂(Hol_UC₂) on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} (V4 eq:two-loop-observable). It is a product of two closed words, not the trace of one holonomy.
- The stopping step is common to both loops: K′ = K − j, with j the least j ≥ max(q₁, q₂, q₁₂) + q̄(j) (map l.193; that j exists and does not depend on ε, l.1093(i)).
  - So the corners of both loops are period points, and every step is commensurate for both.
  - At K′, C_i is a fixed contour C_i^{(K′)} with side ℓ_i = p_iL^{j−q_i} bonds.
  - D ≥ 1 is the ℓ^∞ distance between C₁ and C₂ in step-K′ units.
  - In step-k units the sides are ℓ_iL^{K′−k}, and every traversal of C_i lies at distance ≥ DL^{K′−k} from C_{3−i}.

## What That Forces

- **The normalisation factorises exactly.** The coefficients δm_{k+1} and ζ_{k+1} are ℤ⁴ quantities of a straight line and of a bent path, for one character (D2.3(5) *Independence*). Perimeters add, P_k(C₁ ⊔ C₂) = P_k(C₁) + P_k(C₂), and the pair has eight corners. So the pair's κ^{(K′)} is κ^{(K′)}_{C₁,χ₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂,χ₂}, with no cross factor ((5)). The cross constant E^× of (5) is the only cross term that the step map extracts. The loops stay coupled through the clusters of Q_k, which may hit insertions on both loops, through the cross insertions of (6), and through the common measure.
- **A cross group has no scalar flat value.** A group with arcs on both loops has a flat value in End(V_{π₁}) ⊗ End(V_{π₂}) (more tensor factors when its arc set on one loop has several intervals). This value commutes with π₁(u) ⊗ π₂(u) for u ∈ G, but it is not a scalar in general. So D2.3(3)'s subtraction c_𝒢T is not available for it. Cross groups get the flat value 0, as in V4 (a_W := 0, V4 L3590), and they are not contracted.
- **Cross groups are made with a doubly exponential decay in the distance to the stopping step.**
  - A cross group made at step k spans at least DL^{K′−k} step-k units.
  - The extent weight of (S-w) has the same rate at every step.
  - So the cross mass made at step k is ≤ C𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k}e^{−ν_×DL^{K′−k}/M}, on every labelled term.
- **So the cross groups are carried, not taken out.**
  - They form their own species cross(j), last in D2.0(3)'s order. They are never contracted, so no step needs their flat value.
  - The D2.6 closure's carry lemma carries them without a bridge. Every later step can touch them, since the loops lie in the small-field region on most terms. So each step multiplies their mass by at most 1 + C_δc̄ ((6)).
  - The doubly exponential decay pays this geometric growth: the carried cross total A^× of (7) is uniform in K.
  - So the class carries everything to the stopping step, and the large-field terms are paid there, by B^×_LF, as for one loop. No step below K′ needs a large-field bound for the cross species. The attached species are carried by D2.6 as for one loop.
  - Taking the cross words out at the step that makes them doesn't work: on the large-field terms that step has no bound uniform in K.

## Derivation

### (1) Setting (Quoted from V4 Section 10 and the Map, with Derived Bounds)

- C₁, C₂ are coarse translates of counterclockwise squares in planes parallel to that of e₁, e₂, with V(C₁) ∩ V(C₂) = ∅. χ_i = dim(π_i)^{−1}tr π_i, with π_i irreducible and unitary; the reversed orientation is χ̄_i (V4 L3556). C := C₁ ⊔ C₂. Its step-k traversals are those of C₁ and of C₂, 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k} in all. They are ordered as those of C₁, then those of C₂, each loop cyclically from its own base point ((6A)(iv)).
- *The cross distance.* For a step-k traversal n of C_i, δ_n^{(k)} is the step-k ℓ^∞ distance on the torus (periodic, as at V4 L3560) from n to C_{3−i}.
  - Both squares are unions of step-K′ bonds with corners on the step-K′ lattice (the q₁₂ clause of the stopping rule). Two lattice bonds without a common site are at ℓ^∞ distance ≥ 1. So D ≥ 1, and δ_n^{(k)} ≥ DL^{K′−k}. On the torus of period N_s step-K′ units, D ≤ N_s/2.
  - Put Δ_×^{(k)} := Σ_ne^{−ν_×δ_n^{(k)}/M} over the step-k traversals of C, with ν_× of the constants below. At the first step V4's Δ_× (eq:two-loop-cross-sum) has the same form with the rate c_×/(LM) per fine unit, c_× = 2η/A′_E ((6)).
  - Then Δ_×^{(k)} ≤ 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k}e^{−ν_×DL^{K′−k}/M}.
- *Constants.*
  - c_d = ν/(64ML) and C_S′ are those of the tail bound (T′) (map D2.3(5)).
  - ν_e = ν/(2C_box) is the extent rate of (S-w) (D2.4(12)(b)).
  - C_ζ is that of D2.3(5).
  - 𝔞̄ := max(τ̄², sup_kρ_k·pl_k, sup_kρ_{k+1}·pl_k), with pl_k := 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊} and R-m's exponents q₀, q₁ (not the loops' q₁, q₂). The first two entries are the amplitude of D2.3(7); the third is (S-w)'s amplitude at step k+1, which carries pl_k (map l.1536).
  - 𝔟̄ := max(𝔞̄, sup_kmax(ρ̂_k, b_k)·pl_k) is the amplitude of the wrap groups (map l.294: max(C_S′ρ̂_k, C_S″b_k)), with b_k the collar amplitude of the D2.6 closure §2.2.
  - ν_× := min(ν_e, 2η/A′_E).
  - C_Σ(χ) is D2.3(7)'s constant for the character χ.
  - C_ν is the constant of ρ̃_kδ*_n = O(ρ_k) on the support, under R-m's entailments (map l.290), with C_χ := max(C_{χ₁}, C_{χ₂}) inside it.
  - c̄ := e^{(3/8)ν_e}c̄_*sup_mb_m/ᾱ_m and C_δ := 2 are the D2.6 closure's (5.0), and c_W is map (12)(d)'s share constant (D2.6 closure 5.5), with c_sh^× of (6A)(i) in place of c_sh for every insertion (c_sh^× ≥ c_sh, and the insertions with several intervals and the touched ones of a two-loop M-vertex need it).
    - c̄_* contains C″ = 2(K_an+1)C_be^{(5/8)ν_e}/ε_* (D2.6 closure 5.5). K_an is the animal-sum constant of the closure's (T0) (5.2: ε^{new}_{re-run} ≤ C_Mb_n·pl + K_anb_n/ᾱ_n), written K′ there; it is not the stopping step K′. So c̄ does not depend on K.
  - r₀ := 2 is the range, in step-k units, within which a constituent carries a slot or a contact of a contour bond.
    - At step 1 the contact sets lie within 2L fine units, that is two step-1 units, of the domain (map l.177, V4 L3696).
    - At k ≥ 1 the new slots of a component Y are the bonds of C in Y (map l.478), and the old contact sets lie in the network (D2.3(4), map l.1083–1084), within 2L^{1−k} ≤ 2 step-k units of the step-1 domains.
    - r₀ enters only through e^{c_dr₀}.
- *Norm of a cross group.* Y_𝒢 is a sum of terms, each an ordered product of slot matrices, old insertions, and transports along each interval of ℐ(𝒢). ‖Y_𝒢‖ is the sum over these terms of the product of the factors' operator norms, a projective norm.
  - The rooted sums of D2.4(12) are sums of exactly this form ((6A)(i)).
  - A word containing Y_𝒢 is a normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of products of operators. So at real backgrounds its modulus is at most the product of the insertions' norms (V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-source, first sentence of the proof). At complex backgrounds the transports cost exp(c_π‖a′‖_∞·length) (map l.911), as for one loop.

### (2) The Two-Loop Class (Definition)

For k ≥ 0, the pair's dressed loop of step k on a labelled term is

𝒲^×_k = κ^{(k)}_{C₁}κ^{(k)}_{C₂}e^{𝓔^×_k}[f^{(1)}_kf^{(2)}_k + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}Q_k(𝔅)·w(𝔅)].

- f^{(i)}_k := dim(π_i)^{−1}tr π_i(Hol_{C_i}(U_k)).
- 𝔅 is a family of insertions. Each has arcs on one loop only (a C_i-insertion), or on both (a cross insertion, of species cross(j), or two-loop of a single-loop species by (6)'s rules).
- w(𝔅) is the normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of the two-contour word with the insertions of 𝔅 (D2.0(3)'s display, along each loop). Without cross insertions it is w₁(𝔅₁)w₂(𝔅₂), the product of the single-loop words, and w(∅) = f^{(1)}_kf^{(2)}_k.
- Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ_k(Γ)), over the clusters of the pair's step-k reference gas ((5)).
- κ^{(k)}_{C_i} := ∏_{j≤k}e^{−δm^{χ_i}_jP_{j−1}(C_i)}(z^{χ_i}_j)⁴, with the single-loop coefficients of χ_i (D2.0(3), D2.3(5)).
- 𝓔^×_k is the sum of the local terms and the constant E^×_const,k := E_const,k(C₁,χ₁) + E_const,k(C₂,χ₂) + Σ_{j≤k}E^×_j + Σ_{N_B<j≤k}E^{R×}_j. Here E_const,k(C_i,χ_i) is the single-loop constant of C_i, with the R-steps' remainders E°^R_j(C_i) of D2.7 15.4 in it, E^×_j is the cross constant of (5), and E^{R×}_j is that of the R-steps ((6B)).
- *The other clauses.* The norms (ii) and (ii′), analyticity (iii), the constants (iv), the scalar parts (v), and torus consistency (vi) are D(k)'s clauses (map l.276–325) with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, per traversal of either loop, under the reading (6A).
- *The species.* D(k)'s species (map l.281–286) and one more, cross(j) for j ≤ k. Its insertions are of three kinds.
  - The cross groups made by the step map at step j that are not attached or wrap by the D2.6 closure §2.1's rule ((6)). These pass §2.1's tests (i)–(iv), so they lie in (S-w)'s scope.
  - The cross groups of the R-gas made by the R-operation at step j > N_B that 15.6(e)'s scope rule does not make att(j, ·), with no cross member ((6B), first case). Their units are (6B)'s count.
  - The descendants of both ((6), and (6B)'s second case). In D2.0(3)'s order (map l.286) cross(j) comes after wrap(j). A cross insertion carries no deviation bound and is never contracted.
- *(ii^cr), the cross clause.* For each j ≤ k the cross(j) insertions present at step k satisfy M^cr_k(j) := Σe^{c_W}w(W) ≤ Γ_{j,k}((1 + y^R_j)A^cr_j + A^R_j), with Γ_{j,k} := Π_{j<k′≤k}(1 + C_δc̄)(1 + y^R_{k′}).
  - w(W) := e^{λ_k(W)ν_rσ(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ is the D2.6 closure 5.0's weight without its lineage extent, at the rate λ_k(W)ν_r of (6B), with σ(W) fixed at production. ‖Y_W‖ is the sup over the M-factored domain on 𝓜^c ∩ 𝒟^λ_k before the R-step at k, and over 𝒟^{ev}_k after it, with λ the dilation of *The R-step lands in the family* ((iii), *The M-factored domain*; (R-eval-cr)).
  - Each insertion is counted once, at the first traversal of its arc set. A^cr_j is the production bound of (6); y^R_j and A^R_j are (6B)'s at the R-steps and 0 at the other steps, so that Γ_{j,k} = (1 + C_δc̄)^{k−j} when no R-step lies in (j, k].
- *The base.* At k = 0 there are no insertions and κ^{(0)} := 1, so N^×_0 := W_{χ₁}(C₁)W_{χ₂}(C₂)ρ₀.
- *D^×(1).* This is IT-1's step-1 class built on V4 Section 10's objects for the pair:
  - the groups of Definition def:two-loop-groups;
  - the reference scalars a_W, with a_W := 0 for cross groups;
  - the retreated carrier D(Λ) of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated, with its cross exponent E^×_Λ (part (a)).
  V4's cross families W are the species cross(1). Their factors are bounded by M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{𝔞_W/8}, as in the proof of V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-cross(c) (L3709, by Lemma lem:two-loop-defect). D(1)'s weights read them as IT-1 reads V4's single-loop factors (map l.1350, an inherited reading). The production bound is (6)'s *first step*.
- *The identity (derived in (6)).* With N^×_k := Σ_ℓχT[𝒲^×_ke^{A_k}] the class's numerator density (map l.326), Z·E_ε[W_{χ₁}(C₁)W_{χ₂}(C₂)] = ∫N^×_k dV_k for every k ≤ K′.

### (3) The Step Map for the Pair, Ingredient by Ingredient

- *D2.0(1)–(2), units and background regularity:* D2.0(1)'s contour bullet (C is a union of block bonds at every scale, map l.241) holds for C₁ ⊔ C₂ by the stopping rule with q₁₂. The rest does not involve the contour. Verbatim.
- *D2.1, the leading word (derived by the product rule):*
  - f^{(1)}_kf^{(2)}_k = (f^{(1)}_{k+1} + s^{(1)})(f^{(2)}_{k+1} + s^{(2)}), with s^{(i)} := f^{(i)}_k − f^{(i)}_{k+1} the D2.1 slot expansion of C_i.
  - Expanded, this is the product of the two single-loop expansions: f^{(1)}_{k+1}f^{(2)}_{k+1}, words with slots on one loop, and words with slots on both. All are products of two single-loop words.
  - So the slots are C_i-insertions, and the words keep the two-trace form. At real fields the leading words have modulus ≤ 1; at complex fields D(k)(iii)'s bounds apply to each loop.
- *D2.2, the small-field map:* the observable enters only through the slots and contact sets of C₁ ⊔ C₂. The domains, probe measures, vacuum clusters, and reference data do not involve it, as at V4 L3566. Derived.
- *D2.2(6)(d), the groups (definition):*
  - The merging rules are applied with hulls and long-ness per loop, as V4 Definition def:two-loop-groups does at step 1.
  - The arc set is ℐ(𝒢) = ℐ₁(𝒢) ⊔ ℐ₂(𝒢). The hull on C_i is taken in the cyclic set of C_i's traversals. At k ≥ 1 the threshold P_k(C_i)/2 only names the shorter-arc convention: a gap arc is the shorter of the two arcs (map l.904), and no hull is replaced by all of C_i. V4's replacement of a long hull by I_{C_i} (rule (A2), V4 L3571) is used at step 1 only, where V4 bounds the long groups by their total mass (Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(c)–(d)).
  - The linked hull (map l.904, "the shorter of the two arcs of C between them") is taken per loop. Joined pieces on different loops add no gap arc, and the group is then a cross group. When a joined pair has an insertion D with several pieces on C_i, its gap arc is defined as the shorter arc between the other's piece and the nearest piece of ℐ_i(D), since the shorter arc to another piece can run around the far side of C_i. Each loop's traversals are numbered cyclically from its own base point (V4 L3560).
  - A C_i-group has ℐ_{3−i}(𝒢) = ∅. A cross group has both parts nonempty. A group with a cross member is a cross group, since its arc set contains the member's.
- *D2.3(2), the flat value:*
  - For a C_i-group, Schur's lemma for π_i (as V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-schur(a)). Derived.
  - For a cross group, c_𝒢 := 0. Definition.
- *D2.3(3), the factorisation:* (6). Derived.
- *D2.3(4):*
  - Contour locality: (4), derived by D2.3(4)'s induction for objects with short arcs. Long groups are paid by (T′) in (5), as the map pays whole-contour groups (map l.1086).
  - Periodicity: the corners of both loops are period points under the stopping rule (map l.193). Verbatim.
- *D2.3(5), the coefficients:* δm^{χ_i}_{k+1} and ζ^{χ_i}_{k+1}, one pair for each character. Verbatim.
- *D2.3(6)–(8), the remainder, the sum over the steps, and the assembly:* (5) and (7). Derived.
- *D2.4, the contraction count and (S-w):*
  - These are per position. A position meets the traversals of two contours, so the per-position counts at most double. The multi-interval arc sets and the long groups are (6A)(i)–(ii), with V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(a)–(d) at step 1.
  - The dev contraction is used for C_i-groups only. Cross groups need no deviation factor, since they are carried uncontracted ((6)).
- *D2.5, D2.6, and D2.7:* (6A) and (6B).

### (4) Locality for the Pair (Derived by the Induction of D2.3(4), for Short Arcs)

(L×) Take any step k and a non-cross object of the pair on C_i: an insertion, a group with its occurrences, a cluster of the reference gas, or its weight, all of whose groups have short arcs on C_i (fewer than P_k(C_i)/2 traversals). Suppose its network has no point within r₀ of C_{3−i}.
- Then it is the object of the single-loop class of C_i with the same constituents, and it has the same value.
- Conversely, a single-loop object of C_i whose network has no point within r₀ of C_{3−i} is a non-cross object of the pair.
- The network of a group contains the domains of its old members and of the members of its merged occurrences (map l.1144: the supports, the arc hulls, and the merged occurrences' domains). Inductively, it therefore contains the domains of the group's whole lineage.

*Step 1.*
- V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-grouping(c): the C_i-groups of the pair are the single-square groups of C_i none of whose roots has a contact on C_{3−i}. They have the same interval, weight, footprint, and compatibility.
- a_W = a^{(i)}_W (V4 L3590).
- The paragraph *Cancellation* in the proof of Lemma lem:two-loop-cross: tuples of C_i-groups have the same Ursell coefficient and the same activities.
- A constituent with a contact on C_{3−i} lies within r₀ of C_{3−i}.
- At the retreated carrier D(Λ) these transfer: by the proof of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated, part (i), the grouping lemma does not involve the carrier (V4 L3737).

*Step k → k+1.*
- A step-(k+1) object is built from its constituents by the composition, the joint cluster expansion, the merging rules, the flat value, and the hard core.
- For short arcs these use the contour only through the slots, arcs, and contact sets inside the object's network (D2.3(4) *Contour locality*, map l.1083; its proof is per object). A group with a short arc has only members with short arcs, since its arc contains theirs.
- The constituents are step-k objects whose networks lie inside the new network, so the hypothesis applies to them. The vacuum activities and occurrences do not involve the contour.
- Consider an old occurrence with arcs on both loops, that is, a cluster containing a C₁-group and a C₂-group. It puts arcs on both loops into the group it merges into, since the third merging rule takes the hull over J_Γ. That group is then a cross group.
- So no non-cross object contains such an occurrence or a cross insertion. Cross groups have activity 0 in the reference gas; in the words they are the cross insertions of (6).
- Groups long on C_i are not covered. D2.3(4)'s contour locality is for short arcs (map l.1083, l.1086), and the long-ness and the membership of a long group are properties of the whole term. The step-(k+1) analogue of V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-grouping(a) and (c) for long groups is not derived. (5) pays the clusters with long groups by (T′) instead.

### (5) The Factorisation of the Reference Gas (Derived)

Work on ℓ₀ at the map k → k+1, k ≥ 1. The first step is V4's (*The first step* below).
- S°(ℓ₀) is the pair's reference group gas: C₁- and C₂-groups with their activities c_𝒢, cross activities 0, and D2.3(3)'s hard core.
- S°_{C_i}(ℓ₀) is the single-loop gas of C_i.

Put E^×_{k+1} := log S°(ℓ₀) − log S°_{C₁}(ℓ₀) − log S°_{C₂}(ℓ₀).

- *Allocation.* In each gas put a(b) := Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}Φ°(Γ)/|J_Γ| (D2.3(6)(a)).
  - Then log S° = Σ_ba(b) over the step-k traversals of C, and log S°_{C_i} = Σ_{b∈C_i}a_{C_i}(b).
  - So E^×_{k+1} = Σ_iΣ_{b∈C_i}(a(b) − a_{C_i}(b)). The rearrangement uses absolute convergence, from the pair gas's Kotecký–Preiss condition ((6A)(v)).
- *Cancellation.* Let b ∈ C_i, and let Γ ∋ b be a cluster of either gas whose network has diameter < δ_b^{(k)} − r₀ and which contains no group long on C_i.
  - Its network has no point within r₀ of C_{3−i}.
  - By (L×) it is a cluster of both gases, with the same weight and the same J_Γ; the Ursell function depends only on the incompatibility graph, which is the same in both.
  - So a(b) − a_{C_i}(b) contains only clusters of network diameter ≥ δ_b^{(k)} − r₀ and clusters with a group long on C_i.
  - The latter have network diameter ≥ ℓ_iL^{K′−k} = P_k(C_i)/4: the arc of a long group has at least 2ℓ_iL^{K′−k} consecutive traversals of C_i, so it contains a whole side, as for the whole-contour groups of map l.1086.
- *Bound.* By (T′) for each gas (for the pair gas by (6A)(v)), |a(b) − a_{C_i}(b)| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞_{k+1}(e^{−c_d(δ_b^{(k)} − r₀)} + e^{−c_dℓ_iL^{K′−k}}). Hence
  |E^×_{k+1}| ≤ 8C_S′e^{c_dr₀}𝔞_{k+1}(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′−k}e^{−c_dDL^{K′−k}} + 8C_S′𝔞_{k+1}Σ_iℓ_iL^{K′−k}e^{−c_dℓ_iL^{K′−k}}.
  The second term does not decay in D. It would, given the long-group analogue of V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-grouping(a) and (c) at the steps k ≥ 1; the bound of (8.2) does not need it.
- *The first step.* E^×_1 is V4's cross exponent at the retreated carrier, E^×_Λ of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated(a), with the bound of Lemma lem:two-loop-cross(b): |E^×_1| ≤ C_×ρ_VΔ_× ≤ 4C_×ρ_V(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′}e^{−c_×DL^{K′−1}/M}. There are 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^{K′} fine traversals, each with δ_a ≥ DL^{K′} fine units, and V4's ρ_V (at g₀) is ≤ C_V(1+β₀)⁶ρ₁ (map l.298), with C_V depending on χ₁ and χ₂ through V4's constants (V4 L3614).
- *Assembly.*
  - By D2.3(8) for C_i alone, log S°_{C_i}(ℓ₀) = −δm^{χ_i}_{k+1}P_k(C_i) + 4ζ^{χ_i}_{k+1} + E°_{k+1}(C_i).
  - So log S°(ℓ₀) = −Σ_iδm^{χ_i}_{k+1}P_k(C_i) + 4Σ_iζ^{χ_i}_{k+1} + E°_{k+1}(C₁) + E°_{k+1}(C₂) + E^×_{k+1}.
  - Then D2.3(8)'s assembly holds for the pair, with κ_{k+1} := κ_{C₁,k+1}κ_{C₂,k+1} and E°_{k+1} := E°_{k+1}(C₁) + E°_{k+1}(C₂) + E^×_{k+1}.
  - E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) := log S°(ℓ) − log S°(ℓ₀) is D2.3(8)'s, for the pair gas.
