### (6) The Cross Groups: Production and Carry (Derived Given the Inherited Inputs)

- *The step.* Apply the map k → k+1 to N^×_k. After the interactions are expanded (D2.3(3)), the family sum runs over the compatible families of step-(k+1) groups of the pair.
  - D2.3(3)'s multilinear expansion Y_𝒢 = c_𝒢T + Y^d_𝒢 and its resummation apply as for one loop. Each factor of the two-trace word is expanded along its own loop, as in V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-schur(b)–(c) at step 1.
  - For a cross group c_𝒢 = 0, so Y^d_𝒢 = Y_𝒢: the group enters the new word as an uncontracted insertion.
  - The result is 𝒲^×_{k+1} of (2), with insertions of every species, Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) = S°^{[D]}(ℓ)/S°(ℓ), and the factor κ_{k+1} of (5).
  - Nothing is split off. So the numerator identity (map l.326) gives the identity of (2) by induction on k. At the R-steps the R-operation preserves the integral (D2.7 item 3, for the pair by (6B)).
- *The species of a new cross group.*
  - The D2.6 closure §2.1's rule, which amends map l.282's (its *Rule changes*), applies with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, as for any group. It makes the group att(k+1, Z) if only test (iv) fails (§2.1(a)), att(k+1, coll) if (ii) fails or (i) or (iii) fails (§2.1(b)–(c)), and wrap(k+1) if its set does not lift to the cover (§2.1(e)). These species carry no deviation bound, so they need no flat value. They are carried and paid as for one loop ((6A), D2.6).
  - A cross group made from parents that are not all dev takes the first species of its parents in D2.0(3)'s order (§2.1 *Merged groups*, map l.286), with cross(j) after wrap(j).
  - The other new cross groups are cross(k+1). They pass §2.1's tests (i)–(iv) and lift, so they lie in (S-w)'s scope, the groups passing (i)–(iii) (SW §1(a1)).
  - Map l.281's reclassification is a rule for dev insertions, so a cross insertion keeps its species.
  - At an R-step these rules are replaced by (6B)'s. The single-loop members' lineages end in the slots (D2.7 15.8(b)). A cross group of the R-gas with a cross member is a descendant of its first cross member. One without is att(k, ·) when a polymer of it meets a second-class or unchanged component (15.6(e)), and cross(k) otherwise.
- *Production.* Let 𝒢 be a cross(k+1) group, and m := K′ − k − 1.
  - (S-w) applies to 𝒢 on every labelled term (D2.4(12)(b), for the groups satisfying (i)–(iii), the scope that the D2.6 closure 5.0 states).
  - 𝒢 has a traversal n ∈ ℐ_i(𝒢) and a traversal of C_{3−i} in ℐ(𝒢) ⊆ X(𝒢). So its extent from n is ext_{k+1}(𝒢) ≥ δ_n^{(k+1)}/M, since π_{k+1}-cubes have side M step-(k+1) units.
  - Pin 𝒢 at the first traversal of its arc set and use (S-w) at the positions n. Here ‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ S_𝒢, with S_𝒢 read as the projective majorant of (1) (map l.1575: S_𝒢 is at most the product of the vertex sups and the transports), ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≥ 1, and the whole extent weight is spent on δ_n. (S-w)'s amplitude is ρ_{k+1}pl_k, with pl_k := 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}. Since ν_× ≤ ν_e and ρ_{k+1}pl_k ≤ 𝔞̄ ((1)),
    A^cr_{k+1} := Σ_{𝒢∈cross(k+1)}e^{c_W}w(𝒢) ≤ e^{c_W}C_S′ρ_{k+1}pl_kΔ_×^{(k+1)} ≤ 4C_cr𝔞̄(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^me^{−ν_×DL^m/M}.
  - *The first step.* The cross(1) insertions are V4's cross groups at the retreated carrier D(Λ), with ‖Y_W‖ ≤ M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{𝔞_W/8} ((2)) and 𝔞_W = η𝔰(W) (V4 L5249). At step 1, σ = 𝔰, ν_r = ν = η, λ₁ = 1, and e^{θ(W)} ≤ e^{η𝔰/16} (map l.1531, V3 L2806). So e^{c_W}w(W) ≤ e^{c_W}e^{(19/16)η𝔰(W)}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W).
    - The distance. V4 Lemma lem:two-loop-cross(a) gives 𝔰(W) > δ_a/(A′_ELM) for every a ∈ ℋ(W) (its proof, L3677: the envelope is connected, has at most A′_E𝔰(W) cubes, and meets both loops). So e^{−2η𝔰(W)} ≤ e^{−c_×δ_a/(LM)}, with c_× = 2η/A′_E (L3658).
    - The pins. The pins (c4-6-5) hold for the groups of the pair (Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(d), L3644), and at D(Λ) for M^{(ρ)}_Λ with the same constants (proof of Lemma lem:two-loop-retreated, (v)–(vi), L3737). The pin per fine traversal a carries e^{4η𝔰}.
    - Pin each cross group at a traversal of its hull, and spend 2η𝔰 on the distance and (19/16)η𝔰 ≤ 2η𝔰 on the reserve, θ, and e^{𝔞_W/8}. At complex backgrounds the group bounds carry e^{C′𝔰ρ₁} ≤ e^{η𝔰/8} more (map l.1529), and (21/16)η𝔰 ≤ 2η𝔰 still. So
      A^cr_1 ≤ e^{c_W}Σ_{a∈ℐ_C}e^{−c_×δ_a/(LM)}Σ_{W∋a}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{4η𝔰(W)} ≤ e^{c_W}C_pinρ_VΔ_×,
      with C_pin the pins' constant (C_pin ≤ C_×, L3711).
    - A fine traversal a inside a step-1 traversal n has δ_a ≥ Lδ_n^{(1)} fine units, so V4's Δ_× is at most L·Δ_×^{(1)}, since ν_× ≤ c_×. And ρ_V ≤ C_V(1+β₀)⁶ρ₁ (map l.298), where C_V depends on χ₁ and χ₂ through V4's constants (V4 L3614).
  - So the bound holds at every step with C_cr := e^{c_W}L·max(C_S′, C_V(1+β₀)⁶C_pin) on 𝒟^{sub}, and with C_cr^λ := λ²C_cr in place of C_cr on 𝒟^λ, the domain of (ii^cr) ((R-eval-cr), *Production at the T-steps*). Below, C_cr stands for C_cr^λ.
- *Lemma (cross carry).* Let j ≤ n < K′. On every labelled term, on 𝒟^λ_{n+1}, with the weights w at the rates λ_{n+1}(D)ν_r of (6B):
  - M^cr_{n+1}(j) ≤ (1 + C_δc̄)(1 + y^R_{n+1})M^cr_n(j), and M^cr_j(j) ≤ (1 + y^R_j)A^cr_j + A^R_j;
  - summed over the terms of the multilinear expansion at step n+1, the products of the cross(j) insertions are at most Π_D(1 + e^{c_W}w(D)Γ_{j,n+1}) ≤ exp(Γ_{j,n+1}((1 + y^R_j)A^cr_j + A^R_j)), with Γ_{j,n+1} := Π_{j<k≤n+1}(1 + C_δc̄)(1 + y^R_k) and D over the cross(j) insertions present after the R-step at j;
  - y^R_k and A^R_k are (6B)'s at the R-steps k > N_B, and 0 at the other steps.
  The constants do not depend on j, n, K, or the labelled term.
- *Proof.* This is the D2.6 closure §5 at lineage extent zero, without (T1) and (T3).
  - (T0) holds as written. The cross(j) insertions present at step n are fixed data of a term, with at most 1020Mσ(D) positions each ((6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor*, at u = 6 for those the R-steps make and their descendants, (6B)'s count), or 3230Mσ(D) for the merged insertions of (R-member), at u = 19, charged to b(D) = (ν_r/4)σ(D).
  - 5.1: a cross insertion carries no deviation, and its bound is a sup bound on 𝒟^λ_n. The step-(n+1) evaluation points of data in 𝒟^λ_{n+1} lie in 𝒟^λ_n (*The dilated sub-domains*, the bullet on data in 𝒟^λ_{k+1}). So its norm does not grow at the new evaluation point.
  - 5.4: its transports do not grow, interval by interval, under D2.6 §5.4's condition on the family, 4c_πMλα_{1,n+1} ≤ 1 (*The consumers of the gradient and current conditions*).
  - (T2) and 5.5 without a bridge give 5.6's displays for the steps without a bridge, at lineage extent zero; those displays use only the exponents. A touched descendant has weighted mass ≤ c̄w(D), and a merged insertion adds ≤ c̄w(W).
  - So y_{n+1} ≤ C_δc̄M^cr_n(j) and M^cr_{n+1}(j) ≤ (1 + C_δc̄)M^cr_n(j), as in 5.3. Step 3 of (T0)'s product form gives the product bound.
  - 5.5's reserve budget is unchanged: for the insertions with several intervals its share is (6A)(i)'s, within (ν_r/4)σ + c_sh^× for L ≥ L₀^×. Its merge-extent charge (ν_r/16)σ is not used, so the budget needs (15/16)ν_rσ, within the rate λ_n(D)ν_r ≥ (15/16)ν_r, since λ_n ≥ 31/32 and s(D) ≤ 1 ((6B), *The rates*).
  - At an R-step the factor 1 + y^R_k and the new mass A^R_k are (6B)'s, and so is the count of the positions of the R-made insertions.
  - A group whose parents have several species is counted in its first parent's lineage, and the other parents are data of the term (5.5). So a cross insertion that becomes a non-first member of an attached lineage keeps its own factor in the product, and it stays in M^cr.
  - There is no bridge. A later step touches the insertion through supp B′ ⊆ Ω_{n+1}, which can contain the loops, so no distance is gained. The growth per step is the price, and (7) pays it.
- *The smallness (H^cr):* C_δc̄ ≤ 1. Like the D2.6 closure's C_δc̄S_R ≤ log 2, this is a smallness of g under (R-m′) with q > p₀, since b_m/ᾱ_m ~ (log g_m^{−2})^{−(q−p₀)}. Then 1 + C_δc̄ ≤ 2 ≤ L.
- *The large-field terms.* Nothing is taken out below K′, so the cross species need no bound on the large-field terms of any step. The attached species still need the D2.6 closure's per-step bounds, which (6A) transfers to the pair. At K′ the words of the class are bounded as for one loop, with the cross insertions inside them. Summed over the expansion, the products of the cross insertions are ≤ e^{A^×} ((7)), uniformly over the labelled terms.

### (6A) The Two-Component Reading of the Map (Derived Given the Inherited Inputs)

Each item is the one-contour statement with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, checked where two components change it. At step 1 the model is V4: Lemmas lem:two-loop-pins(a)–(d), lem:two-loop-source, and lem:two-loop-retreated.
- (i) *Multi-interval arc sets.*
  - *Intervals.* A group's arc set on C_i is the union of the linked hulls of its classes of joined pairs on C_i ((3)). A class can have several intervals on one loop: two of its pieces on C₁ can be joined only through a piece on C₂, and then no gap arc joins them. The counts below use the number of intervals only at step 1 (*Base*).
  - *Connectedness.* For an insertion D, X_D is its counted domain, the cubes of its constituents' domains (map l.1525), and X^{Cov}_D is its Cov domain (map l.622), the set of the configuration that D depends on, which contains ℐ(D). The proof is by induction on the step, with the following hypothesis for every old insertion D.
    - (H0) X^{Cov}_D lies in the closed ℓ∞-neighbourhood of radius 8M fine units of X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(D)), k the current step. The cubes of a scale are unions of those of the finer scales, so (H0) at one step gives it at the later ones.
    - (H1) A closed-connected set S_D contains X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D, with diam_ℓ∞(S_D) ≤ u_DMσ(D) in production units, u_D the factor of *Units with a factor*.
    - (H2) E_D := X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D)) is closed-connected, H_i(D) the arcs of *Filled hulls*.
    D's domain one step later is the set of π_{k+1}-cubes meeting the 8M-neighbourhood of E_D. By (H0) and (H2) it is closed-connected and contains X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D), since E_D contains the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(D) ⊆ H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D), and *The share of D* counts it. For a new group 𝒢, X(𝒢) is the union of its constituents' domains, the old insertions' domains one step later, and S_𝒢 := X(𝒢) ∪ ℐ(𝒢). Map l.1314's argument needs I(𝒢) to be one arc, so it is replaced by the following.
    - The constituents of a class are connected through its joined pairs.
    - Each constituent's domain meets ℐ(𝒢); or it meets X^{Cov}_D for a touched old insertion D, whose domain one step later is connected and contains X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D), with ℐ(D) ⊆ ℐ(𝒢); or it is a vacuum-link cluster, which touches M (map l.1553).
    - The linked hulls of the classes are connected by overlaps on one loop or the other (map l.1547, part (3), per loop).
    - So S_𝒢 is closed-connected, and diam_ℓ∞(S_𝒢) ≤ M#_{k+1}(𝒢) + n_{k+1}(𝒢) = Mσ_{k+1}(𝒢). The pieces are the π_{k+1}-cubes of X(𝒢), each of diameter M, and the bonds of ℐ(𝒢), and a connected union of closed pieces has diameter at most the sum of theirs.
    - So 𝒢 has (H0)–(H2), with S_𝒢 and u = 1. Its Cov domain lies in S_𝒢, since its constituents' Cov domains lie in their domains one step later. E_𝒢 is closed-connected, since S_𝒢 is and each H_i(𝒢) is an arc meeting ℐ_i(𝒢).
    - *R-made insertions.* D_g^× of (6B) has X_D = hull_c(g), the counted cubes of hull(g) ((6B), 15.6(b)–(c)), which need not be connected, and ℐ(D) = ℐ(g), which holds the members' arc sets.
      - (H0): D_g^× depends on U^R on hull(g) and, through its members, on their Cov domains. The π_k-cubes meeting hull(g) lie in hull_c(g) or meet ℐ(g) ((6B), *Lengths*). The members' (H0) puts their Cov domains in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_W ∪ (the cubes meeting ℐ(W)), with X_W ⊆ hull(g) and ℐ(W) ⊆ ℐ(g). So X^{Cov}_D lies in the 8M-neighbourhood of hull_c(g) ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(g)).
      - (H1) holds with S_D := S_g of (6B), *The count*, and u = 6.
      - (H2): E_D := hull_c(g) ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(g) ∪ H₂(g)), the form of (H2) with X_D = hull_c(g). E_D contains every π_k-cube meeting hull(g), since those outside hull_c(g) meet ℐ(g) ⊆ H₁(g) ∪ H₂(g).
        - The members need no cubes of their own. A member W lies in a slot of a polymer P ∈ g, and dom P contains Dep(W) = X_W ∪ X^{Cov}_W ((6B), *Lengths*), which contains ℐ(W) (*Connectedness*). So dom P holds X_W and all of W's intervals, on both loops.
        - T_W is closed-connected, contains X_W and ℐ(W), and lies in X_W ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting ℐ(W)) ((6B), *The units of the members*), so in E_D, since X_W ⊆ dom P and ℐ(W) ⊆ ℐ(g).
      - E_D is closed-connected. Each dom P is connected (*Lengths*), so the π_k-cubes meeting it, all in E_D, form a closed-connected set, and it contains its slots' bonds, which lie in ℐ(g), and its slot members' sets X_W and intervals (above). Each H_i(g) is an arc containing ℐ_i(g). And g has a polymer with slot bonds on both loops, or a cross member, whose intervals on both loops lie in its polymer's dom P (*The groups of 15.4*).
      - The spans in S_g need not lie in H₁(g) ∪ H₂(g), and E_D does not use them.
    - *Base.* At step 1 the hypothesis holds by V4's derived connectivity.
      - *Connectivity* (V4 lem:two-loop-pins(a), its proof): for a component γ of W, X_γ is connected; a contact of γ on C_i lies in the hull H^i_γ, a cyclic interval or I_{C_i}, and within 2L fine units of X_γ (proof of lem:two-loop-pins(b)); overlapping components share a traversal; and in a group long on C_i every overlap class has a contact on C_i, or the group is long on both loops and a class has contacts on both. So X(W) ∪ π₁(ℋ(W)) is connected. X(W) itself need not be.
      - *Support.* Y_W depends on the configuration on X(W), on the coefficient stencil halo, which lies within 2R_h < 8M fine units of the constituent domains (V4 L5229; map l.177: the hosts are rectangles of at most four M-cubes per direction), on the contour and free-input stencils of the traversals of ℋ(W), within 2L of their initial vertices (V4 L5229), and on the transports of ℐ(W).
      - Put S_W := the closed ℓ∞-neighbourhood of radius 8M fine units of X(W) ∪ ℐ(W), X(W) the union of its π₁-cubes. It contains the support and ℐ(W), so X^{Cov}_W ⊆ S_W, which gives (H0) and (H1). It is closed-connected by *Connectivity*, since 2L ≤ 8M. (H2) is *Connectivity* at the cube level, since π₁(ℋ(W)) lies in the cubes meeting H₁(W) ∪ H₂(W).
      - *Counts.* #_1(X_W) ≤ n(W), since n(W) counts the π₁-cubes of the roots and constituent domains (V4 L5197). ℐ(W) has at most 2n(W) intervals, at most |W| ≤ n(W) on each loop (proof of lem:two-loop-pins(a), *Count*), and each has at most two partial step-1 bonds. So n_1(W) ≤ |ℋ(W)|/L + 4n(W), and #_1(X_W) + n_1(W)/M ≤ (1 + 4/M)𝔰(W).
      - *Diameter.* S_W is the union of the 8M-neighbourhoods of the at most n(W) π₁-cubes of X(W), each of diameter at most M + 16M/L step-1 bonds, and of the at most 2n(W) intervals, an interval of t bonds giving at most t + 16M/L. So diam_ℓ∞(S_W) ≤ Mn(W) + n_1(W) + 48(M/L)n(W) ≤ (1 + 4/M + 48/L)M𝔰(W).
      - So the step-1 insertions have the units of *Units with a factor* with σ := 𝔰 and u ≤ u₁ := 1 + 4/M + 48/L. A single-loop step-1 insertion is the case of one loop.
  - *Vertices on two loops.* For a vertex with intervals on both loops, diam_v := max_i max_{y∈supp v} dist(y, int_i(v)), over the loops on which v has an interval.
    - For a W-vertex and a Γ-member with an interval on C_i, map (12)(d)'s bound holds on C_i. For an M-vertex it does not hold per loop as the map states it: a point of a touched X_W is within diam_W of W's interval, which can lie on the other loop. It is replaced by the following bound.
    - Put Q_v := M ∪ ∪_{W∈𝔅(M)}S_W ∪ ∪_{𝒱(M)}supp 𝒞. It is closed-connected: M is connected (map (10)), each touched W has X^{Cov}_W meeting M (map l.676: the W with X_W meeting a component Y ⊆ M, X_W the Cov domain of map l.622), X^{Cov}_W ⊆ S_W with S_W connected ((H1) of *Connectedness*), and each 𝒞 touches M.
    - Each int_i(v) contains a point of Q_v: a bond of C_i in a component of M, or a bond of a touched interval ℐ_i(W) ⊆ S_W. supp v ⊆ Q_v, so
      diam_v ≤ diam_ℓ∞(Q_v) ≤ M#(M) + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}(M#(𝒞) + 1),
      with diam_ℓ∞(S_W) ≤ u_W(M/L)σ_k(W), u_W the factor of *Units with a factor* (u_W ≤ 16). For a single-interval W made after step 1, S_W = X_W ∪ int(W) has diameter ≤ (M/L)#_k(W) + n_k(W)/L = (M/L)σ_k(W), so u_W = 1; at step 1, u_W ≤ u₁ (*Base*). The bound is the map's with diam_W replaced by diam_ℓ∞(S_W).
    - The window extends each int_i(v), so |win(v)| ≤ Σ_i|int_i(v)| + 80(diam_v + 1) ≤ |win_M| + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}80(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}80(M#(𝒞) + 1), with |win_M| of *M-vertices* below. A Γ-vertex's window is the union of its members' windows (map l.1543).
    - *Touched W.* A touched W with several intervals has window part 80(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) ≤ 80u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) + 80, within its own |win(W)| ≤ 88u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) + 84 (*The share of D*, *Units with a factor*), so its share is as there. A touched single-interval W has |win(W)| ≤ |int(W)| + 80(diam_ℓ∞(S_W) + 1) ≤ 81u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) + 80 in place of map sh(W)'s 41(M/L)σ_k(W) + 40. Map sh(W)'s computation, with ℓ(W)'s ν_f|win(W)|/M (map l.1554), gives
      sh(W) ≤ ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 160/M) + u_W(1186ν_f + 81ν_e)σ_k(W)/L + ν_e(1/8 + 1/L)ext_k(W) + ν_e(2 + 80/M) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)|,
      so sh(W) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ_k(W) + (ν_e/8 + ν_e/L)ext_k(W) + c_sh′ + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)|, c_sh′ := 2ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 81/M) + 2ν_e(2 + 81/M) ≤ c_sh^×, when u_W(1186·17/16 + 81/8)/L ≤ 1/4, that is L ≥ 5081u_W, which L₀^× covers. Its transport condition u_Wc_πα_{1,k+1}·81M/L ≤ ν_r/16 follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν.
    - *Vacuum links.* |win_𝒞| := 80(M#(𝒞) + 1), so |win_𝒞|/M ≤ 81#(𝒞). Map sh(𝒞)'s computation, with ℓ(𝒞) and ext ≤ #(𝒞) + 1, gives sh(𝒞) ≤ (164.125ν_f + 85ν_e)#(𝒞) ≤ 2(83ν_f + 86ν_e)#(𝒞) ≤ #(𝒞)/4 for ν ≤ 1/1000, under c_πα_{1,k+1}·81M ≤ ν_f, which follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν. So map l.1553's bound stands.
  - *Filled hulls.* Let D be an old insertion (a W-vertex, a member of a Γ-vertex, or a fixed member of the D2.6 closure's (T0)) with intervals on both loops or several intervals on one.
    - H_i(D) := the smallest arc of C_i containing ℐ_i(D).
    - win(D) := the union over i of H_i(D), extended along C_i by 20(diam_D + 1) at each end, with diam_D := max_i max_{y∈supp D} dist(y, ℐ_i(D)).
    - The positions of D are unchanged: the traversals of ℐ(D) and the cubes of map (12)(e).
    - In D's production units, map *Arcs* (l.1550) and the connectedness give |H_i(D)| ≤ 4diam(ℐ_i(D)) ≤ 4diam(S_D) ≤ 4Mσ(D) and diam_D ≤ diam(S_D) ≤ Mσ(D). One step later these are at most 4(M/L)σ(D) + 2 and (M/L)σ(D) step bonds, as map (12)(d) has diam_W ≤ (M/L)#_k(W).
  - *Covering.* The covering lemma's part (2) holds piece by piece.
    - Let the supports of v and D meet or touch at x, and let v have an interval on C_i. Then x is within diam_v of int_i(v) and within diam_D of a traversal b ∈ ℐ_i(D).
    - By part (1) the shorter arc between them has length at most 2(diam_v + diam_D + 1). So it lies in win(v), or it starts at b ∈ H_i(D) and lies in win(D).
    - For a member with several pieces on C_i, the gap arc of the pair in the linked hull is, by (3)'s definition, the shorter arc between int_i(v) and the nearest piece of ℐ_i(D). It is no longer than the arc above and starts at the same interval, so it is covered too.
    - If D has no interval on v's loop, the pair has no arc to cover ((iii)).
    - Part (3) runs per loop. So ℐ(𝒢) ⊆ ∪_vwin(v), and map (12)(c)'s bounds of σ_{k+1}(𝒢) and ext_{k+1}(𝒢) by the shares hold.
  - *Positions.* In the D2.6 closure's units (5.2 (T0): n(D) ≤ Mσ(D), diam_D ≤ (M/L)σ(D), σ(D) ≥ 1), |win(D)| ≤ |H₁(D)| + |H₂(D)| + 80(diam_D + 1) ≤ 8Mσ(D) + 80((M/L)σ(D) + 1) ≤ 89Mσ(D).
    - So D has at most 81Mσ(D) + 89Mσ(D) = 170Mσ(D) positions, in place of (T0)'s 163Mσ(D).
    - A traversal of win(D) is within |H_i(D)| + 20(diam_D + 1) ≤ 24((M/L)σ(D) + 1) step bonds of ℐ_i(D). So map (12)(e)'s window sum holds with C_win^× := 4Σ_{d≥0}e^{−c_w(d/24−1)₊} ≤ 196/c_w (four window ends, and the same spare (ν_r/8)σ(D) ≥ (ν_rL/(8M))(d/24 − 1)). C_win^× depends on L, M, and ν only.
    - In (T0)'s condition the window term counts the other vertices whose windows reach a traversal of D, at most n(D)C_win (D2.6 closure 5.2). A two-loop vertex has four window ends, so that constant is at most 4Σ_{d≥0}e^{−c_w(d/20−1)₊} ≤ 4Σ_{d≥0}e^{−c_w(d/24−1)₊} = C_win^×, term by term, and C_win^× serves for it.
    - For these insertions (T0)'s condition becomes (170 + C_win^×)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8, again a smallness of g under (R-m′). *Units with a factor* below gives the condition for the whole class.
  - *The share of D.* E_D = X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D)) is closed-connected ((H2) of *Connectedness*).
    - By *Arc cubes* (map l.1545) it has at most #(X_D) + Σ_i(8|H_i(D)|/M + 80) ≤ 65σ_k(D) + 160 cubes, in place of map (12)(d)'s 8σ_k(W) + 80.
    - D's domain one step later, the π_{k+1}-cubes meeting the 8M-neighbourhood of E_D, has at most 16 + 128N/L cubes for E_D of N cubes, as in map l.1551. Its spanning walk cuts into pieces of at most ⌊L/2⌋ − 1 steps, and the 8M-neighbourhood of a piece has extent at most ⌊L/2⌋·(π_k-side) + 16M fine units < π_{k+1}-side per direction for L ≥ 6, so it meets at most 2⁴ π_{k+1}-cubes. The neighbourhood matters only for the halos of step-1 insertions (*Base*).
    - So #_{k+1}(X_D) ≤ 16 + 20480/L + 8320σ_k(D)/L, and |win(D)| ≤ 88(M/L)σ_k(D) + 84 step-(k+1) bonds.
    - Map (12)(d)'s computation then gives sh(D) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ_k(D) + (ν_e/8 + ν_e/L)ext_k(D) + c_sh^× + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(D)|, with c_sh^× := 2ν_f(16 + 20480/L + 84/M) + 2ν_e(2 + 84/M). It needs (8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4, which holds for L ≥ 4·10⁴, since (8496·17/16 + 11)/(4·10⁴) ≤ 0.226.
    - The transport condition c_πα_{1,k+1}·88M/L ≤ ν_r/16 follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν.
  - *Units with a factor.* Let an insertion D have the units above with uσ(D) in place of σ(D), for a u ≥ 1: in production units n(D) ≤ uMσ(D), diam(S_D) ≤ uMσ(D), and #(X_D) + n(D)/M ≤ uσ(D). The counts above use only these bounds and (H0)–(H2) of *Connectedness*, so they hold with uσ(D) in place of σ(D).
    - D has at most 170uMσ(D) positions.
    - A traversal of win(D) is within 24u((M/L)σ(D) + 1) step bonds of ℐ_i(D), and the spare (ν_r/8)σ(D) is ≥ (ν_rL/(8M))(d/(24u) − 1). So the window sum holds with C_win^× ≤ 196u/c_w.
    - The share is within (ν_r/4)σ_k(D) + c_sh^× + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(D)| when u(8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4. The transport condition u·c_πα_{1,k+1}·88M/L ≤ ν_r/16 still follows from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν, since 1408u ≤ 4096L.
    - The factors: the step-1 insertions have u ≤ u₁ = 1 + 4/M + 48/L (*Base*), the ones made at the T-steps after step 1 have u = 1 (*Connectedness*), the single-loop ones made at the R-steps have u ≤ 21/10, or u = 16 when a slot holds a carried insertion ((6C)), the R-made cross insertions and their untouched continuations have u = 6 ((6B), *The count*, *The units of the members*), and the merged insertions of (R-member) have u = 19. An untouched insertion keeps its production σ and factor; (6B)'s *The units of the members* bounds its domain at every later step by (21/20)σ + 17, whatever the factor, so no factor grows along a lineage.
    - An R-made cross insertion has E_D = X_D ∪ (the π_k-cubes meeting H₁(D) ∪ H₂(D)) with X_D = hull_c(g) (*Connectedness*), and the units with u = 6. So it has at most 390σ_g + 160 cubes, D's domain one step later has at most 16 + 20480/L + 49920σ_g/L, and the share needs 6(8496ν_f + 88ν_e)/L ≤ ν_r/4, which holds for L ≥ 2.5·10⁵, since 6(8496·17/16 + 11)/(2.5·10⁵) ≤ 0.217.
    - So (T0)'s condition for these insertions is (2720 + 59584/c_w)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8, a smallness of g under (R-m′): 170·16 positions, and the window term n(D)C_win with n(D) ≤ 16Mσ(D) and C_win ≤ 196·19/c_w, since the other vertices whose windows reach D have factors up to 19, the merged insertions of (R-member) included. (R-member)'s condition at u = 19, (3230 + 70756/c_w)M(…) ≤ ν_r/8, implies it. The shares hold for L ≥ L₀^× := 10⁶, since 16(8496·17/16 + 11)/L₀^× ≤ 0.145, the R-made cross ones need 6(8496·17/16 + 11)/L₀^× ≤ 0.055, and the merged insertions of (R-member), at u = 19, need (219024·17/16 + 209)/L₀^× ≤ 0.233.
  - *M-vertices.* An M-vertex has an interval on each loop where it has slots or touches an insertion with an interval.
    - *The enlargement.* D2.2(4)(e) enlarges I(Y) at each end to the end of any old interval that it cuts (map l.676). For the pair an old interval is a piece of an old insertion's arc set on one loop, not its filled hull. The enlargement adds nothing. A cut piece J would contain an end bond b of the smallest arc containing S′(Y). The touched pieces lie inside that arc, and the pieces are pairwise disjoint, so b is a bond of C_i in Y. J's owner W has X^{Cov}_W ⊇ ℐ(W) ⊇ J (Cov(k), map l.622; the touched relation of map l.676 is read with this domain, which at step 1 lies in S_W, *Base*), so X^{Cov}_W meets Y, W is touched, and J lies inside the arc. The counts below use #(X_W), the constituents' count, and S_W ⊇ X^{Cov}_W, so they are unchanged.
    - Map (12)(d)'s count holds per loop with diam_ℓ∞(S_W) in place of diam_W + |int(W)|: diam_ℓ∞(S_M^{(i)}) ≤ M#(M) + 2max_Wdiam_ℓ∞(S_W), since a touched W's interval on C_i reaches S_M^{(i)} through S_W, and n^{(i)}_{k+1}(M) ≤ 4(5diam_ℓ∞(S_M^{(i)}) + 2max_W(piece of ℐ_i(W))) ≤ 20M#(M) + 48(M/L)Σ_Wu_Wσ_k(W), over the touched W with an interval on C_i. A piece has at most n_k(W)/L ≤ u_W(M/L)σ_k(W) step bonds, and the map's allowance for the enlargement is kept as slack.
    - So n_{k+1}(M) ≤ 40M#(M) + 96(M/L)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}u_Wσ_k(W) and |win_M| := n_{k+1}(M) + 80(M#(M) + 1), the M-part of the window bound in *Vertices on two loops*.
    - Map (12)(d)'s computation of sh(M), with #(X_M) ≤ #(M) + 8n_{k+1}(M)/M + 161 (arc cubes on two loops) and ext_M bounded by the same plus the touched boxes, gives
      sh(M) ≤ ν_f(282#(M) + 22) + ν_e(441#(M) + 164) + (3ν_f + 9ν_e)(96/L)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}u_Wσ_k(W) + ν_eΣ_{W∈𝔅(M)}(ext_k(W)/L + 1) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win_M|
      for M ≥ 100, in place of the map's ν_f(142#(M) + 11) + ν_e(221#(M) + 84) and 48/L.
    - A cube of M∖D′ then costs log 64 + log 2 + 2 + 1/8 + 24c + 283ν_f + 441ν_e ≤ 7.6 for ν ≤ 1/1000 and the vacuum constant c ≤ 1/100 (map l.1525), against r/648 ≥ 8. The bracket of a component cube stays within κ_s^{(12)}'s constant 9.
    - The constant part, 22ν_f + 164ν_e ≤ 0.045 for ν ≤ 1/1000, is charged to one π_k-cube of the components (#(G_M) ≥ 1). That cube's cost then has log 4 + 1 + 1/8 + 0.045 ≤ 2.6 outside the bracket, within the constant 4 of κ_s^{(12)}/4.
    - The window sums of a two-loop vertex have four ends, with C_win^×'s constant (*Positions*).
    - The part passed to a touched W is (3ν_f + 9ν_e)(96u_W/L)σ_k(W) + (ν_r/64)σ_k(W) ≤ (ν_r/16)σ_k(W) when 414u_Wν/L ≤ 3ν_r/64, that is L ≥ 8832u_W, which L₀^× covers at u_W ≤ 16. This stays within the reserve budget of an old W (map l.1555).
    - The transport conditions c_πα_{1,k+1}·120M ≤ ν_f and u_Wc_πα_{1,k+1}·96M/L ≤ ν_r/64 follow from c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν, since 96·16·64 ≤ 4096L.
    - The per-piece constants 80c_π (the map's 40c_π) and 84c_π of the transports, ℓ_X's 16|win|/M + 320 for two window arcs (the map's + 160, l.1554), and map (12)(f)'s n⁴ arc choices for two arcs (the map's n²) change C_pc by a fixed factor, which the smallness of g absorbs.
- (ii) *Long groups.* At k ≥ 1 a long arc is a union of linked hulls with shorter gap arcs, and no hull is replaced by C_i ((3)). The covering lemma of (12)(c) is used for pairs of vertices with intervals on one loop, and its part (1) is that loop's square geometry. So a long arc is covered like any arc, and (S-w) and its counts hold for groups long on one or both loops, as for the single loop's whole-contour groups (map l.1039, l.1086). Long-ness enters only (4)–(5), where (T′) pays it. At step 1 V4 replaces a long hull by I_{C_i} and bounds the long groups by their total mass (Lemma lem:two-loop-pins(c)–(d)); D^×(1) takes this as V4 states it.
- (iii) *The linked hull.* As in (3): the covering lemma's part (2) is applied to the joined pairs with intervals on one loop. A joined pair on different loops is linked by (ii) or (iii) of (12)(c), and it has no arc to cover.
- (iv) *Base points.* Each loop's traversals are numbered cyclically from its own base point (V4 L3560; the base point of map l.1163, per loop). The traversals of C are ordered as those of C₁, then those of C₂. "The first traversal" of an arc set refers to this order.
- (v) *The reference gas.* Its species are C₁- and C₂-groups only (cross activity 0), so their positions and networks lie on one loop, and map l.1144's network argument for (T′) transfers.
  - Its Kotecký–Preiss condition is the c-version of the pair's (S-w) (map (12)(f) and (13)(b)): the positions of 𝒢, at most 81Mσ, times C_S′ρ·pl, are at most ν/(64·81M).
  - The pair enters through the per-position counts of (12)(e)–(f): the traversals through a cube, the arc choices, and C_M. Each of them grows by a factor fixed before g when a position meets two loops. So the condition holds with the pair's C_S′, and S°(ℓ) ≠ 0 and (T′) follow as for one loop.
- (vi) *The range r₀.* r₀ := 2 ((1)).
- *D2.5.* Covariance is a property of each insertion at the ends of each interval of its arc set.
  - For an insertion with intervals I ∈ ℐ(W), Cov(k) (map l.622) is read with one factor per interval: Y_W(𝐔^u, …) = (⊗_Iπ_{i(I)}(u(x_I)))Y_W(𝐔, …)(⊗_Iπ_{i(I)}(u(x′_I)))^{−1}, with x_I, x′_I the ends of I and i(I) its loop.
  - The composition argument (map (4)(c)–(e), l.634, l.718) acts factor by factor, and the pair's word is a normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of two closed products. D2.5's proof is per insertion and per end, so it holds with two loops.
- *D2.6.* The attached species, their carry, and their payment are per large-field component and per position.
  - (T3) uses the square geometry of the loop that carries the gap arc, and gap arcs are per loop ((iii)).
  - A fixed member D″ with several intervals adds at most 12uσ(D″)/L π_{n+1}-sides to a group's reach, u ≤ 16 its factor of (i)'s *Units with a factor*, in place of 2σ(D″)/L (5.5, *The bridge of a merged W*). The reach is a crossing of S_{D″} ∪ H₁(D″) ∪ H₂(D″), whose diameter is at most 5diam(S_{D″}) and one cube side (each point of H_i lies within 2diam(ℐ_i) of ℐ_i, map *Arcs*), and a gap reached around one H_i(D″), of length at most 4diam(S_{D″}). The charge is 12uν_e/L ≤ ν_r/16 for L ≥ 384, since ν_e ≤ ν_r/8, inside D″'s merge-extent charge.
  - 5.5's share of a fixed member, sh(W) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ(W) + c_W, holds for the members with several intervals by (i), with c_sh^×, for L ≥ L₀^×. Their positions are (i)'s 170uMσ, with u ≤ 16.
  - §7.2's payment is per component. The contour-density constant c_C of §7.1–7.2 at most doubles, since a position meets the traversals of at most two loops.
  - So the D2.6 closure holds for C₁ ⊔ C₂. Attached cross groups need no flat value, since attached insertions carry no deviation bound (5.1).
- *B^×_LF.* B^×_LF is the continuum note's large-field payment (4.5) for C₁ ⊔ C₂, with the words' bound taken without the cross insertions: B^×_LF := sup_{ℓ≠ℓ₀}sup|e^{𝓔^×_{K′}(ℓ)}|·Π_{W not cross}(1 + e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖)/(κ^{(K′)}_{C₁}κ^{(K′)}_{C₂}), on the terms where the payment is made, with e^{E^×_const,K′} and e^{E^∂_{K′}(ℓ)} in e^{𝓔^×_{K′}(ℓ)}. The cross insertions enter (8.2) through e^{A^×} ((7)).
  - The far-component decay of (4.4) uses the 4(ℓ₁+ℓ₂) traversals of the pair in place of 4ℓ_C.
  - (4.5) is derived given D2.7 (continuum note l.62) and rests on the D2.6 closure §7. So B^×_LF rests on (6B) as well as on §7 for the pair.
- *D2.7.* (6B). Items 2 (the class and its exclusions do not involve the observable) and 12 (D2.0(3)(b) at k > N_B, constants only) transfer verbatim.

### (6B) The R-Steps for the Pair (Derived, except (R-eval))

The R-operation is the identity at the steps k ≤ N_B (D2.7 item 1), with N_B Bałaban's N, written N_B here to keep it apart from the step index. Fix k > N_B and a term of LFR II (1.72), in the notation of D2.7 15.0 with C := C₁ ⊔ C₂, and put m := K′ − k.
- *Item 3.* R′'s integral identity holds for every integrable integrand. The gauge-fixing equalities before it need the word to be invariant under the multi-scale gauge transformations. The pair's word is a normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of two closed products of link variables and insertions, and each insertion is covariant at the ends of each interval of its arc set ((6A), D2.5). So it is invariant, and ∫dV_k(R′N^×_k) = ∫dV_kN^×_k. Derived.
- *15.0, 15.2, and 15.3.* C ∩ X⁺ is a union of arcs on one or both loops, and the arc words are averaged per component as in 15.0. 15.2's covariance is per arc. 15.3's decomposition of the configurations does not use the observable, so Ξ^C = Σ_𝔉Ξ_{¬C}(𝔉)A^C(𝔉) with A^C(𝔉) the normalised trace on V_{π₁} ⊗ V_{π₂} of the two path-ordered products. Derived as identities, with 15.3's bounds.
- *15.5.* The Möbius expansion is per bond and per insertion. So it holds for the words of both loops, and for the cross insertions, which are functions of U^{old} on their domains as the slot insertions are.
  - The sums of 15.5(ii) are run at the rate δ/4 in place of δ. Then D2.7 15.5(ii)'s sum over the attachment bonds gives a_k(X) ≤ 16(100MR_k + 2Mw_k + 8Mδ^{−1})δ^{−1}e^{−δw_k/4}, still a polylog of order r, and the factor e^{−(3δ/4)ℓ(i,𝒦)} of each attached insertion is left for the lengths below. The carried insertions take the same expansion in their U-argument (D2.7 15.5(i)), about their home h(D): the first-class components whose X⁺ meets D's dependence set Dep(D) := X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D, on which D is a function of U^{old} (Dep(D) ⊆ T_D, and Dep(D) = X_D when j < k, *The units of the members*), with Z′ for an att(j, Z′) or lw insertion of a first-class Z′. An insertion whose interval meets an arc of C ∩ X⁺ has X ∈ h(D), since X^{Cov}_D contains ℐ(D) (l.508; map l.622). The components of h(D) keep their old data in every configuration, as h(i) does for a bond, and 𝒦 runs over the other first-class components. The parts D^{𝒦} := Σ_{𝒥⊆𝒦}(−1)^{|𝒦∖𝒥|}D(U^{𝒥∪h(D)}) sum to D(U^{old}) exactly; D^{∅} is joined to the polymers of h(D) (none when h(D) = ∅: D is then untouched), and D^{𝒦} to those of h(D) and of the components in 𝒦. They obey |D^{𝒦}| ≤ ‖D‖C_α^{|𝒦|}e^{−δℓ(D,𝒦)}, with ℓ(D,𝒦) the scaled length of the shortest tree connecting Dep(D) and the components of 𝒦, C_α the O(1) of (α), and ‖D‖ D's bound at the configurations U^{𝒥∪h(D)}, 𝒥 ⊆ 𝒦 (l.619). This is the clause (α′) of R-far″ (D2.7 15.5(ii)): Bałaban's factor exp(−δd((Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ ∩ Y_i′, X)) (p.376 l.10–13) is the decay of a term localised in a domain X in the scaled distance from X, and (α) is its case of a single bond. Each X ∈ 𝒦 has X⁺ ∩ Dep(D) = ∅, and X⁺ contains the MR_k-neighbourhood of X (*Lengths*), so ℓ(D,𝒦) ≥ MR_k for 𝒦 ≠ ∅. The parts' sums run at the rate δ/4, which leaves e^{−(3δ/4)ℓ(D,𝒦)} for the lengths below, and their total over 𝒦 ≠ ∅ is a factor of D's own, η(D) := Σ_{𝒦≠∅}C_α^{|𝒦|}e^{−(δ/4)ℓ(D,𝒦)} ≤ c_rσ(D)e^{−δMR_k/8}, c_r := 18.05·3^d(3^d + 2^dM), paid from D's weight (*The rates*, *The rooted sum*). Proof: the MR_k-cubes meeting the shortest tree of 𝒦 form a ∗-connected set S of m ≤ 2^{d+1}(ℓ/(MR_k) + 1) ≤ 2^{d+2}ℓ/(MR_k) cubes (*Cubes*' argument, with cubes of side MR_k, and ℓ ≥ MR_k), one of which meets Dep(D). The components are unions of MR_k-cubes (LFR I p.177 l.17–19), so each X ∈ 𝒦 contains a cube of S, distinct components distinct cubes, and the 𝒦 with a given S contribute at most (1 + C_α)^m. Half of the rate δ/4 gives e^{−(δ/8)ℓ} ≤ e^{−δMR_km/2^{d+5}}, the other half e^{−δMR_k/8}. The ∗-connected S of m cubes containing a given one number at most (e3^d)^m, and the given cube is one of at most c_rσ(D). Dep(D) ⊆ X_D ∪ ℐ(D) (*The units of the members*), so a cube meeting Dep(D) meets X_D ∪ ℐ(D). R_k is a power of L, so each π_k-cube of X_D lies in one MR_k-cube, and a closed MR_k-cube meeting X_D is that cube or a neighbour; a bond lies in at most 2^d closed cubes. So at most 3^d#_k(X_D) + 2^dn_k(D) ≤ 3^d(3^d#_k(X_D) + 2^dn_k(D)) ≤ 3^d(3^d + 2^dM)(#_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M) ≤ c_rσ(D) cubes meet Dep(D), by #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(D) + 17 ≤ 18.05σ(D) (*The units of the members*, σ(D) ≥ 1). With x := e3^d(1 + C_α)e^{−δMR_k/2^{d+5}} ≤ ½, a smallness of g_k, η(D) ≤ c_rσ(D)e^{−δMR_k/8}Σ_{m≥1}x^m ≤ c_rσ(D)e^{−δMR_k/8}.
  - The attachment cost a_k(X) counts the bonds of C near a component, per straight side. C₁ ⊔ C₂ has eight sides where C has four, so a_k at most doubles. 15.3's amplitude c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} is still ≤ e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} for small g_k.
  - R-far″ is inherited, with the pieces' domains Y′ of (ii)(β) in 𝐃_k, the connected unions of π_k-cubes (RG I p.257 l.7–11). This is where Bałaban's domains lie: Y_1 is "the smallest localization domain Y_1 ∈ 𝐃_k" containing the term's domain (p.373 l.15), and each component of Y_2′, Y_3′, and Y_4′ belongs to 𝐃_k (p.373 l.33, p.374 l.10, p.374 l.28).
  - This amends D2.7 15.5(ii)(β), whose text has "a connected union of MR_k-cubes". The volume factor is per π_k-cube under either class. The shapes of the Y′ in 𝐃_k are summed in the resummation over Y′, which Bałaban runs over his Y_i′ ∈ 𝐃_k (p.376 l.39), at the rate of *The free decay* below. The sizes that 15.4–15.7 use count MR_k-cubes, at most the number of π_k-cubes, so the finer class adds nothing to those sums.
  - For a slot holding a carried insertion D, of any species, the slot's bonds are read as those of D's domain X_D, the union of the π_k-cubes of its constituents' domains (map l.310's #_k). So the base Y′₀ (the cubes of the slot's bonds, its components, and its tree, D2.7 15.5(ii)) contains X_D and, D being a function of U^{old} on its dependence set, Dep(D) = X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D (*15.5* above), and R-far″ claims no decay across Dep(D). This is the reading of 15.5(i), which joins D to the polymer. *The count* counts X_D by σ(D) for a big member, and for a small one in #π_k(B_P) or in half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins it to P (*Big and small members*). Dep(D) ∖ X_D is empty when D was made before step k and lies in ℐ(D) when D was made at the T-step into step k (*The units of the members*). The π_k-cubes meeting it, the set Q(D), are cubes of Y′₀; they lie in T_D, which bounds their diameter, and they are left out of the counted domains, since each meets ℐ(D), whose bonds are counted as traversals (*Lengths*). The reading is for Y′₀ and the polymer's domain only: in the arc set ℐ(g) of *The arc set* below, a slot contributes the bonds of its tensor, its arcs of C ∩ ∪_{X∈P}X⁺ and the intervals of its carried insertions outside every X⁺ (*The slots of a cross member*).
  - A carried insertion of a single-loop species in a slot is part of that slot's word, bounded inside e^{2ΣA} as for one loop (15.1, 15.3, 15.8(b)). The cross insertions are not: on each term of (6)'s multilinear expansion they are fixed factors of the words, with their own norms.
  - A cross member W is evaluated where 15.5(i) evaluates every slot insertion: on the integration domain of 𝐓′_k(X), and at the configurations U^{𝒥∪h(W)}, 𝒥 ⊆ 𝒦, for its parts with 𝒦 ≠ ∅ (*15.5* above). 15.5(i) bounds a slot insertion there by its D(k) mass, and (6B) uses the same bound for W, ‖W‖ with its weight. That these bounds hold there, for W and for the single-loop slot insertions alike, is (R-eval) with (R-eval-cr), derived and not reviewed: W's weight and the D(k) masses are sup bounds on 𝒟^λ_k ((6), the cross clause; *The dilated sub-domains*). The D2.6 closure 5.1 places the T-steps' evaluation points in 𝒟^{sub}_n, which (b3)–(b4) extend to the family, and (6C) places the R-step's integration over the large-field old variables, at U_k^0, in 𝒟_k only.
- *The groups of 15.4.* For the pair, a span is taken only for a polymer whose slots lie on one loop and include no carried cross insertion: its span of 15.4 on that loop. A polymer with slots on both loops, or with a cross insertion among its slots, has no span. Two polymers are in one group when a bond of a slot of one lies in the span of the other.
  - For two polymers with spans this is 15.4's interleaving, so the groups all of whose polymers have spans are 15.4's groups, per loop.
  - Such a group's span is span(g) := ∪_{P∈g}span(P), as in 15.4. The spans of its polymers are chained by the links, so it is an arc of C_i or C_i itself. Its ends are slots, and no slot of another group lies in it, since a slot in span(P) joins its polymer to P. 15.1's Schur argument and 15.6(a)–(c) use only these properties, so 15.1's joint average applies to g.
  - A polymer without a span needs none: its tensor is contracted on its slots' bonds only (*The arc set* below). A cross insertion's arcs on one loop can be far apart, joined through the other loop, and the shortest arc containing them would hold the slots of unrelated polymers.
  - A link through a span joins two polymers with slots on one loop. So a group with slots on both loops contains a polymer whose slots lie on both loops: its domain meets both loops, or one of its slots is a carried cross insertion. Call such a group a cross group of the R-gas.
- *15.1 and 15.4(i).* For a group with slots on C_i only, the contraction over span(g) ⊂ C_i is covariant at its two ends, and Schur's lemma for π_i gives the scalar c_g (15.1, per loop). For a cross group of the R-gas, the average at the flat reference commutes with π₁(u) ⊗ π₂(u), and that is all. Put c_g := 0, as 15.4 does for the groups meeting a second-class component.
- *Lengths.* Let P be a polymer of 15.3, X′_1, …, X′_s the localisation domains of (1.90) in P, a its attached bond insertions Y_i^{𝒦}, with the scaled lengths ℓ_a := ℓ(i,𝒦) of 15.5(ii)(α) of their trees, a′ the parts D^{𝒦}, 𝒦 ≠ ∅, of the carried insertions D in P's slots (the members of *The count*) with ℓ_{a′} := ℓ(D,𝒦), and Y′ its pieces' domains. Every part has ℓ_{a′} ≥ MR_k, since the components of 𝒦 lie outside D's home h(D), so their sets X⁺, which contain their MR_k-neighbourhoods (below), miss Dep(D) ⊇ X_D (*15.5* above). Put
  D_P := Σ_r(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92) + Σ_a(ℓ_a/M + 3) + Σ_{a′}(3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M + 2Σ_{Y′}#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀),
  with d_k the linear size in M-units (RG I p.257 l.14–16; it equals LFR II's d_{k,Z} of (1.67) when X′_r ∩ ∪_iY_i = ∅, the case of *The free decay*) and #π_k the number of π_k-cubes, of side M in step-k units. In (6A) and (6B) a π_k-cube is a block of the partition π_k of the step-k lattice's sites, and it meets a set of sites and bonds when it contains one of the set's sites (for a bond, one of its two end sites). So the π_k-cubes meeting a union of π_k-cubes are its own cubes, a bond meets at most two π_k-cubes, and a lattice path of at most M steps at most 2^d. Diameters and closed-connectedness are those of the cubes' closed hulls. The MR_k-cubes of *15.5* are closed continuum cubes and are named as such there.
  - dom P, the union of the X′_r, the sets X⁺ of P's first-class components, the trees of the attached insertions, the Y′, and the sets Dep(D) ⊇ X_D of the carried insertions D in P's slots (the trees end on Dep(D), and Y′₀ ⊇ Dep(D), *15.5*), is connected. Let 𝔅(P) be the set of these D. Its counted domain dom_c P is the set of the π_k-cubes meeting dom P, other than the cubes of the sets Q(D), D ∈ 𝔅(P), of *The units of the members*. Each cube of a Q(D) meets ℐ(D), so every π_k-cube meeting dom P is in dom_c P or meets ∪_{D∈𝔅(P)}ℐ(D). For the diameter, Dep(D) ∪ Q(D) ⊆ T_D (*The units of the members*). Then diam_∞(dom P) ≤ MD_P + Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}diam_∞(T_D) in step-k units, with diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M(21/20)σ(D) for every big member, and the small members' T_D inside the sets X^{(44)} or, for the others, inside D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them (*The count*, *Big and small members*). When 𝔅^{big}(P) = ∅, diam_∞(dom P) ≤ MD_P. A connected union of finitely many closed pieces has diameter at most the sum of theirs, connected or not, and the bullets below bound the pieces.
  - A tree meeting every M-cube of X′_r gives diam_∞(X′_r) ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 2).
  - The π_k-cubes of Y′₀ stick out by at most M. The cubes covering a tree of length ℓ have ℓ^∞-diameter at most ℓ + 2M, and a slot bond on ∂X⁺ lies in a cube outside X⁺. The constants 4R_k + 92 and ℓ_a/M + 3, and the half length ½ℓ_{a′}/M ≥ 23 (*The count*), include this.
  - Each first-class component X of P lies in one X′_r, since X′_0 = ∪_{Y∈𝐃}Y ∪ ∪_jX_j and the X′_p are unions of the components of X′_0∖∪_iY_i (p.387 l.42, p.388 l.2–3).
  - X⁺ widens X by two layers of MR_k-cubes: X^∼ adds one, since ∼ means "adding one layer of cubes" of the family that defines the set (CRE p.245 l.40–43), and 15.0 adds one more. So diam_∞(X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X⁺) ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 2), and ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 4) with the cubes of Y′₀. X^{(44)} widens X⁺ by 44 layers of π_k-cubes, so diam_∞(X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X^{(44)}) ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92), and this set contains the sets T_D of the small members whose T_D meets such an X⁺ (*The units of the members*).
  - The scaled length is at least the length in step-k units, since (L^jη)^{−1} ≥ (L^kη)^{−1} for j ≤ k (PRT2 (2.46)).
  - Every cube of Y′∖Y′₀ is joined to Y′₀ by a chain of at most #π_k(Y′∖Y′₀) cubes.
- *The free decay.* Put c_F := min(1, δM/2). For a polymer P that meets no second-class or unchanged component, each part of D_P has a factor that the uses of Bałaban's bounds leave free, at least c_F per unit:
  - d_k(X′_r): the activity carries e^{−(1+β/4)κd_k(P∖fixed)} (D2.7 15.3(b)), and D2.7's gases use e^{−κ(size)} of it, the size counted in MR_k-cubes (15.4(i), 15.7). A tree of length Md_k meets at most 2^{d+1}(d_k/R_k + 1) MR_k-cubes (*Cubes*' argument, with cubes of side MR_k), so the size of X′_r is at most d_k(X′_r) + 2^{d+1} for R_k ≥ 2^{d+1}, and e^{−κd_k} ≤ e^{2^{d+1}κ}e^{−κ(size)}. The rest, e^{−(β/4)κd_k}, is (β/4)κ ≥ 3·2^{d−1} ≥ 1 per M-unit, since 1 ≤ βκ/(6·2^d) (p.389 l.12).
  - ℓ_a/M and (3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M: the factor e^{−(3δ/4)ℓ} left by 15.5 at the rate δ/4 (by (α) for a bond insertion and by (α′) for a part D^{𝒦}), which is 3δM/4 per M-unit of ℓ, so at least c_F per unit of ℓ_a/M and, since (3/2)c_F ≤ 3δM/4, of (3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M.
  - #π_k(Y′∖Y′₀): the pieces carry e^{−(κ₁−1)} per π_k-cube of Y′∖Y′₀ (15.5(ii)(β)), and the budget per cube is as follows.
    - Bałaban extracts the tree decay of the enlarged domain from half of his volume factor (p.376 l.34–35), and of the other half he writes "This we use now to control the resummations" (l.37–39).
    - D2.7 spends the pieces' factor on the resummation over Y′, at Bałaban's rate ½(κ₁−1), and on the pieces' part of the size decay e^{−κ(size)}: 15.3–15.4's Kotecký–Preiss weights, 15.4(i)'s group entropy, and 15.7's diameter. It extracts no tree decay over Y′. Since the size counts MR_k-cubes, at most the π_k-cubes, that part is at most κ per π_k-cube.
    - RG II assumes ½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ (p.20 l.17). So ½(κ₁−1) − κ ≥ (2L−1)κ ≥ 2 ≥ 2c_F per cube, that is c_F per unit of D_P.
  - κ and κ₁ here are RG II's. LFR II's κ₁ is that of its localisation expansions (6.10) [I], which are RG II's (1.10), with the parameters |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁}, and "κ₁ is a sufficiently big positive number" (RG II p.5 l.6–8). The identification of [I] with RG II is by CRE's (I.6.34) and (I.6.41) (p.276 l.23–28), which are RG II's analyticity space (1.34) and expansion (1.41).
  - κ > 6·2^d. By p.389, the sixth line below (1.94), κ ≥ 6·2^d/β, and β < 1: p.376 l.5–6 bounds exp(−(1−β)κ(L^jη)^{−1}d_k(Y₀)) by exp(−(1+3β)κd_k(Y₀)), which fails for β ≥ 1 when d_k(Y₀) > 0.
  - The constants 4R_k + 92 and e^{2^{d+1}κ} per X′_r: the exponents of e^{c_F(4R_k+92)} and e^{2^{d+1}κ} are constants or polylogs of order r < p₀, so these factors are paid by the margin of (1.89) (*The volume*). Each X′_r has a first-class component to pay them: a localisation domain disjoint from ∪_iY_i has q ≥ 1 in p.389's sum (the three lines after (1.95)).
  - The constant 3 per attached bond insertion is not a per-component constant. 15.5(ii)'s sum over the attachment bonds of a component is a_k(X), so the factor e^{3c_F} of each insertion replaces a_k(X) by e^{3c_F}a_k(X) ≤ e³a_k(X). This is still a polylog of order r, and 15.3's amplitude bound c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} ≤ e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} stands for small g_k.
  So 15.3's bounds and Kotecký–Preiss sums hold for the activities multiplied by e^{c_FD_P}, with the amplitude e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} for small g_k.
- *The volume.* (1.95)'s volume factor is βκ/(6·2^d) per π_k-cube of X′∖∪_iY_i, the Y_i the second-class components. X′_r is disjoint from them: P meets none, and a localisation domain X′ either contains a Y_i or is disjoint from ∪_iY_i (p.389, the three lines after (1.95)). (1.96) uses e^{−p₀(g_k)/2}100^dR_k^{−d} of it per cube (p.389, (1.96) and the three lines after it). The rest, a_V ≥ βκ/(12·2^d) ≥ ½ for small g_k, is unused, since (1.97) drops it ("the above bound is cancelled by the last exponential in (1.95)", the second line above (1.97)). The sets X⁺ are paid by the components instead.
  - *The factor of (1.89) per component.* Each first-class component carries e^{−2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_k)+1}. With its volume term e^{2κ(100R_k)^d} (p.389, the eighth line below (1.94)) this is ≤ e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_k)} (the display after "g_k is so small that").
    - (1.96)'s sum over the components uses e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} of it, as Bałaban's ((1.96)).
    - The rest, c₁ = e^{−p₀(g_k)}, is the component's factor in 15.3(b), and the component's loop factor e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k} is counted with it, once. 15.3's amplitude takes e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} of c₁e^{3+2ΣA+2a_k}, and 15.4(iii)'s rooted bound m^R_k is that amplitude.
    - The margin, e^{−p₀(g_k)/2+3+2ΣA+2a_k}, has −p₀(g_k)/2 plus polylogs of order r < p₀ in its exponent. (6B) takes from it e^{5p₀(g_k)/64} for X^{(44)} at the rate (5/2)ν_r (*The volume*) and the constants of *The free decay*, and the rest is ≤ 1 for small g_k.
    - 15.6(e)'s att charge is paid in D26 7.2's reserve Res(Z), not from (1.89).
  - A first-class component X lies in a cube of side 100MR_k (LFR I p.177 (i)), and X⁺ widens X by two layers of MR_k-cubes (*Lengths*). X^{(44)} adds 44 layers of π_k-cubes (*The count*). So #π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ (104R_k + 88)^d ≤ (105R_k)^d, for R_k ≥ 88.
  - p.389's assumption gives 2κ(100R_k)^d < p₀(g_k)/2. With 8(1.05)^dν_r ≤ κ (κ > 6·2^d, *The free decay*), 2ν_r#π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ p₀(g_k)/16. At the rate (5/2)ν_r of *The payment* and (6C) the cost is at most 5p₀(g_k)/64, which the margin e^{−p₀(g_k)/2 + (polylogs of order r)} pays for small g_k.
- *The cross constant of the step.* Let Ξ^c_{12}, Ξ^c_1, and Ξ^c_2 be 15.4's group gases of C₁ ⊔ C₂, C₁, and C₂ on the all-small-field term, and Ξ the gas without the loop. Put
  E^{R×}_k := E_k^R(C₁ ⊔ C₂;ℓ₀) − E_k^R(C₁;ℓ₀) − E_k^R(C₂;ℓ₀) = log Ξ^c_{12} − log Ξ^c_1 − log Ξ^c_2 + log Ξ.
  - These gases are 15.4's series at the flat reference: c_g and F(P)|_flat depend only on the shapes and relative positions of the polymers and of C's bonds (15.4(i)). No carried insertion enters them, so the domains that 15.5(i) joins do not either. Here a polymer meets C_i when its domain does, with dom P containing the X⁺ of its components and its trees.
  - (a) A cluster with no polymer meeting C₂ has the same polymers, activities, c_g, and compatibility in Ξ^c_{12} as in Ξ^c_1, and in Ξ^c_2 as in Ξ. (b) Among the rest, a cluster with no polymer meeting C₁ is the same in Ξ^c_{12} as in Ξ^c_2, and in Ξ^c_1 as in Ξ. So these clusters cancel in pairs, and E^{R×}_k is the signed sum, over the four gases, of the clusters with a polymer meeting C₁ and a polymer meeting C₂.
  - The rest contain a polymer meeting C₁ and one meeting C₂. Their objects are linked by overlaps and by interleaving, and an interleaving link joins two polymers with slots on one loop. So a chain of overlapping domains joins a domain meeting C₁ to one meeting C₂, and Σ_PD_P ≥ DL^m/M over the cluster's polymers ((1), *Lengths*; no polymer of these gases has a cross member, since c_g = 0 removes the cross groups).
  - With *The free decay*, the clusters of each gas through a π_k-cube with Σ_PD_P ≥ s sum to ≤ C_KPe^{−p₀(g_k)/2}e^{−c_Fs/2}, with 15.3's amplitude; the other half of c_F pays the interleaving entropy (*The rooted sum* below). At most 2^d·4ℓ_iL^m π_k-cubes meet C_i, and four gases enter. Hence
    |E^{R×}_k| ≤ C^R(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)L^me^{−p₀(g_k)/2}e^{−c_FDL^m/(2M)}, C^R := 2^{d+4}C_KP.
  - The rest of 15.4 holds per loop, with μ^R_k and ζ^R_k computed for each character: E_k^R(C₁ ⊔ C₂;ℓ₀) = Σ_i(P_{k−1}(C_i)μ^R_k(χ_i) + 4ζ^R_k(χ_i) + E°^R_k(C_i)) + E^{R×}_k. So the R-step's factor of κ factorises, and E^{R×}_k joins E^×_const,k.
  - The term-dependent part E^{R,∂}_k(ℓ) is charged per MR_k-cube to its component, with 15.4(iii)'s rooted bound m^R_k, as for one loop.
- *The sum over the R-steps.* p₀(g) decreases in g, so e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} ≤ e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2} with ḡ_R := max_{N_B<k≤K′}g_k. By (7)'s sum lemma at p = 1 and a := c_FD/(2M),
  Σ_{N_B<k≤K′}|E^{R×}_k| ≤ C^{R×}_E := C^R(ℓ₁+ℓ₂)e^{−p₀(ḡ_R)/2}e^{−c_FD/(2M)}(1 + 12M/(c_FD)).
  It is bounded uniformly in K, it decays in D, and it is small when ḡ_R is.
- *15.6–15.7 for the groups on one loop.* As written, per loop: D_g is a dev insertion, contracted by D2.0(5)(c)'s recursion.
- *15.6 for the cross groups of the R-gas.* Since c_g = 0, every cross group of a family is in the marked set, with D_g^× := its whole contraction.
  - *The arc set.* D_g^× is the tensor of g's slots, contracted in loop order between adjacent slots, on ℐ(g) := the union of the slots' bonds. It has one pair of indices per interval of ℐ(g), and the words at U^R on the gaps of ℐ(g) stay in the loop's word. By the rule of *The groups of 15.4*, no bond of a slot of g lies in the span of another group, and distinct groups have distinct slots. So the spans of the other groups and the slots' bonds of the cross groups are disjoint, and 15.6's expansion over the marked sets runs with ℐ(g) in place of span(g). A cross member's intervals outside every X⁺ can lie in the span of a group on one loop; (R-member) merges such groups.
  - *The slots of a cross member.* Let a cross member W lie in a polymer P. Its slot is the contraction, in loop order, of W with all the arc pieces of the arcs of C ∩ ∪_{X∈P}X⁺ that contain intervals of W, and W's intervals outside every X⁺ stay open.
    - A single product (arc piece)·W·(arc piece) does not serve. W is covariant at the ends of each of its intervals ((6A), D2.5), so an interval of W with an end at a fixed site of another component of P is not telescoped by it, and two intervals of W on one arc need the arc piece between them contracted into W's index pairs.
    - Every interval of W inside a first-class X′⁺ lies in a component of P. The arc of C ∩ X′⁺ that contains it is a slot of the polymer of X′, whose base Y′₀ then contains X_W (*15.5*). So that polymer overlaps P and is P, since the polymers of a family are disjoint.
    - The pieces are not arc words of 15.2 on their own: an end of an interval of W inside X⁺ can be a site where 𝐓′_k(X) fixes a gauge, and 𝒯_X acts on the whole slot, since W depends on X's integration variables through U^{old}.
    - The slot is invariant under every transformation supported at the fixed sites of the components of P: each end of an interval of W inside some X⁺ is telescoped by the arc piece that contains it, as in 15.2(α), and a_X, b_X ∈ ∂X⁺ are not fixed sites.
    - It is covariant under the external transformations at the ends of the arcs and at the ends of W's intervals outside every X⁺, by 15.2(β)'s change of variables and D2.5.
    - So 15.2(α)–(β) apply to P's tensor T_P, the joint average over the components of P is 15.2's, and the contraction in loop order is unchanged.
  - 15.6(a)'s covariance holds at the ends of each interval of ℐ(g): 15.2 for the arc words, and the previous bullet for the slots that hold cross members. (b) and (c) hold with hull(g) := ∪_{P∈g}dom P, which contains the slots' arcs and the sets Dep(D) of the members. Its counted domain is hull_c(g) := ∪_{P∈g}dom_c P (*Lengths*); #π_k(hull_c g) below is the number of its cubes, and #π_k(hull g) that of all the π_k-cubes meeting hull(g). The others are the cubes of the members' sets Q(D), which hold the base Y′₀ over Dep(D) ∖ X_D ⊆ ℐ(D) (*The units of the members*). D_g^× depends on U^R on them, but they meet ℐ(g), whose bonds are counted as traversals, and the cubes meeting ℐ(g) enter D_g^×'s domain one step later through E_D ((6A)(i), *R-made insertions*). 15.6(b) states hull(g)⁺, and its argument gives hull(g).
  - D_g^× does not vanish at pure gauge, so (d)'s deviation bound does not apply. D_g^× depends on U^R on hull(g) only. Its box is hull(g)⁺, as in D2.7.
  - Its bound is (c)'s, ‖D_g^×‖ ≤ B(g), with ‖W‖ for each cross member W in its slot's bound. Its mass carries e^{|Ψ_g|}, by 15.6(d)'s convention, and the field-dependent part of e^{−Ψ(S)} is handled by 15.6(d)'s last sentence, as for one loop.
  - The clusters incompatible with g overlap hull(g) or are joined to g. A join is a slot of g in the span of one of the cluster's polymers, or a slot of one of its polymers in span(P) for a P ∈ g with a span (*The groups of 15.4*). So the clusters are rooted at a π_k-cube of hull(g), at a slot bond of g, or at a bond of such a span, and
    |Ψ_g| ≤ C_Ψ′e^{−p₀(g_k)/2}(#π_k(hull g) + |ℐ(g)| + Σ_{P∈g with a span}|span(P)|), C_Ψ′ := O(1 + MC_jn),
    with C_jn of *The rooted sum*, and |span(P)| ≤ 4M(D_P + (21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(P)}σ(D)) (*The rooted sum*, *Spans*).
  - *15.6(e)'s scope rule.* Its first alternative, a box within d₀ = 6 layers of Λ_kᶜ ∪ S_k ∪ Ω_kᶜ, is SW closure §1(a3)'s rule for dev terms, whose deviation bound needs the box in the small-field region. D_g^× is not a dev term and carries no deviation bound, so this alternative does not apply to it, and the two cases below hold wherever hull(g)⁺ lies.
  - The second alternative, a polymer of g meeting a second-class or unchanged component, concerns the activities: c₁ = α^{1/3} there (p.390 l.1), and *The free decay* and *The volume* are derived above for the polymers that meet none. (6C) extends them to the others. Such a group is att(k, ·), and its activities are charged to the component as for one loop (D2.7 15.6(e)).
    - Its D_g^× has *The count*'s units, which do not use the activities.
    - A cross member W of it pays its part of |Ψ_g| and its join count from its weight, as in *The payment* and *Join count*, so its factor is at most w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/4)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W). D_g^× is then in the lineage of its first cross member, as in the second case, and stays in its register: M^cr, or D2.6 5.3's for a two-loop att first member ((6C), *The members' weights*).
    - The polymer part of its weight is paid in (6C).
  - The other cross groups have no polymer meeting a second-class or unchanged component, and their D_g^× is of one of the two cases below.
- *The count.* For every cross group g of the R-gas, att ones included, let 𝔅(g) be the carried insertions in g's slots, 𝔅^×(g) ⊆ 𝔅(g) the cross ones, 𝔅^s(g) := 𝔅(g)∖𝔅^×(g) the single-loop ones, and B_P := ∪_rX′_r ∪ ∪_{X∈P}X^{(44)} for P ∈ g, with X^{(44)} the set X⁺ widened by 44 layers of π_k-cubes. The first layer holds the cubes of Y′₀ that contain a slot bond on ∂X⁺ (*Lengths*), and the 44 layers hold the small members (*The units of the members*). Put
  σ_g := Σ_{W∈𝔅^×(g)}σ(W) + ½Σ_{D∈𝔅^s(g)}σ(D) + Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P),
  with 𝔅(P) the carried insertions in P's slots, and σ(D) the production value of each member, the one its weight carries (map l.1531: a carried insertion's σ is in the units of its step of production). A single-loop member counts half: the other half of its weight pays the joins across its domain (*The payment*, *The rooted sum*, *First case*). The rounding of the members' domains is in #π_k(B_P) (*The rounding*).
  - *The units of the members.* Let D ∈ 𝔅(g) be produced at step j ≤ k, with the factor u_D ≤ 19 of (6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor*. Let Q(D) be the set of the π_k-cubes meeting Dep(D) ∖ X_D. Put T_D := S_D ∪ Q(D) when j = k, and T_D := X_D, D's step-k domain, when j < k. When j < k, X_D is D's domain one step later, which contains X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D) by (H0) and (H2), so Dep(D) = X_D and Q(D) = ∅. When j = k, D is made at the T-step into step k, since R acts only at k > N_B and N_B > N₀ ≥ 1 (D2.7 item 1(b); LFR I p.179 l.8–10). So u_D = 1, and X^{Cov}_D ⊆ S_D = X_D ∪ ℐ(D) (*Connectedness*): Dep(D) ∖ X_D ⊆ ℐ(D), and each cube of Q(D) meets ℐ(D) ⊆ S_D. In both cases Dep(D) ⊆ X_D ∪ ℐ(D), and T_D is closed-connected and contains X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D ∪ ℐ(D) and the cubes of Q(D), which are the cubes of the base Y′₀ over Dep(D) ∖ X_D (*15.5*). Then
    #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(D) + 17 and diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M((21/20)σ(D) + 17).
    - When j = k, u_D = 1, so #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ σ(D) and diam_∞(S_D) ≤ Mσ(D) (*Units with a factor*, (H1)). Every point of a cube of Q(D) is within M of a point of ℐ(D) ⊆ S_D, so diam_∞(T_D) ≤ diam_∞(S_D) + 2M ≤ M(σ(D) + 2).
    - When j < k, they come from *The share of D*. At step j + 1, #_{j+1}(X_D) ≤ 16 + 20480/L + 8320u_Dσ(D)/L, with 215680σ(D)/L in place of the last term for a merged insertion of (R-member) (*Units with a factor*). At each later step E_D has at most #(X_D) + Σ_i(8|H_i(D)|/M + 80) cubes, with |H_i(D)| ≤ 4u_D(M/L)σ(D) + 2 (*Filled hulls*), so the count stays at most 16.1 + 0.23σ(D) for u_D ≤ 19 and L ≥ L₀^× (8320·16/L₀^× ≤ 0.23 and 215680/L₀^× ≤ 0.22). The intervals lie in the filled hulls, so n_k(D)/M ≤ 8u_Dσ(D)/L + 4/M. This gives #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ 17 + σ(D)/4. And diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M#_k(X_D), since a closed-connected union of N π_k-cubes has ℓ∞-diameter at most NM (the sum-of-diameters bound of *Lengths*).
    - The 17 is the rounding of a small domain to whole cubes: the 8M-neighbourhood of one π_j-cube can meet 2^d cubes one step later (*The share of D*). Production units alone do not bound a carried member's later domain.
    - *Big and small members.* Call D big if σ(D) ≥ 40 when j = k, or σ(D) ≥ 22 when j < k, and small otherwise. A big member has #_k(X_D) + n_k(D)/M ≤ (21/20)σ(D) and diam_∞(T_D) ≤ M(21/20)σ(D), without the 17: for j = k by the first bullet, since σ(D) + 2 ≤ (21/20)σ(D) once σ(D) ≥ 40, and for j < k since 17 + σ(D)/4 ≤ (21/20)σ(D) and 16.1 + 0.23σ(D) ≤ (21/20)σ(D) once σ(D) ≥ 22. A small member has #_k(X_D) < 40, n_k(D) < 40M, and diam_∞(T_D) < 42M: for j = k by the first bullet with σ(D) < 40, and for j < k since #_k(X_D) ≤ 16.1 + 0.23·22 < 22, n_k(D) ≤ M(17 + σ(D)/4) < 22.5M, and diam_∞(T_D) ≤ 21M. A small member whose T_D meets X⁺ for a first-class component X of its polymer has T_D within ℓ^∞-distance 42M of X⁺, so inside X^{(44)}, the set X⁺ widened by 44 layers of π_k-cubes. Every small member in an arc slot does: T_D contains ℐ(D), and an interval of D meets C ∩ X⁺ for the component X in whose slot D lies. 𝔅^{big}(P) and 𝔅^{small}(P) are the big and the small members of 𝔅(P).
      - 𝔅(P) holds the carried insertions in P's slots (D2.7 15.5(i)): those in P's arc slots, whose interval meets an arc of C ∩ X⁺ (D2.7 15.0); the whole-joined ones, an att(j, Z′) or lw insertion of a first-class component Z′ of P, which 15.5(i) makes "a slot inside their activity", the activity of the polymers of its home, here P; and the attached ones, a carried insertion that 15.5(i) joins to the polymers of its home h(D), the first-class components whose X⁺ meets Dep(D) = X_D ∪ X^{Cov}_D, or, by a part with 𝒦 ≠ ∅, to those of the components in 𝒦 (*15.5*; "the attached insertions are slots of the polymers of 15.3", 15.5(ii)). Each X_D and each cube of Q(D) lies in dom P (l.617), its intervals are in ℐ(g), and D_g^× depends on it. A big member is counted by (21/20)σ(D). A small member has #_k(X_D) < 40, n_k(D) < 40M, and diam_∞(T_D) < 42M (above). If T_D meets X⁺ for a component X of P, T_D lies in X^{(44)} ⊆ B_P (above). Otherwise T_D is at ℓ^∞-distance at least 2MR_k from every component of P, since X⁺ widens X by two layers of MR_k-cubes (*Lengths*), and D is joined to P in one of two ways, each with a term of D_P whose half covers D's count: its fewer than 40 cubes of X_D, its fewer than 40M interval bonds, and the 43M by which T_D and an end cube lengthen the piece that joins it. R_k ≥ 46 holds for g_k small, since R_k ≥ (log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)). (a) Through a part D^{𝒦}, 𝒦 ≠ ∅. The components of 𝒦 are components of P, so the tree has ℓ_{a′} ≥ 2MR_k, scaled lengths being at least step-k lengths (*Lengths*). So ½ℓ_{a′}/M ≥ R_k ≥ 46, and the half length in D_P's term (3/2)ℓ_{a′}/M covers the count: 2^{d+1}·½ℓ_{a′}/M ≥ 2^{d+1} + 40 with the tree's own 2^{d+1}(ℓ_{a′}/M + 1) cubes, 40M ≤ 16M·½ℓ_{a′}/M, and 43M ≤ ½ℓ_{a′}. (b) Through its part with 𝒦 = ∅, for a whole-joined member. Each piece of that part has a connected domain Y′ containing X_D and Z′ (15.5(ii)(β)), whose base Y′₀ is the cubes of X_D, of Q(D), and of Z′, all but Z′'s inside T_D. So #π_k(Y′∖Y′₀) ≥ 2R_k − 1 ≥ 91, and of D_P's term 2#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀) one half is the piece's own (*Lengths*, *Cubes*) and the other covers the count: 40 ≤ 2^{d+1}#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀), 40M ≤ 16M#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀), and 43M ≤ M#π_k(Y′∖Y′₀). No per-member constant of them enters, and their cost is paid by their trees' decay or their pieces' volume factors (*The free decay*).
    - The factors: u_D ≤ u₁ for the step-1 insertions, u_D = 1 for the ones made at the T-steps after step 1 (*Connectedness*), u_D ≤ 21/10 or u_D = 16 for the single-loop ones made at the R-steps ((6C)), and u_D = 6 for the R-made cross ones and their untouched continuations (the bullets below), with u_D = 19 for the merged insertions D_G of (R-member) and their untouched continuations. The T-step products of every species, lw and wrap included, have u = 1. The proof is by induction on the production step. A descendant made at a T-step takes its own σ at production (D2.6 closure 5.5), with u = 1. An untouched insertion keeps its production σ and factor (map l.683, l.718; D2.6 closure 5.3), and the bound for j < k covers it at every later step, whatever its factor.
    - *The rounding (derived).* The rounding is counted per cube, not per member. The number of members in X's slots is not bounded uniformly in k: members made at a step j < k can have L^{k−j} disjoint intervals in one step-k bond (D2.6 closure 5.2 (T0) *Positions*).
      - A big member carries no rounding constant. Its cubes, traversals, and diameter are at most (21/20)σ(D) in π_k-units, paid from its own weight at the rates below.
      - The small members of X's slots lie in X^{(44)}. Their cubes are cubes of X^{(44)} ⊆ B_P, their intervals lie on the arcs of C in X^{(44)}, and their sets T_D lie in the set X′_r ∪ X^{(44)} of *Lengths*, of ℓ^∞-diameter at most M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92). So they are counted in #π_k(B_P) and D_P, once per cube and per bond, whatever their number.
      - #π_k(X^{(44)}) ≤ (105R_k)^d (*The volume*), so the cost at the rate (5/2)ν_r is paid by the margin of (1.89) of X (*The volume*), and the constant 4R_k + 92 of D_P is a polylog of order r < p₀, paid as in *The free decay*.
      - No per-member constant of the slot members 𝔅(g) remains in the uses below. The span members 𝒲_g of a single-loop group keep a constant 17 each, which (6C) pays through their masses, not by the margin ((6C), *The members' weights*).
    - A single-loop insertion D_g made at an R-step j (D2.7 15.6) is a function of U^R on hull(g) and of its members W ∈ 𝒲_g (D2.7 15.6(b), (f); 15.6(b) states hull(g)⁺, and its argument gives hull(g), as for D_g^× below). D2.7's hull(g) is the union of span(g) and the domains of g's polymers (D2.7 15.6). The counted domain X_{D_g} is the set of the π_j-cubes of ∪_{P∈g}dom_c P (*Lengths*), counted once as a set (the slot members 𝔅(g) included), together with the span members' domains X_W, W ∈ 𝒲_g, counted with multiplicity as in map l.1527. The arc of D_g is span(g), so σ(D_g) := #_j(X_{D_g}) + |span(g)|/M (map l.310): the bonds of span(g) are counted as traversals, not as cubes. Put S_{D_g} := ∪_{P∈g}dom P ∪ span(g) ∪ ∪_{D∈𝔅(g)}T_D ∪ ∪_{W∈𝒲_g}T_W. Then (H0)–(H2) hold.
      - (H2): E_{D_g} = X_{D_g} ∪ (the π_j-cubes meeting span(g)) is closed-connected. The cubes meeting the arc span(g) form a closed-connected set. The π_j-cubes meeting a dom P form a closed-connected set, since dom P is connected (*Lengths*); it contains P's slot bonds, which lie in span(g); and those of its cubes outside dom_c P are cubes of the sets Q(D), which meet ℐ(D) ⊆ span(g). So all of them lie in E_{D_g}. Each member's T_W is closed-connected, contains ℐ(W) ⊆ span(g), and lies in X_W ∪ (the cubes meeting ℐ(W)) ⊆ E_{D_g}. X_{D_g} itself need not be connected.
      - (H0): X^{Cov}_{D_g} ⊆ hull(g) ∪ ∪_WX^{Cov}_W. The π_j-cubes meeting hull(g) lie in X_{D_g} or meet span(g) (above), and the members' (H0) puts X^{Cov}_W in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_W and the cubes meeting ℐ(W), which lie in X_{D_g} or meet span(g). So X^{Cov}_{D_g} lies in the 8M-neighbourhood of X_{D_g} and the π_j-cubes meeting span(g).
      - (H1): S_{D_g} is closed-connected for the same reasons and contains X_{D_g} ∪ X^{Cov}_{D_g}. Its pieces are *Lengths*' pieces of each dom P with each X_D replaced by T_D, the bonds of span(g), and the sets T_W. So, by *The units of the members* and the cube bound below, σ(D_g) and diam_∞(S_{D_g})/M are both at most
        σ̄(g) := Σ_{P∈g}(#π_j(B_P) + (2^{d+1} + ½)D_P) + |span(g)|/M + (21/20)Σ_{D∈𝔅^{big}(g)}σ(D) + Σ_{W∈𝒲_g}((21/20)σ(W) + 17).
        The small members of 𝔅(g) lie in the sets X^{(44)} ⊆ B_P, inside *Lengths*' pieces (*The rounding*), or are counted in half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them (*Big and small members*).
        (6C) bounds σ̄(g) by a multiple of D_g's weight.
      - A member with intervals on both loops, a cross insertion whose interval on C_i lies in span(g), makes D_g a cross insertion, with ℐ(D_g) := span(g) ∪ ℐ(W) over these members. The bounds above are unchanged, since T_W contains ℐ(W) and n(W) is counted in (21/20)σ(W) + 17. Such a D_g is the merged insertion D_G of (R-member), with G = {g} when no other group is linked to its cross members. Its (H2), units (u = 19), lineage and register, rate λ_k(D_G), and θ are those of (R-member).
  - Cubes: #π_k(hull_c g) ≤ Σ_{D big}#_k(X_D) + Σ_P(#π_k(B_P) + (2^{d+1} + ½)D_P). The counted cubes of Y′₀ lie in B_P, on the attached trees, or in the big members' X_D, the cubes of the sets Q(D) being left out (*Lengths*); the small members' X_D lie in B_P or are counted in half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them (*Big and small members*). Each cube of a Q(D) meets a bond of ℐ(D) ⊆ ℐ(g), and a bond meets at most 2^d π_k-cubes, so #π_k(hull g) ≤ #π_k(hull_c g) + 2^d|ℐ(g)|. A lattice tree of length ℓ meets at most 2^{d+1}(ℓ/M + 1) π_k-cubes: its contour walk, of length 2ℓ, cuts into pieces of length ≤ M, each meeting at most 2^d cubes. The pieces' domains add at most D_P/2 cubes outside Y′₀.
  - Traversals: the bonds of ℐ(g) outside the big members' intervals lie in the arcs of C ∩ X⁺, X a first-class component of a P ∈ g (*The slots of a cross member*), or in the small members' intervals, which lie in X^{(44)} or, for the others, have fewer than 40M bonds each, counted in 16M times half of D_P's term of the tree or piece that joins them. Each other such bond lies in one set X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X^{(44)}, of ℓ^∞-diameter ≤ M(d_k(X′_r) + 4R_k + 92), or on one attached tree. Each of the eight sides of C₁ ⊔ C₂ meets a box of side s in at most s + 1 bonds. So n_k(D_g^×) := |ℐ(g)| ≤ Σ_{D big}n_k(D) + 16MΣ_PD_P, whatever the number of small members.
  - Together, #π_k(hull_c g) + n_k(D_g^×)/M ≤ (21/20)Σ_{D big}σ(D) + Σ_P(#π_k(B_P) + (2^{d+1} + ½ + 16)D_P) ≤ 6σ_g, since 21/20 ≤ 6·½ and 2^{d+1} + ½ + 16 ≤ 2^{d+2} at d = 4.
  - Diameter: S_g := hull(g) ∪ ∪_{P∈g with a span}span(P) ∪ ∪_{D∈𝔅(g)}T_D is closed-connected, and it contains ℐ(g) and X^{Cov} of D_g^×, which depends on U^R on hull(g) and on the members.
    - Each dom P is connected (*Lengths*), each X_D and each cube of Q(D) lies in the dom P of its slot (Y′₀ ⊇ Dep(D)), each T_D contains X_D, Q(D), and ℐ(D), and a link between two polymers of g is a slot bond of one in the span of the other, whose ends are slots.
    - A connected union of closed pieces has diameter at most the sum of theirs. The pieces are *Lengths*' pieces of each dom P, with each X_D and Q(D) replaced by T_D ⊇ X_D ∪ Q(D), and the spans. diam_∞(span(P)) ≤ diam_∞(dom P) (*The rooted sum*), and the cross members lie in polymers without a span.
    - The small members' T_D lie in the pieces X′_r ∪ ∪_{X⊆X′_r}X^{(44)} (*Lengths*), or, for the others, lengthen the tree or piece that joins them by at most 43M, inside half of its term of D_P. So diam_∞(S_g) ≤ 2MΣ_PD_P + (21/20)MΣ_{W∈𝔅^×(g) big}σ(W) + (21/10)MΣ_{D∈𝔅^s(g) big}σ(D) ≤ 6Mσ_g, since 2·21/20 ≤ 6·½.
  - So D_g^× has (T0)'s units with 6σ_g in place of σ, with counted domain X_{D_g^×} := hull_c(g) and S_{D_g^×} := S_g ((H0) and (H2) as in (6A)(i), *R-made insertions*: the π_k-cubes meeting hull(g) outside hull_c(g) are cubes of the sets Q(D), which meet ℐ(g)): n_k ≤ 6Mσ_g, diam_∞(S_g) ≤ 6Mσ_g, and #π_k(hull_c g) + n_k/M ≤ 6σ_g. At later steps it is a member like any other, with the bounds of *The units of the members*. (6A)(i)'s *Units with a factor*, at u = 6, then give it at most 1020Mσ_g positions in (T0), and its share is (6A)(i)'s, for L ≥ L₀^×.
- *The rates.* Put ε_k := e^{−p₀(g_k)/4} and ϑ_k := e^{−δMR_k/16} at the R-steps, and λ_n := 1 − Σ_{N_B<k≤n}(ε_k + ϑ_k) (λ_n := 1 for n ≤ N_B). ϑ_k pays the members' parts: η(D) ≤ c_rσ(D)e^{−δMR_k/8} ≤ (ϑ_k/4)ν_rσ(D) (*15.5*), under 4c_re^{−δMR_k/16} ≤ ν_r, a smallness of g_k. The single-loop weights carry no decrement; their parts are booked in (6C), *The members' weights*. The weights of the cross insertions after step n carry the rate λ_n(D)ν_r: w_n(D) := e^{λ_n(D)ν_rσ(D)}e^{θ(D)}‖D‖, with λ_n(D) := λ_n − s(D)/32 and the shift s(D) ∈ {0, 1}. s(D) := 1 for an R-made insertion whose first cross member is a two-loop att insertion that D2.6 5.3 carries ((6C), *The members' weights*), for an R-made insertion with a cross member of shift 1, and for a touched or merged descendant of an insertion of shift 1; s(D) := 0 otherwise, the two-loop wrap and att insertions of l.467 included. So λ_n(D) ≥ λ_n − 1/32 ≥ 15/16 under (H^R). An R-made insertion has w_k(D_g^×) := e^{λ_k(D_g^×)ν_rσ_g}e^{θ(g)}‖D_g^×‖, θ(g) := Σ_{W∈𝔅^×(g)}θ(W), and λ_k(D_g^×) ≤ λ_k(W) for each cross member W, since a member's shift passes to D_g^×. An untouched insertion has w_k ≤ w_{k−1}. The cross insertions here are all the insertions with intervals on both loops (l.713), the two-loop wrap and att ones of l.467 and the merged ones of (R-member) included. A two-loop wrap insertion keeps its (S-w) weight, with e^{ν_rσ_j} (l.737), and a two-loop att one keeps D2.6 5.0's weight e^{ν_rσ_j}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}e^{θ}‖Y‖ in its species' register. Both dominate e^{λ_nν_rσ_j}e^{θ}‖Y‖, since λ_n ≤ 1, so the rate costs their registers nothing, and their R-step payments (l.264) are made from w_n.
- *The payment.* For a cross group g none of whose polymers meets a second-class or unchanged component, the polymer part of e^{λ_kν_rσ_g}, together with the polymer part of e^{|Ψ_g|}, is paid by the activities, at the rate 2ν_r per unit of Σ_{P∈g}(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P). For the other cross groups it is paid in (6C), at the rate (5/2)ν_r. The member part below holds for all.
  - The polymer part of |Ψ_g| is at most C_Ψ′e^{−p₀(g_k)/2}(1 + 20M)Σ_P(#π_k(B_P) + 2^{d+2}D_P), by *The count*'s bounds #π_k(hull g) ≤ #π_k(hull_c g) + 2^d|ℐ(g)| and |ℐ(g)|, the spans' 4MD_P, and 2^{d+1} + ½ + (2^d + 1)16M + 4M ≤ (1 + 20M)2^{d+2} at d = 4. For small g_k this is at most ν_r per unit, and λ_k ≤ 1.
  - e^{2ν_r#π_k(X′_r)} is paid by a_V/2 per cube, since 2ν_r ≤ ¼ ≤ a_V/2 (*The volume*).
  - e^{2ν_r#π_k(X^{(44)})} ≤ e^{p₀(g_k)/16} is paid by the margin of (1.89) (*The volume*). It includes the small members (*The rounding*).
  - e^{2ν_r2^{d+2}D_P} is paid by e^{(c_F/2)D_P}, under 2^{d+4}ν_r ≤ c_F = min(1, δM/2), that is 2^{d+4}ν_r ≤ 1 and δM ≥ 2^{d+5}ν_r. The first holds by the map's ν ≤ 1/1000 (l.1614) at d = 4. The second is a lower bound on M, given that δ is fixed before M.
    - δ is the decay rate of the 𝐇-functions in the scaled distance (p.376 l.11–13). LFR II bounds them as B₃exp(−δd(·,·)) (p.368, (1.47)), CRE's form with the same B₃ (CRE p.250, the third and fourth lines below (1.19); p.251, the eighth line below (1.21); p.268, the fourth line below (3.17)). CRE fixes δ and then takes M large: below (1.21) it bounds |𝐇_{1,Ax}| < O(1)B₃exp(−δMR₁)ε₁, "much smaller than δ₀, for MR₁ large enough" (p.251, the eighth and ninth lines below (1.21)), and p.250, the third to fifth lines below (1.19), does the same for M₂R₁. M is chosen after L and M₂ (map l.973, whose δ₀ is VAR's rate). That LFR II's δ is this δ is a reading of the shared form B₃exp(−δ·), listed under *Inherited*.
  - Of the rest of e^{−c_FD_P}, e^{−(c_F/4)D_P} pays the interleaving entropy (*The rooted sum*) and e^{−(c_F/4)D_P} is left for the join of the first case.
  The member part of |Ψ_g| is at most C_Ψ′e^{−p₀(g_k)/2}Σ_{D∈𝔅(g) big}(#_k(X_D) + (2^d + 1)n_k(D) + 4M(21/20)σ(D)), the factor 2^d + 1 for the cubes of Q(D) and the other cubes meeting D's intervals, and the last term for the single-loop members of polymers with a span (*Spans*). The small members are in the polymer part, through B_P and D_P (*The count*). By *The units of the members*, #_k(X_D) + n_k(D) ≤ M(21/20)σ(D) for a big member. So the member part is ≤ 23C_Ψ′Me^{−p₀(g_k)/2}Σ_Dσ(D), since 17·21/20 + 4.2 ≤ 23 at d = 4. This is ≤ (ε_k/2)ν_rΣ_Dσ(D) for small g_k, under 182C_Ψ′Mε_k ≤ ν_r, since e^{−p₀(g_k)/2} = ε_k².
  - A cross member W enters D_g^×'s weight with at most e^{λ_k(D_g^×)ν_rσ(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖W‖e^{(ε_k/2)ν_rσ(W)} ≤ w_{k−1}(W)e^{−(ε_k/2 + ϑ_k)ν_rσ(W)}, since λ_k(D_g^×) ≤ λ_k(W) = λ_{k−1}(W) − ε_k − ϑ_k (*The rates*).
  - A single-loop member D enters D_g^×'s weight with at most e^{(λ_k/2 + ε_k/2)ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖. It also pays e^{(ε_k/4 + ϑ_k/4 + 1/8)ν_rσ(D)} in *The rooted sum* and e^{(ν_r/8)σ(D)} in the first case. In all this is ≤ e^{(1/2 + 3ε_k/4 + ϑ_k/4 + 1/4)ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖ ≤ e^{ν_rσ(D)}‖D‖, since ε_k, ϑ_k ≤ 1/32 under (H^R).
  - This is within its weight, species by species, since σ(D) is the production value that its weight carries (map l.1531).
    - dev: e^{ν_rσ + ν_e·ext} inside (ii)'s and (iii)'s per-position sums, in production units (map l.292, l.1531).
    - att(j, ·), j ≤ k, att(k, ·) included: the weighted mass e^{ν_rσ_j(W)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ (D2.6 closure 5.0, its production weight from (S-w); map l.302).
    - lw: each piece of an lw slot is counted as a fixed member with its own σ, the one its weight carries (D2.6 closure §6.4, *(T0)'s count for lw slots*).
    - wrap(j), j ≥ 2: (S-w) carries e^{θ + ν_rσ_j + ν_e·ext_j} (map l.292, D2.4(12)–(13)). The D2.6 closure 2.1(e) spends the extent weight on ext_j ≥ N^{(j)}_min/2 − 1 and drops e^{ν_rσ_j}, and the map states the weight for the dev species only (l.292, l.1531). Keeping e^{ν_rσ_j} on the wrap(j) insertions is an amendment of 2.1(e), derived here: it gives the same bound, Σ_{𝒢∋n, wrap(j)}e^{θ(𝒢) + ν_rσ_j(𝒢)}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j)·pl·e^{ν_e}e^{−(ν_e/2)N^{(j)}_min}, merged groups with a wrap member included (2.1, *Merged groups*). The insertions are carried unchanged in (ii) with the growth L per step (map l.367). The weight is a fixed factor of each insertion, so the weighted sums grow in the same way.
